# State, data and API contracts

## Implemented configured owner v6 transport and client

OwnerReviewConfiguration/OwnerReviewDocument accept optional metadata_review: MetadataHistoryLimits with explicit finite max_records/max_payload_bytes/max_dependencies/max_work/max_summary_bytes, max_inspections<=64 and writes_per_minute<=100. metadata_review requires auth_review. Both composition configuration and direct OwnerMetadataReview construction require max_inspections and writes_per_minute equal the original owner_metadata limits. Missing/null preserves v1/v5; unknown fields, booleans/coercions, unpaired groups or mismatched writer limits fail before external initialization. The real factory passes exact group objects. Review pins original group identity/values through delivery and passes the original request configuration fingerprint to the private v6 reader; no HTTP flag selects the profile.

Existing POST review path now has a closed v6 discriminated response variant in addition to v1/v5. V6 requires auth_history_policy_resolved, historical_auth_authority_proven, metadata_history_policy_resolved and historical_metadata_authority_proven all false, plus the existing historical/read-only/no-cleanup/no-erasure/no-fulfillment contract. Request bodies, route admission, MFA/owner/CSRF/Origin and expected request/gate/control versions remain unchanged.

Existing POST policy-obligations returns either auth-policy-obligations-v1/accounting-v5 or privacy-policy-obligations-v2/accounting-v6. V2 adds exactly metadata_family_counts (four closed categories), validated_metadata_records, metadata_historical_dependencies_unavailable and the two false metadata policy/authority flags. Auth and metadata category sums must match their validated counts, and their combined count cannot exceed source_records. Earlier unavailable references are counted independently; they are not recovered payloads or authority. Explanation projection charges N+4 additional work against the original metadata limit, alongside the existing auth projection budget; no new allowance or retry. Original response-byte ceiling, cumulative read budget and final source/drain/authority checks remain.

Executed TypeScript parsers enforce exact version/key sets, false flags, safe counts/sums and fresh request binding. The owner screen accepts v6, shows both category groups, and clears stale context on malformed/error/offline/new-request/identity changes. Review does not select or save a policy, reset write allowances, approve deletion or resume inspections. Old bundled clients reject unknown v6 responses; deliver the matched current backend/frontend wheel when opting in. Independent v6 classification and complete privacy execution remain unfinished.

## Implemented explicit private metadata-history/v6 accounting contract

metadata_history.derive validates the original complete held inventory and current active request/proof/scope, reuses metadata_request_contract.validate_request, discovers every supported metadata operation and exact matching immutable audit peer, rejects orphan/duplicate/missing/unknown peers, and checks create/cancel result/body/binding/actor/cookie references. Available gate/runtime/user declarations must match; unavailable historical payloads are counted, never recreated or treated as proven authorization. Reconstruct all quota write revisions from genesis with exact minute/count hashes and the configured original writer limits. Minute-candidate work is charged to the original finite history budget; gaps, ambiguity, rollback and incomplete chains hold. Quotas are shared context, never a refund or inspection-content owner.

MetadataHistoryLimits requires finite record/payload/dependency/work/summary bounds and original writer max-inspections/writes-per-minute limits. The frozen private result binds inventory/proof/scope/configuration/auth history and minimized references; raw payload/identifiers stay out of repr/public output. Root records alone copy inspection selector memberships. Flags historical_authority_proven, policy_resolved, can_confirm_cleanup and request_fulfilled remain false; read_only true. Validate the final result after the last external checkpoint against its original summary length/digest. Original limits, source, drain, target/configuration and deadline remain fenced through final held-reader reconciliation.

HeldCorpusReader.read_metadata_accounting is an explicit private opt-in requiring metadata limits/configuration plus existing runtime/access/auth/accounting limits. No classification/collateral combination or partial configuration is accepted. Accounting v6 independently frames the usual header/context plus auth and metadata history digests. Roots retain METADATA_RETENTION_POLICY_REQUIRED even with validated scope; opaque/shared peers remain context without copied inspection IDs. Existing v1–v5 entrypoints/defaults/authority remain unchanged. No v6 public host/review/client wiring or ownership-classification contract is implemented yet. No schema/history consistency supplies actual D11 policy, erasure/fulfillment, retained-copy disposition or inspection resumption. Continue complete downstream privacy workflows under actual decisions.

Existing app body validation returns sanitized400 INVALID_REQUEST for extra fields; query handler422 applies if reached, otherwise held boundary may503. Closed19-field response: original5 request bindings id/scope_sha256/request_version/gate_version/control_version; policy=auth-policy-obligations-v1; accounting_policy=conservative-family-accounting-v5; family_counts; validated_auth_records; source_records; historical_dependencies_unavailable; next_step=OWNER_D11_POLICY_DECISION_REQUIRED; historical/read_only=true; auth_history_policy_resolved/historical_auth_authority_proven/can_confirm_cleanup/erasure_complete/request_fulfilled=false. Family counts contain exactly access-head,bootstrap,user,identity,session,cookie,invitation,owner-transfer,access-replay,oauth-flow,oauth-quota. Unknown fields/categories, coerced values or completion flags are denied by server/client contracts.

Reserve auth.work+N+11 within the original max_work before category allocation; checkpoint each record, retain original response-byte/cumulative corpus read budgets. No detached summary, extra arbitrary scan, budget reset or automatic retry. No identities, cookie/session locators, row keys/payloads, capability, private digest or dependency arrays are exposed. Counts are structural historical hints across reviewed supported deployment-auth records, not inspection ownership, legal rights or a complete executable policy. Zero counts do not resolve D11. ## Worker original-configuration and exception lifetime — WC01–WC06

Internal ordinary-worker contract only; no new API/schema/grant/configuration field. Construction pins exact admission and optional ResourceOwner identities and exact integer lease/attempt values. Check before acquire/prepare/admit/checkpoint/business/release/retry and after admission/business callbacks. Each actual call uses captured original objects and the exact original intent; retries are original1–3 CAS bookkeeping only, never business retries. Mutation, float/bool substitutions and changed identities hold rather than extending bounds or switching targets.

Known synchronous completion/error releases its local ticket only through the captured original ResourceOwner. Configuration drift/failed durable release sets private release_pending and prevents further polls; retain durable registration if release has not actually succeeded. Never report a retained registration if original release already completed. Local actual-work drain is distinct from durable privacy drain. Deferred/awaitable/Future/termination keeps original local and durable ownership pending; no cancellation or forced-release proof. An ordinary release failure must not hide an already raised ordinary business exception; BaseException remains unconfirmed and retains ownership. No automatic reconciliation, force release, cleanup/inspection-resumption or requester-fulfillment authority follows. [Source-bound selected verification](../.loop/repairs/worker-config-fence/verification-current-v1.json).

## Current held metadata request — CR01–CR08

Fresh enabled MFA owner only: `GET /api/v1/privacy/metadata-requests/current`, no selectors/query/body authority. Literal route precedes `/{ident}`. Existing enabled owner-control boundary admits only exact raw GET path with empty query during a hold. Encoded/trailing/similar paths, other methods and selectors receive no hold exemption; selectors are rejected422 if they reach the handler while OPEN. Existing denial/503 boundary behavior remains.

Closed seven-field wrapper: `status` (`open` or `held`), `request` (null only for open; strict22-field existing request view for held), `unknown_writes_reconciled:false`, `inspection_resumption_authorized:false`, `can_confirm_cleanup:false`, `erasure_complete:false`, `request_fulfilled:false`. Nested held snapshot must be statusheld/current_holdtrue, gateHELD and consistent revisions/scope/epoch/counts/sorted unique IDs; unknown fields/types and completion flags are denied. Another fresh owner's session may recover under existing authority; `original_owner_session:false` never transfers original-session action authority.

Domain reuses the shared owner, one read allowance, original deadline, fresh authorization, validated gate/registry/runtime/data/request/history and final checked delivery. ID comes only from the current gate. Concurrent gate/request/authority/config changes, missing/malformed rows, resource/deadline exhaustion and unknown completion hold before partial delivery. No scan/retry/renewal/new grant/business mutation. Protected HTTP admission still registers/checkpoints/releases the control lifetime (positive fixtures observe registry revision+2, identical empty active payload); “read-only” never means bypassing this bookkeeping. This distinction underlies the pending independent CR-T01 candidate-oracle review.

OPEN is a point-in-time absence of current hold, not history absence or unknown-write reconciliation. Live or expired-but-unconfirmed business work remains visible and blocks downstream confirmation. This endpoint does not classify writes, implement erasure or authorize inspection resumption. [Current verification](../.loop/repairs/current-hold-recovery/verification-current-v1.json).

The established exact retry after inspection close remains supported and returns its original history. Human disposition remains distinct from machine approval and independently verified truth. ## Metadata payload erasure — storage contract reported before implementation

PP09/W20 needs removal of operational record payloads, not only selected object versions. Gap reported before implementation: the repository supported conditional puts without a scrubbed payload/retained-marker representation. That prerequisite now has selected local implementation/test/package evidence in BUILD-STATUS. The complete discovery/preview/confirmation/job/UI workflow remains unimplemented; a storage operation alone cannot satisfy it.

Introduce an internal typed `Erase` operation binding a current integer source revision from 1 through 2^63−2 and opaque 64-hex erasure-job/scope reference. The incremented marker revision stays within 2^63−1. It participates in the same existing maximum-100-item transaction as ordinary writes/checks. Replace the entire original payload with an empty map and a versioned top-level marker containing only the opaque reference and logical deployment/table/resource identity digest. Retain existing opaque primary keys and the incremented revision for denial/idempotency; those retained identifiers are not a claim of complete legal erasure. Do not copy original fields, free text, user/session identities, images, credentials or timestamps into the marker.

Ordinary snapshot and prefix-query reads withhold valid erased records. Keep one atomic transaction per snapshot (up to 100 original keys), with no extra network requests or added marker lookup. Prefix queries preserve the underlying bounded cursor even when the visible page is empty. A separate internal `erasures(keys)` read returns only validated minimal marker status, never the original payload. Corrupt, foreign, unsupported or nonempty-payload markers fail closed; absence is not proof of removal.

All ordinary updates and conditional checks must reject the top-level erasure tag, even if a caller knows the marker's current revision. New inserts remain attribute-not-exists conditioned; stale writers cannot recreate an erased key. Erase itself requires a live unmarked row at the exact reviewed revision; reconciling an unknown result must read the original job/idempotency/marker state rather than renew or rewrite it. Role/owner/MFA, closed-work/capacity coordination, full dependency discovery, exact preview/confirmation, leased progress, independent restore-journal replay, external copies/backups and requester communication still require higher-level implementation and evidence. No cleanup runs against real data or accounts in this build phase, and beta remains NO_GO.

The marker is an application-storage guard, not a provider policy or independent backup journal. Raw privileged writes, restoring a pre-erasure record or running an older incompatible writer require a separate restore/compatibility gate and tombstone replay before data exposure. These obligations are unimplemented here; do not treat the empty payload as proof of completed privacy fulfillment.

### Complete metadata workflow — remaining integration contract

The following remains required under PP09/W20/W25; it is not accepted by the storage checks. Establish a current owner/MFA-authorized request and exact inspection scope, coordinate terminal closure and allocation release, and discover all affected records before any payload is removed. Never expose an arbitrary-key `Erase` endpoint. Preserve the source/target storage receipts needed for object-version cleanup until that work is actually verified; scrubbing their metadata first would lose cleanup authority.

The structural [key-prefix inventory](../evidence/local/metadata-record-families.json) identifies source locations, not an erasure policy or a complete dependency graph. Discovery needs explicit family adapters and validated relationships; matching a UUID substring is insufficient.

#### Source-confirmed dependency and lineage gap — reported before implementation

Inspection payloads do not all contain a direct inspection selector. `capture/service.py` links permits/observations to `inspection_id`; `capture/publication.py` links actions, assets and object intents through `observation_id`, including separately derived original assets. `review/labels.py` stores the inspection in immutable revisions' `source_context.inspection_id`; label heads alone are insufficient. `review/manifests.py` copies label/source information into example `content` under `dataset_id`. `stations/handoffs.py` writes both the main request and a separate recipient-scoped inbox reference. Each adapter must validate the actual relationship and include all relevant revisions/copies; deleting only directly matching inspection fields misses this data.

An additional gap exists in `AccessService._record`: its idempotency payload contains `body_hash`, `result` and `at_ms`; the separately emitted access event contains operation kind, actor/session and optional details, without a direct typed link to that idempotency row. Some results expose only a generic `id`. This is source evidence of missing discovery provenance, not a claim of an observed unauthorized disclosure. No cleanup service currently exists to handle the gap.

Before implementing complete discovery, define and install versioned typed resource bindings at the shared writer and direct event/job writers. Bind exact deployment/table/resource and the relevant record version or immutable digest, distinguish written resources from checked dependencies, and deduplicate/bound the bindings. Keep the original operation identity and result; do not infer authority from the model or a client-supplied arbitrary key list. Commit the bindings with the existing event/idempotency/business writes so a lost response cannot create an unlinked effect. Do not add payload images, credentials, private capabilities or request bodies to this provenance.

The shared writer's internal `resource_bindings` envelope uses `schema_version: 1`, exact logical `deployment`, original `operation`, and separate `writes`/`checks` arrays. Each write reference carries `table`, `resource`, `previous_revision` (null for creation), resulting `revision` and canonical payload `sha256`; each check carries `table`, `resource`, current `revision` and canonical payload `sha256`. The event and idempotency record receive the same envelope. Business results are unchanged. Do not include those newly generated event/idempotency rows as recursive self-links. Bound references by the existing transaction capacity, sort/deduplicate deterministically and reject conflicting duplicates. A check that is also updated must describe the actual pre-write condition, not pretend the new payload was checked. Logical binding does not establish real AWS target identity; retain the separate verified target/configuration boundary.

Shared-writer implementation follow-through: `evidence/resource_bindings.py` prepares frozen ordinary writes/checks for `AccessService._record`. Its canonical JSON convention preserves integral DynamoDB Decimal values, excludes raw payloads from the envelope and rejects unsupported values. Keep the original 100-item transaction ceiling: at most 98 distinct business/check resources leave two items for event/idempotency; overlapping pre-write checks can appear in both arrays (at most 196 references). Deployment/operation lengths are 1–256/1–512 characters, resource keys remain 1–256 characters, exact integer revisions are bounded by the marker contract, and the encoded envelope is limited to 131,072 bytes. These are internal schema bounds, not new runtime quotas or a relaxation of existing request limits. Checked acknowledgments may have no business writes, as in repeated purpose withdrawal; do not invent a mutation for them.

Preparation copies business/check payloads before the final callback, so later mutation of the caller's input cannot make the stored bindings describe a different committed business payload. Recheck current authorization after preparation and before the existing final source/time callback; retain all original commit conditions and idempotency/body-hash behavior. Bindings are private: public results remain minimized, and the delivered export must omit them even when it includes the linked event. Selected native and installed checks are recorded in BUILD-STATUS. Selected direct/nested event and replay writers now have local evidence below; no-event job adapters, discovery consumers, legacy reconciliation, the maintenance barrier and complete cleanup remain unfinished.

Historical bindings describe the referenced version when the operation was recorded; they are not a claim that mutable records remain at that version. Complete discovery needs validated family relationships and a separately reviewed plan of the current rows/versions/digests to be removed. Never silently substitute a current payload digest as proof of an unavailable historical payload, or treat a historical link as current permission. This distinction does not waive unknown/ambiguous-source holds or exact final plan checks.

#### Erasure lease versus caller session — conflict reported before repair

Source review after the direct-writer change identifies a conflict: `AssetErasure.advance` now rechecks owner session/membership at final progress commit, while the existing authorization contract below and `test_already_authorized_cleanup_can_finish_after_requestor_logout_without_new_authority` require already-authorized cleanup to finish under its exact current service lease after logout. Its public job response must still deny the logged-out caller. The new direct-lineage session-race test encoded the conflicting requirement and cannot supersede that existing contract. The second full-suite cohort now confirms this conflict: 229 PASS / one FAIL at the unchanged logout regression, with progress left processing. Later cohorts remain running; no full-suite result is inferred.

Repair must retain initial fresh owner/MFA authorization when claiming new work, immutable job/scope/storage binding, original runtime incarnation, exact lease token/version, monotonic and wall deadlines, execution gate, final asset/job conditions and private event provenance. Final completion of the claimed destructive cleanup uses that service lease, without adding a new session dependency. Logout cannot authorize a new claim or return private job metadata. Invalid/replaced runtime, expired/superseded lease, changed scope/storage/asset or execution-gate denial still prevents progress commit. Preserve the original session-race test and failed source as historical contradictory oracle evidence; replace its acceptance with both allowed post-logout progress plus denied read and atomic invalid-lease/runtime rejection. Rerun the unchanged original logout/runtime regressions and selected lineage/erasure/maintenance integration before closing this repair. No authority for actual external deletion is implied.

#### Direct and nested event transactions — contract before implementation

Source review finds direct events in bootstrap/runtime start, calibration/recipe diagnostics, capture admission/completion/failure, reference finalization, media/export access/progress, review/dataset/preparation/qualification access and selected-asset cleanup progress. Some events have no `kind` field, so the trusted producer must supply an explicit bounded operation name; do not add or change existing public event fields to infer one. Capture admission can also create an idempotency record directly. Shared operations such as dataset declassification can carry an additional domain event alongside the shared access event.

Introduce explicit linked-transaction preparation for all event/idempotency outputs in such a transaction. Freeze ordinary business writes/checks, exclude every output event/idempotency key from its own binding references, and attach the same versioned envelope to all those outputs. Preserve original result/body-hash fields, business payloads, output identities and append/update conditions. Existing replay records finalized in the transaction receive the new transaction's links; their original immutable events retain earlier history. Do not hash an output before adding its own envelope and then claim that hash describes the final output.

Reserve the actual number of output items against the existing 100-item ceiling; a direct transaction with one event may use 99 other distinct resources, while a shared event/idempotency pair keeps its existing 98. Conflicting duplicate keys/versions remain failures, not dropped dependencies. No extra database request, audit item, action quota, replay or role is introduced by preparation. Events and replay outputs are private metadata, not removal authority.

Prepare before the producer's final authorization, source, lease and deadline checks, then commit the frozen values under all original conditions. Trusted startup or failure reconciliation that has no user authorization/deadline must preserve its specific version/current-instance checks rather than acquiring a fabricated user grant. No-event job updates still require validated family ownership/lineage adapters; generic event linkage does not accept them or complete discovery. Tests must cover real bootstrap/runtime/capture events, direct admission idempotency, nested events, output exclusion, exact physical capacity, frozen inputs, preparation-time expiry and continued archive omission. Implementation follow-through: the selected 56-case native and 56-case installed runs in BUILD-STATUS now verify these event/replay mechanisms, including the unchanged original direct oracle; counts overlap. No-event job ownership adapters, complete discovery/consumer/legacy and cleanup/restore acceptance remain unimplemented or NOT_RUN. The full current Python suite is separate and remains in progress at this checkpoint.

#### Stored provenance reader — gap reported before implementation

The writer envelope currently has no typed stored-record decoder for discovery. Before a discovery consumer uses it, add a pure, non-authorizing decoder for an actual typed event/idempotency `Row`, using the repository's exact logical deployment. Distinguish missing legacy bindings from malformed/foreign bindings; both hold dependent cleanup until an explicit family policy resolves them. Reject unsupported schema/fields, wrong deployment, invalid output/table/resource identity or row revision, duplicate/unordered references, invalid digests/revisions, output self-links, event/replay outputs masquerading as business writes, inconsistent overlapping prior versions, empty effects/dependencies and envelope/item-capacity overflow. Preserve the existing writer's schema and limits; do not add permissive normalization of strings, floats or booleans into revisions.

DynamoDB nested integral Decimal values must decode exactly, while nonintegral/nonfinite values fail. Return frozen typed write/check references detached from the mutable input. Historical versions/digests remain historical, including check references to earlier immutable events; do not substitute current values, fetch/mutate records, infer family ownership, follow a global dependency or accept a cleanup decision. Family adapters must separately verify recognized producer/operation/schema, ownership and the current reviewed closure. A parser alone supplies no endpoint, arbitrary-key selection or deletion authority. Test actual writer-to-storage serialization round trips, missing versus malformed data, private-field minimization, mutation independence, capacity boundaries and hostile values before integration.

Source media/purposes, users/sessions/runtime/DATA and quotas remain shared dependencies, not owned resources. Full source-media closure/current rights, reference intake, idempotency copies, complete inventory/barrier/read-set admission and exact aggregate cleanup remain separate; this adapter returns only frozen references/links and no raw notes/notices, capability or erasure authority. Test unconsumed/committed/withdrawn/repeated-withdrawal histories, operational-only versus explicit derived metadata, actual stored frame and reference grants, exact historical references, corruption/foreign/orphan/legacy forms and independent budgets before integration.

Optional-improvement follow-through: the three-file parser has transferred to 602 canonical inputs after 371 native / 371 installed / 501 package cases, with overlap. Canonical verification passes 371 cases / 1,113 phases; the 602-input full Python regression now passes 2,687 cases / 8,061 phases / 21 cohorts with all cleanup verified. Pure and stored-history evidence is recorded in [build status](BUILD-STATUS.md#optional-improvement-history--isolated-implementation-and-verification). DATA can carry changed_at_ms and other preserved shared-authority metadata. Reconcile the exact historical DATA revision/digest between preview and confirmation; never invent an epoch-only payload hash or assume epoch equals storage revision. Full shared-authority/source payload reconciliation remains separate, and this adapter cannot independently certify the historical epoch scalar or grant current rights.

#### Dataset direct-transition lineage — gap reported before implementation

Dataset creation/admission/end operations already emit typed audit bindings, but successful example/seal commits and terminal source-failure transitions currently call the unlinked transaction helper. Before a family validator can rely on those transitions, add one new immutable event in the same transaction as each actual direct effect. The event must identify the dataset, exact request digest, zero-based processed index, prior job revision and the transition outcome. Use explicit dataset-example-committed and dataset-source-failed kinds and unique event keys; store the existing typed resource binding envelope over the actual job/example/seal/group writes and exact transactional checks. No inferred retrospective events or legacy backfill is permitted.

Do not add grants, read rights, independent-benchmark status, new step reservations, quotas or idempotency renewal. Exact admission retry and immutable example semantics remain unchanged. Failed, cancelled, retired and restarted histories remain visible; a typed event is discovery evidence, not complete family admission or deletion authority. Existing legacy records still require explicit reconciliation.

Prepare the linked transaction before the final nonrenewable admission checks. After preparation, recheck reviewer/session authority, exact current lease/runtime, trusted execution gate, original source/effective/session/lease deadlines and monotonic lease budget immediately before committing. Preserve the original atomic source read set; late expiry, revocation, cancellation, source change or event-binding failure must leave no terminal job/example/seal/group/event effect. No longer deadline, retry budget or event-only partial commit is permitted. Tests must reproduce absent completion/failure bindings and late-preparation rejection, then exercise actual stored success/failure/retirement/retry and unchanged manifest regressions. Full dataset/reuse closure, barrier, job and requester fulfillment remain separate.

| Record group | Required discovery and handling |
|---|---|
| Inspection work | Inspection, observations, capture permits, actions, human dispositions and their source/event links; prevent in-flight publishers and stale next-kit reuse |
| Support | Main requests/outcomes, recipient inbox references and exact immutable/mutable chain dependencies |
| Media lifecycle | Asset metadata, object/reference intents, original/crop and selected-version erasure receipts; verify original target/version cleanup before discarding needed receipts |
| Review/reuse | Labels/revisions, optional source/review permissions, dataset manifests/examples/groups and admitted-access records; distinguish source removal from aggregate retention and independence history |
| Derived work | Evaluation examples/reports, qualification preparation/qualification entries/reports, their maintenance jobs and source lineage; invalidate dependent use rather than silently approving replacement policy |
| Copies and audit | Export jobs/manifests/tickets, access/capture/export events, idempotency and catalog/user references that retain selected data; minimize retained denial/receipt markers under explicit policy |
| Shared controls | Active policy/recipe/calibration heads, runtime/data authority, allocation, identity/accounts and global quotas cannot be blindly erased because they mention a related ID. Their dependency invalidation, replacement or separately scoped offboarding must be explicit |

Admission must bind the complete bounded plan, exact record versions/digests, target configuration, notice, affected uses and owner's separate irreversible confirmation. Coordinate a privacy-maintenance read/write barrier and background execution checkpoints for the whole approved scope; root removal or DATA advancement alone must not be assumed to block every metadata reader. Test all affected read/write/worker paths during partial progress. Unknown families, changed sources, scan exhaustion and incomplete scope hold the plan rather than becoming a partial-success erasure receipt.

Processing needs durable bounded leases, exact-intent recovery after lost acknowledgment, marker/job reconciliation, source and authority checks at each commit, independently retained restore instructions and a receipt that separates removed operational payloads from retained opaque markers, unfinished provider/backups/downloaded-copy duties and actual requester communication. Temporary account/station offboarding, actual target verification and human comprehension remain separate acceptance evidence. This contract preserves the full workflow; the current storage implementation is its prerequisite only.

#### Idempotency-result copy provenance — missing reader contract before implementation

W20 discovery must include private `state/idempotency#<sha256>` result wrappers. Source review confirms ordinary writes use `AccessService._record`, which emits one access event and replay wrapper with the same `prepare_linked` envelope. Capture admission instead emits an `observation_admitted` capture event and optional replay wrapper directly. The request body is unavailable: its digest cannot reconstruct it, and copied result fields cannot establish ownership or current permission. Earlier family adapters do not validate these wrappers.

Add a pure internal supplied-output parser, with no application route or cleanup authority. It accepts one revision-one replay Row and an explicit tuple of supplied sibling event Rows, the deployment and independent positive record/encoded-byte limits. Require the exact replay keys `body_hash`, `result`, `at_ms`, `resource_bindings`, canonical lowercase SHA key/body digest, a dictionary result and supported integer timestamp. Count every supplied position and canonical payload byte before detached processing, including matching duplicates; matching copies may reconcile, conflicting copies or erasure markers hold. Return only immutable full-payload references, typed historical bindings and links, never private results, body hashes, actor details or event payloads.

Every supplied sibling must be an immutable canonical-UUID event with exactly the same decoded envelope; foreign, legacy, malformed, self-linked or mismatched evidence holds. For ordinary writes require exactly one `access#UUID` event: exact kind/actor_scope/session_id/at_ms and optional dictionary details, kind equal to envelope operation, a bounded nonempty actor scope, canonical session UUID, access time no later than replay time. Other supplied nested events must have bounded kind and integer at_ms no later than the outer access time. For direct `observation_admitted`, require one capture event with the exact installed observation-admission fields and result tuple (inspection/observation UUIDs and positive sequence), matching IDs/sequence and source SHA; capture time cannot exceed replay time. An access event cannot substitute for this direct form. Expired authority does not delete historical provenance, and no current clock is used to renew it.

This reader verifies supplied output structure and shared transaction provenance only. Envelopes deliberately omit audit/replay self-links and therefore cannot prove the inventory includes every sibling, authenticate historical identities, recover the original request, reconcile every operation-specific result against raw business families or infer affected-person scope. Complete inventory admission, typed source-family reconciliation, explicit classification/unknown holds, the maintenance barrier and exact final owner plan remain mandatory before removal. A valid result wrapper or global runtime/user check never establishes ownership of a business record. Add independent malformed/corrupt/conflicting/bounds/privacy-output and storage Decimal round-trip tests; exercise actual ordinary and direct writers at the appropriate native/installed boundary after the current canonical regression terminates.

#### Inventory-to-family read set — missing bridge before implementation

W20 has a finite payload-free `InventoryPass` and private family parsers requiring raw Rows. There is no supported bridge that binds those raw inputs to the exact complete inventory or detects insertions after its first pass. Report this gap before implementing it; neither a summary nor individually valid family inputs prove complete, stable classification.

Add internal `DynamoRepository.inventory_snapshot(keys)` using exactly one existing atomic `TransactGetItems` for 1–100 distinct typed state/events keys. Return live Rows, validated minimal ErasureMarkers or explicit absence; ordinary snapshot/query visibility remains unchanged. No new HTTP route, write, credential or deletion authority is introduced. Reject malformed keys before SDK access and preserve existing strict stored-marker decoding.

Add `inventory.reconcile(repository, inventory, *, limits, max_snapshot_batches, max_payload_bytes, deadline_ns, checkpoint, tick)` under an already admitted maintenance checkpoint. Validate the exact inventory target, supported frozen entry shapes/order/uniqueness, independent limits, summary bytes and digest before hydration. Snapshot every original key in bounded batches, including markers, under one unchanged absolute deadline and target/client identity. Check real admission/target/time before and after reads and at delivery; unknown/missing, changed version/payload/marker, malformed/extra response identities, checkpoint failure or unavailable store holds without a partial read set. Count canonical live-payload bytes before retaining detached private copies; marker metadata remains covered by the inventory summary limit.

After hydration run another complete two-table pass under the same deadline/checkpoint and explicit page/record/summary limits. Require identical target, ordered entries, summary digest and byte count. An insertion, deletion, marker change or update after the original pass holds, including changes to unrecognized families. Empty inventories still require a complete follow-up scan. Return detached live Rows in a private repr-suppressed field, frozen marker/read-set references, and explicit consumed snapshot/byte counts. This internal raw-data object is not an export or public response.

Combined-candidate challenge, before repair: both collection and reconciliation used `id(client)` as the only retained client identity. Actual local probes accepted a different released/reallocated client with the same integer ID. A separate collector probe accepted target replacement during its final clock callback. Retain the exact initial client object for the complete operation, compare with `is` before/after callbacks and reads, and recheck the collector target after its clock sample. A recycled integer cannot establish target continuity. These are local protocol-oracle failures, not claims of a hosted exploit or completed barrier.

The supplied original pass can be forged; reconciliation must obtain actual reads and a complete follow-up rather than trust its hash as authority. Two equal scans are not a distributed barrier or an atomic multi-batch snapshot. The caller still must prove admission/drain/fencing, classify every family/unknown, establish affected-person scope, revalidate the final owner plan and preserve journal/object cleanup authority before any removal. No current session, grant, TTL expiry or summary can authorize cleanup. Test exact boundaries, 100+ keys, empty inventory, markers, alias mutation, new/missing/changed unknown rows, target/clock/deadline and checkpoint failures; actual-store/installed evidence remains separate.

#### Durable admission and drain registry — detailed gap before implementation

Source review confirms ordinary ASGI requests are not registered as active work; MaintenanceDriver.checkpoint only verifies stopping/runtime identity, and AccessService._fresh verifies session/member/role. Inspection disablement is not a metadata barrier. Add a private durable coordinator before wiring cleanup: it must deny new affected requests/ordinary workers while preserving proof that every previously admitted task has finished. An empty process-local counter, TTL expiry, lost response or runtime restart cannot establish drain.

Use two strictly versioned deployment records: a gate (OPEN or HELD, original epoch, configuration/enrollment digests and immutable held request/scope) and a bounded active-work registry. Registry entries contain only random work/instance UUIDs, kind, runtime/gate epochs and original issued/expiry times, never cookies, user IDs, inspection contents or request bodies. Explicit limits cap concurrent entries (at most 64) and original admission duration (at most 300000 ms); these are private protocol bounds, not enabled deployment defaults. Initialization is explicit on an enrolled current runtime with inspections disabled, uses conditional absence writes and never resets an existing hold or unknown/erased record. An enrollment digest is an external approval reference, not authentication performed by this adapter.

Prepare an immutable exact intent before admission so unknown confirmation/retry cannot extend its original deadline or change kind/instance/epoch. Admission conditionally updates the registry against the exact OPEN gate and current runtime; HELD, capacity, expiry, target change, unsupported records and unresolved commit status deny execution. Active entries survive process death and expiry. Release is only bookkeeping after actual work ends, requires its exact original intent and never clears the gate. Lost acknowledgment reconciles the original entry/absence rather than creating replacement work. Bounded contention returns a conflict for explicit same-intent retry, without clock renewal.

Holding binds one immutable request UUID/scope digest, requires the current runtime with inspections disabled, and conditionally changes only the gate. The gate condition fences racing admissions without requiring a quiet registry first. Old entries remain until their actual executor releases them; a superseded executor may release its own exact entry but cannot admit/checkpoint as current. Expired or crashed entries keep drain HELD; there is no generic HTTP force-release or auto-expiry shortcut. Only a supported later host-stop/reconciliation procedure can resolve an unknown executor.

Protocol challenges before repair: an observed future-issued entry must deny a skewed hold before any gate write. However, a valid admission can commit immediately before the gate closes while a different executor's wall clock is ahead. Gate CAS ordering, not cross-instance wall-time comparison, defines that cutoff; the already admitted entry must remain readable/releasable and must continue blocking drain. Do not permanently reject the whole registry solely because an admitted entry's time exceeds held_at_ms. Separately, proof consumption must validate exact integer/UUID/hash/reference types; Python's bool/int equality is insufficient for version identity. Preserve the five failing clock/race/proof oracles before correcting these cases.

A drain proof requires HELD at that exact request/scope and an empty durable registry, and binds the exact gate/registry/runtime revisions and full payload hashes. Every inventory/cleanup checkpoint must reread and match those references and the current disabled runtime; a proof is not a bearer permission or owner approval. No resume/open-after-cleanup method is provided until the complete original job, independent journal/restore and owner/readiness contract is implemented. Restart/initialization cannot silently open a held gate.

The coordinator alone does not cover work. Subsequent ASGI/ordinary-worker integration must register before access, retain admission until response/background execution has ended, checkpoint before consequential reads/commits/delivery and release only after completion. Cancellation/abandoned threads, multiple processes, failed releases and already emitted bytes require explicit failure tests; unknown work keeps cleanup held. Cleanup control routes/executors need separately scoped owner/admitted-job authority rather than an arbitrary bypass. Current factory/runtime behavior remains unchanged until that integration is implemented and verified. No actual cleanup, deployment, spend or external contact is authorized by this protocol.

#### ASGI admission lifetime — missing integration contract before implementation

A durable registry does not count real work until callers use it. Add an optional private ASGI wrapper covering the complete HTTP application call: prepare/admit one original intent before downstream execution, checkpoint before emitting every response fragment and keep the entry until the application returns, including awaited background work. Propagate a private ContextVar binding into async tasks and copied worker-thread contexts; the binding itself is not authorization, and checkpoint_current must verify the actual active registry entry. Reset context after the call. No caller-selected path, header or query can bypass admission.

Before any response headers, a held/unavailable admission yields a bounded static 503/no-store response without internal IDs or exception text. If headers were already emitted, suppress further private content and terminate with an empty body; previously emitted bytes remain delivered copies, not recalled data. Disable ASGI file-offload extensions and reject offload messages so file delivery cannot outlive registration. Lifespan is passed through; unsupported websockets are closed rather than treated as registered metadata work.

Only bookkeeping CAS conflicts may retry, with one configured ceiling from 1–3 and the exact original intent/deadline; never repeat the downstream business call. Release only after the awaited application scope actually ends. Cancellation while execution is unconfirmed retains the durable entry; expiry cannot clear it. Shield release once completion is known. If bounded release/reconciliation fails, retain the entry and expose a private release_pending diagnostic, never a drained/erased success. Unexpected/unmanaged tasks, abandoned threads and dead processes remain unsupported until their lifecycle is covered; a handler mock cannot establish production drain.

This wrapper is not automatically installed or a privacy-control bypass. Configured factory wiring, fixed non-data liveness handling, ordinary maintenance-worker registration, service/checkpoint coverage, exact owner cleanup-control authority and renderer/provider tests remain required. A held gate must not trigger an uncontrolled liveness-restart loop, and a private owner route must not become a general exemption. Unit ASGI/model tests must cover first/partial delivery, hold/expiry, context copying/reset, awaited background work, cancellation, release failure, unsupported scope and no business replay. Current canonical application behavior remains unchanged while its full regression runs.

#### Ordinary maintenance admission — contract before implementation

Wrap the complete ordinary MaintenanceDriver poll/dispatch call in one exact worker admission before its first runtime/query/quota/authority read. Use the same coordinator object as HTTP, propagate its private context and consult checkpoint_current from driver checkpoints. Retain registration through executor return and all final callbacks; stop/drain must not clear a still-running entry. A held/unavailable gate prevents the ordinary job query/advance, and expected metadata denial is reported as held rather than internal success/error.

Add an optional strictly shared runner to the existing driver, preserving existing unconfigured behavior for compatibility tests. A configured runner retries bookkeeping CAS conflicts only under explicit 1–3 bounds, preserves one original intent/deadline and executes the poll body at most once. Release after known synchronous completion/ordinary exception; BaseException/termination keeps unconfirmed work registered. Failed release sets a private pending diagnostic and prevents that driver starting more ordinary polls. Context binding cannot grant role/owner permission; existing service authorization and budgets remain unchanged. Manual jobs may use the already registered HTTP context of the same coordinator. Cleanup execution while HELD requires a separate admitted cleanup path; it cannot use an arbitrary ordinary-worker exemption.

Deferred-result challenge before repair: the synchronous runner accepted an async function or a callable returning an awaitable, released admission, and returned work that could execute later. Reject an async function before calling it; if a purported synchronous operation returns an awaitable or concurrent Future, retain its unconfirmed active entry and pending diagnostic, close only an unstarted coroutine handle where supported, and deny further ordinary work. Never cancel/forget a running future and claim drain. Preserve the original two failing deferred-work tests; add the concurrent-Future case as regression coverage.

The optional runner/wrapper do not activate themselves. Configured factory wiring, enrollment, fixed liveness, all independent worker/service entrypoints and owned process lifecycle must be verified before calling the product barrier complete. In-memory/model dispatch tests are not actual DynamoDB, process-stop or hosted evidence. Existing owner UI/driver status is not silently extended with new public claims; full owner control/diagnostics and cleanup workflow remain required.

#### Admission and restoration gaps to resolve before higher-level execution

Source review confirms `AccessService._fresh` checks current session/member grants, while `RuntimeControl.matches` identifies the current instance/build rather than requiring inspection enablement. Neither is a privacy-maintenance barrier. Ordinary inspection stop intentionally leaves the control plane reachable. Keep that behavior; introduce separate coordinated privacy admission rather than treating the inspection switch as denial of all metadata access.

Before the first scrub, the cleanup workflow must stop new affected reads/writes and background work, drain or fence already-admitted work within existing bounds, and then revalidate the complete reviewed dependency scope. A changed scope or source requires new review; do not silently expand the owner's irreversible approval. If admission/draining cannot be proved, keep the request pending/held and remove nothing. Tests must challenge a response already being assembled, an in-flight writer/worker, unknown confirmation, a second instance and a process stop at each transition. Already delivered copies remain external obligations.

Persist the maintenance hold and exact request before processing. A restart must retain denial and reconcile the original job/markers; it cannot clear the hold, renew approval or resume ordinary access merely because inspections start disabled. Retain an independently recoverable journal entry before removing any payload, containing only the target/configuration and opaque resource/version/intent references needed to replay denial. Do not store the removed payload in that journal. Journal unavailability, unverified append or scope mismatch holds removal. Reconcile a successful append with a lost acknowledgment instead of creating a new intent.

Recovery must verify the actual restored target, supported writer/marker protocol, complete journal replay and disabled prior sessions/work before exposure. A local marker or same-table receipt is not independent recovery evidence. Older incompatible binaries and raw privileged writes are not made safe by the new repository guard; the deployment/restore procedure must exclude them. These are required workflow contracts awaiting implementation and actual target evidence, not claims that a barrier, journal or recovery gate already exists.

## W11 acknowledged support handoff — missing contract before implementation

### Handoff export lineage — clarified before implementation

Local delivery follow-through: this contract now has selected native, actual local HTTP and offline installed-package evidence in BUILD-STATUS. The blanket hold is replaced by scoped complete history, mutable-row conditional fences and fresh source-to-copy comparison. Incompatible jobs remain held. Real provider, privacy cleanup and human completion remain unaccepted; the earlier contract wording below records the requirements, not an unimplemented promise.

The temporary generic-export hold must be replaced by complete scoped historical lineage, not by omitting support records. General reviewer export permission does not confer another reviewer's handoff detail access. Each included request additionally requires the existing sender-role, designated reviewer, or originating-operator grant. Unauthorized or partial histories cannot yield an archive; owner-only access remains insufficient. Historical export does not require the original sender session to remain live and never grants present support eligibility.

Add a scoped handoff collection stage under existing scan/page/record/lease/time limits. Reuse the domain's intact retained-record validator. Require exact request count, latest pointer, ordered source-inspection revisions, accepted-predecessor chain, accepted-recipient pointer and explicit supersession links; no disconnected, duplicated, missing or foreign records. Require the corresponding request/response/cancel audit events; supersession is linked through the next request. Preserve every source hash/version and shorten job validity to the earliest included metadata expiry. Recheck retention, source hashes and per-record permission during progress, ticket issuance and final delivery.

The additive manifest fields `support_handoffs` and `support_handoff_state` contain pseudonymous sender/recipient/decision identities, reason, recorded station/kit/source revisions, request/decision hashes and historical chain pointers. Explicitly state historical=true, machine_approval=false and capture_transferred=false; recorded acceptance is not present availability. Exclude cookie locators, session IDs, access versions, inbox references, credentials and current-eligibility claims. Existing media-purpose and original/crop rules remain independent. A new export configuration/schema marker holds incompatible old jobs rather than silently upgrading them. Manifest readback must bind the complete sanitized support fields to the collected job data. Physical metadata/inbox cleanup and full erasure remain separate required work; export completion is not deletion or launch approval.

Additional copy-integrity requirement, reported before its implementation: compare every persisted sanitized handoff copy with a fresh minimized projection of its exact referenced source row during progress and delivery guards. A matching manifest/job hash pair alone does not prove those copies describe the referenced request. Reject missing, duplicated, altered or extra copied fields, including a self-consistent edit of job data and manifest that leaves raw handoff sources untouched. Preserve the original probe and separately verify per-record scope and transactional mutable-row fences.

The overall saved `support_handoff_state` must likewise equal a fresh projection of the bound inspection itself during every guard, including completed-job/ticket/delivery reads. Rechecking request copies alone does not prevent a consistently rewritten summary from inventing a recorded accepted assignment. This missing state-copy check is reported before its repair and challenged with a separate retained oracle.

### Support screen contract — clarified before implementation

The missing interaction contract is reported here before frontend work. Add Support handoff to the invited workbench for explicit operator/reviewer grants. An originating operator's same-inspection entry stops camera/checks before navigation and seeds the inspection selector only. It does not send a request. Ownership handoff remains separate. No automatic polling, directory, notification or contact is introduced.

For an initial request, explicitly read the selected inspection and station under a fresh same-session operator grant, verify station ownership/open state and display inspection/station/version before a separate stop acknowledgment and request; the returned request supplies authoritative recorded kit context. For a successor, use freshly read accepted/current-assignment detail under the designated reviewer grant, its inspection version and station epoch. A response needs current exact detail, its request digest/row/inspection versions and explicit no-capture-transfer acknowledgment. Inbox pages are bounded historical references in UUID key order; choosing an entry only reads detail. A recorded pending row is never eligibility. Cancel requires a current pending record and original-requester authority. Field edits, failed reads, hidden/offline state and five-second review expiry withdraw action eligibility.

Every write freezes path, body, expected version and idempotency key before submission; a malformed/mismatched reply is unconfirmed, not success. Keep selectors/other writes frozen until exact original retry yields a matching receipt. Unrelated reads never settle the intent. Workbench's existing acknowledged-leave dialog governs discarding local retry information without canceling the server operation. Explicit refresh obtains new context only after the previous intent is resolved or separately investigated; no budget/TTL renewal. Show retained kit/station/request identity, recorded/effective status, historical acknowledgment versus current assignment, and manual/no-response instructions without claiming staffed support. Export/cleanup remain incomplete and must be disclosed. Keyboard access, narrow layout, interruption and unknown-write behavior require rendered tests; actual understanding remains NOT_RUN.

Reported gap: dispositions and station takeover exist, but no durable support request or recipient acknowledgment exists. A person named in a note has not accepted responsibility. This contract completes the existing P02/P03/P04 handoff requirement without a new persona, role, notification channel or capture transfer. Implementation is phased: domain, protected HTTP routes, recipient inbox service and optional host/factory configuration have selected local checks. Support screen mechanisms and offline installed software now have selected local evidence in BUILD-STATUS; configured-host/browser acceptance, complete export/privacy lifecycle and complete human journey acceptance remain required. No phase alone accepts W11.

1. Only the originating owning operator may initiate support for an open inspection. Its currently accepted reviewer may request a successor, under an explicit reviewer grant. The recipient must be another enabled, invited reviewer in this deployment; owner/operator status alone cannot receive another operator's inspection context. No member directory, email, provider subject or contact details are disclosed. Actual staffing, agreed response window and the business contact route remain owner inputs, not inferred from an account.
2. Configure `handoffs.max_requests_per_inspection=4`, `lifetime_ms=3600000`, `reads_per_minute=60`, `read_window_ms=5000` and `page_size=20` in controls. These are bounded development defaults, not a support promise. Pending lifetime cannot outlive the sender's current session. Explicit constructor limits are required; an absent service/configuration is unavailable. At most one eligible pending request exists; exact request/response/cancel retries keep the original body, key, versions, lifetime and spent count. New requests consume the same inspection's retained count, including declined, canceled or superseded requests. Exhaustion means manual handling, not a replacement inspection to bypass uncertainty.
3. Request intent binds exact inspection revision/station epoch, intended reviewer, bounded reason code (`unresolved_inspection`, `capture_interrupted`, `support_needed`), explicit stop acknowledgment, sender identity/session/grant, current DATA epoch and any accepted predecessor assignment. Require no in-flight observation. The atomic commit sets REVIEW_REQUIRED, clears current-result/stability/action authority and records the request and recipient inbox reference. It preserves correction/model/original-capture counts, station ownership and capacity. Outstanding permits cannot bypass the changed state/version. No handoff action creates a human disposition or machine approval.
4. Keep the immutable request and digest separate from its versioned pending/accepted/declined/canceled/superseded outcome. Store only bounded pseudonymous references and reason codes; no free-text messages, images, capabilities or credentials. Inbox references are recipient-scoped, minimal and verified against the request. Reads/pages enforce current access, bounded quota, expiry and final source/session/role checks. Request and outcome metadata follow the existing metadata-retention ceiling; TTL is not proof of actual erasure.
5. Only the designated current reviewer may accept or decline; self-acceptance is prohibited. Explicit response acknowledgment states that no capture transfer or kit approval occurs. Acceptance binds exact request revision/digest, inspection and predecessor state, sender/recipient current grants, original sender session and DATA. Check all dependencies atomically at commit. Missing/expired/revoked/stale work holds pending acceptance. An acknowledged result is historical; it never enables capture, clears uncertainty, refunds requests or returns REVIEW_REQUIRED to OBSERVING.
6. A replacement request retains the last accepted assignment until the successor explicitly accepts. Decline/cancel/expiry is not successful transfer. Only the original requester may cancel a pending request with the required current role, including after reauthentication; cancellation does not require the original session ID. An expired or held pending request can be explicitly superseded within the original request count; it does not renew the old request. A closed inspection, changed station ownership/epoch, lost role, session or DATA invalidates pending acceptance. Previously recorded acknowledgments remain history; a revoked/closed assignment is not advertised as currently available support. Absent accepted support means stop and use the business's manual procedure.
7. Implemented optional routes under `/api/v1`: `POST /inspections/{id}/handoffs`, `GET /inspections/{id}/handoffs/latest`, `GET /handoffs/inbox`, `GET /inspections/{id}/handoffs/{request_id}`, `POST /inspections/{id}/handoffs/{request_id}/respond` and `/cancel`. Existing Origin/CSRF, If-Match, idempotency, role and rate controls apply. No automatic external delivery or polling write. Uncertain request/response outcomes freeze intent; a different read cannot settle them. Read-back may disclose only currently permitted historical context; media still needs its independent purpose/grant checks.
8. P02 stops capture before creating work, reads pending/held/declined versus accepted distinctly and obtains ordinary manual checking. P03/P04 reviews the exact recorded context in a bounded recipient inbox and separately acknowledges responsibility; successor handoff requires its own acknowledgment. Keyboard/focus/zoom, interruptions, role loss and unknown writes must preserve the stopped state. Export must retain request/decision lineage with no current approval; cleanup/offboarding must account for requests and inbox references. Actual handoff, accessible understanding, response time, privacy fulfillment and first/repeat task burden remain human/operational evidence, NOT_RUN.

Additional source/time contract: pending acceptance binds explicit sender/recipient access_version, not member storage-row revision; a benign MFA update alone does not change a grant. Current member rows still participate in conditional commit checks. The five-second whole read window starts at operation entry, including authorization/source lookup; latest uses the same bounded snapshot. Inbox pages are recorded history in UUID key order, not newest-first or response eligibility. Requests freeze station name and opaque kit context. A successor may not extend metadata beyond the original inspection creation plus the configured metadata retention. Time denial and TTL do not prove erasure. The initial export hold is superseded by the locally tested complete scoped lineage described above. Missing permission, source changes, incomplete history or incompatible jobs still hold delivery. Actual privacy fulfillment remains separate and unaccepted.

Private host JSON optionally uses `handoffs` with `max_requests`, `lifetime_ms`, `reads_per_minute`, `read_window_ms`, `page_size`, and `metadata_retention_ms`; `max_requests` maps the controls field `max_requests_per_inspection`, and retention derives from the existing privacy limit. Missing configuration leaves the feature unavailable; explicit installation does not enable inspections, contact a recipient or create support staffing.

Tests must challenge forged recipient or predecessor, owner-only/other-member access, self-acceptance, duplicate/changed retry, grant/session/DATA/inspection/epoch change at final commit and delivery, response/request expiry, quota/count/page bounds, close/racing capture, successor decline, and unchanged correction counts. Current beta remains NO_GO.

## F3 measured correction references — missing contracts before implementation

Reported gaps: measurement merges unknown shape and overlap, emits no measured flat-reference cue, and operator correction text mentions a highlighted part without delivered localization. One overhead image cannot establish 3D flatness. Implement the following within F1/F2/F3/F4 and the existing roles; no new persona or pass authority is introduced.

1. An optional per-constraint `flat_reference_aspect_ratio` is an ordered finite interval with 1 <= minimum <= maximum <= 2000, the existing bounded-image aspect domain. It defaults absent and is omitted from canonical serialization when unset, preserving old recipe/idempotency bodies. Owners explicitly enroll it in a new inactive revision and qualify references/cases before approval. Never parse free-text visibility instructions as measurement configuration. The interval describes an observable planar projection, not height, angle or physical pose.
2. For a recognized expected broad class with that reference, measure oriented contour aspect and conservatively propagate the enrolled planar measurement error through both lengths. An interval not wholly inside the enrolled reference (or unbounded by its error) stays UNOBSERVABLE. The existing diagnostic code `not_flat` means flat-reference projection is unresolved; it is an observation request cue, never an assertion that 3D pose has been measured. Preserve its numeric interval when bounded. The existing PLACE_FLAT action asks for a new flat presentation and fresh same-kit measurement. Without an enrolled reference, do not manufacture this cue or infer flatness from a matching count.
3. Distinguish a visibly nonconvex multi-hole connected region from a supported convex flat-bracket silhouette. Keep the region unclassified and constraints UNOBSERVABLE; emit the existing `overlap` action cue as possible touching/overlap, not inferred object identities/counts. Other unknown blobs remain unknown and may require REVIEW. A candidate correction reason must match its measured reason and broad-class/unknown status. Spatial action publication needs an actual bounded measured target; a reason string alone cannot invent a region.
4. Correction geometry belongs to the exact rectified retained crop, not the moving camera preview. Retain bounded output dimensions, candidate boxes, reason and optional aspect interval in the typed worker result. Parent publication checks all boxes against the actual output profile, binds a correction-basis digest to the source result, crop/source hashes, calibration and observation, and stores it atomically with the action/evidence. Legacy records without a basis have no inferred overlay. Reconstruct/verify the basis before returning geometry or image bytes; mismatched/missing halves fail closed. Existing role/purpose/deadline/read quotas remain unchanged, and owner metadata alone must not disclose image-region detail.
5. The inline operator correction reference reuses authenticated retained-image metadata/download checks and exact length/hash/binding verification. Numbered regions render only over those verified crop bytes, with a matching textual list and explicit historical/not-current labels. Stop the live camera before opening this reference. No cached live box or guessed homography is used. Hidden/offline/expiry, selection/action/inspection change, clear, acknowledgment, camera restart and exit cancel/revoke local display. Acknowledgment cannot clear a measured finding or refund the two-request budget; fresh same-kit reinspection and the deterministic verifier remain required.

6. Export trace gap reported before implementation: historical manifests currently omit measured-correction provenance. New completed observation/event records must retain matching correction-basis digests; a chosen spatial action also records its permitted kind and exact candidate indices. Exports retain those bounded summaries and source lineage, never crop coordinates or private result JSON. Recompute the observation basis from its bounded result/crop/source bindings before collection; require its completion event to carry the same basis. Bind the event to the actual recorded action ID/observation, kind, targets and basis, and retain that action row among the final guarded source references; a different allowed action is not a faithful trace. Missing/mismatched halves or invalid action summaries fail closed. Legacy records without a measured basis remain unchanged and acquire no invented targets. Selected image delivery still needs its existing independently authorized metadata/binary checks; this trace grants no new image scope or current approval.
Qualification/source artifacts affected by changed measurement code require current-version revalidation. Synthetic circles/rectangles verify software contracts only. Physical envelopes, human interpretation, useful effort, target/provider and beta acceptance remain unproven. Original failing image/contract tests, boundary tests, actual worker/API/evidence checks and rendered correction/recovery checks must be retained before claiming the F3 journey delivered.

Target `/api/v1` contract. The protected identity subset is implemented with a service dependency and local database/API tests; [build status](BUILD-STATUS.md) identifies remaining endpoints and provider/runtime gaps. There is no hosted deployment or default configured sign-in. Versioned OpenAPI export/compatibility checks remain build work. All timestamps are UTC internally and localized for display. Durations use monotonic timers where possible. Server receipt/permit age governs freshness, never a trusted browser wall clock.

## State machine

States: CREATED, OBSERVING, ACTION_REQUIRED, REVIEW_REQUIRED, PASSED_CURRENT, INVALIDATED, CLOSED. No autonomous SHIPPED state exists.

| Event | Allowed transition | Required checks |
|---|---|---|
| Start capture | CREATED/INVALIDATED -> OBSERVING | Active approved bindings; valid station ownership |
| Fresh satisfying finding | OBSERVING/PASSED_CURRENT -> PASSED_CURRENT | All predicates and stability hold; linked durable evidence; lease uses original acceptance time |
| Fresh violation/uncertainty while passed | PASSED_CURRENT -> INVALIDATED -> OBSERVING/ACTION_REQUIRED/REVIEW_REQUIRED | Withdraw current result before considering any new action; quality/constraints never latch an earlier pass |
| Useful unresolved observation | OBSERVING -> ACTION_REQUIRED | Action valid and budget remaining |
| Action completed | ACTION_REQUIRED -> OBSERVING | Bound action ID; idempotent acknowledgment; fresh capture required |
| Budget exhausted/unsupported/failure | Any active state -> REVIEW_REQUIRED | Reason and event recorded |
| Detected change or binding/freshness failure | Any active state -> INVALIDATED | Clear pass immediately; discard old pending results |
| Recovery after observation interruption | INVALIDATED -> OBSERVING | Same record keeps budgets; fresh supported capture and bindings required |
| Reviewed restart/next kit | Close old record, then create a distinct CREATED record | REVIEW_REQUIRED cannot restart in place; require reason/disposition for unresolved work, fresh epoch/permits and no re-use of old evidence |
| Close | Any non-CLOSED -> CLOSED | Preserve final historical snapshot; no current status |

Effective state is never PASSED_CURRENT after its lease expires even if the stored row has not been cleaned up. Strong reads check binding versions and current lease on every result fetch/export display. Response includes `effective_state`, `last_observed_at`, `expires_at`, `remaining_lease_ms`, `state_version` and `reason_codes`. Client computes `receipt_monotonic + max(0, remaining_lease_ms - full_request_duration_ms)`, equivalent to `request_start_monotonic + remaining_lease_ms` when the remainder is positive; do not subtract the round trip twice. It clears on capture failure, focus loss or tab return. A frozen browser can display stale pixels; always show timestamp and disclaim unattended physical enforcement.

Station `epoch` is a fencing token incremented on acquire/takeover/restart. One active inspection per station. Capture permits contain server nonce, station epoch, inspection ID and next sequence, stored once and consumed conditionally. Permit lifetime and maximum observation age are separate bounds; the stricter age from permit issuance applies to accepted observations. The camera must capture after receiving the permit. Check permit age again at measurement/pass commit, not just upload admission. Decoder, planner or storage delay cannot extend the original observation acceptance time or its result lease. Challenge reuse is rejected; it cannot prove a malicious browser used a real camera.

Permit issue requires the originating operator session, expected inspection version/station epoch, complete current authority snapshot and remaining dependency/session deadline. It advances the inspection version, reserves the next sequence without claiming a verified observation and stores only a nonce hash. An authorized idempotent retry derives the original nonce only while the original permit remains current/unconsumed/fresh; it never extends either deadline. A pending fresh permit blocks another issue. A permit past its stricter observation-age bound may be atomically expired/replaced without advancing admitted sequence. A pending admitted frame blocks further permits until explicitly completed or failed by the observation workflow; new requests cannot silently replace an in-flight worker.

Internal upload admission requires the exact current permit/nonce/sequence, bounded bytes, current inspection version and both monotonic and UTC dependency deadlines. It hashes the actual bytes on the server, atomically consumes the permit, advances newest admitted sequence, creates a pending observation reservation and records accepted/permit ticks, runtime/station/configuration context digest and byte/hash provenance. No raw frame or nonce is persisted in DynamoDB. The separate three-uploads/second station counter applies, alongside mutating-control quota for permit issue. A consumed permit is never re-exchanged after an ambiguous result; subsequent API retry handling must use its bound operation record. Admission alone is neither measurement, durable evidence nor machine approval. Observation upload/orchestration is not public until bounded decoding, storage, completion/failure and freshness handling are connected.

### Public observation upload boundary

`GET /inspections/{id}/capture-context` is an originating-operator read, requiring current inspection/runtime/configuration/purpose authority and a configured publication service. It exposes only bound recipe name/revision/visible constraints, calibration ID/hash/camera label, frame dimensions, normalized retained-work polygon, origin and policy mode. Compute the polygon from the server's qualified profile; no media/key, arbitrary browser geometry or client readiness flag is accepted. Recheck a complete collected snapshot and expiry before returning it. Context is informational; every prepare, permit and upload independently revalidates authority.

Browser context version 1 uses `center_crop_resize_no_upscale`: center-crop the actual camera frame to the enrolled aspect ratio, then downscale to the profile frame dimensions, with no mirroring/rotation or upscaling. The exact resulting rectangle is previewed/transmitted, including enrolled markers; the work polygon identifies retained evidence. A profile must be qualified using this same transform. Missing geometry, insufficient source resolution, unknown context version or unavailable fresh-frame callbacks blocks capture. Physical qualification cannot be inferred from this convention or synthetic context tests.

`POST /inspections/{id}/observations` requires the originating active operator, Origin/CSRF, If-Match inspection revision and Idempotency-Key. Parse only after session/role/source-inspection checks and configured publication dependencies. Accept `multipart/form-data` with exactly four parts: one file named `image` (declared JPEG or PNG), text `permit_id` (canonical UUID), `nonce` (43-character URL-safe capability), and `sequence` (positive decimal integer). Reject duplicate/unknown parts, duplicate headers, nested multipart, encoded/compressed transfer, malformed/truncated boundaries and content-type/signature mismatch. Filenames are ignored and never used as paths. Neither findings, timestamps, hashes, purpose, object key nor pass assertions are accepted from the client.

Cap image bytes at 2 MiB and the complete encoded body at 2 MiB + 8 KiB; bound metadata/header bytes and four parts during parsing. Keep input in bounded memory, without filesystem spooling, remote fetches or raw-image logging. Enforce a five-second receive deadline including chunked input; that deadline does not extend the permit's stricter one-second freshness bound. Decode only in the resource-limited worker, under its existing decoded-pixel, CPU/memory and call limits. The API must not block its event loop on database/worker/storage operations. Client disconnect before admission creates no reservation; failure after admission uses the exact withdrawal-only reconciliation path. HTTP body deadline/memory checks do not qualify target concurrency, transient-buffer duration or edge protection.

The configured observation service admits at most two in-flight HTTP uploads per process, and one per station, before reading bodies. Tickets are server-owned and released on parse/process failure or cancellation. This bounds simultaneously buffered inputs independently of the worker pool and durable inspection allocation. It is not a deployment-wide edge limiter or permission to run multiple serving processes.

Cancellation during a blocking dispatch retains its ticket until that owned thread finishes; cancellation during preflight also releases any ticket acquired after the caller disappeared.
