A disconnect does not create retry authority or automatically restart processing. A dispatched bounded operation may finish only against its original session, dependencies and freshness deadline. Compute effective lease after the last blocking authentication/metadata read, never before it; a delayed read can only withdraw or shorten current status.

Admission atomically persists an operation record alongside nonce consumption, next sequence and observation reservation. Its request digest binds inspection, original expected revision, permit, nonce digest, sequence, actual byte SHA-256/length and declared image content type. Same actor/key/body returns the original observation reference and recomputed effective status under current permissions; it never replays processing, consumes another slot/quota/action, exposes the nonce, or renews a lease. Same key with any changed bound field returns 409. Another session, even for the same person, cannot replay an originating-session operation. A concurrent loser may refetch its exact operation; it cannot reapply admission to changed state. Pending/failed historical operation references remain explicit, and retry does not silently restart lost processing. Durable processing/orphan recovery is a separate required workflow.

Response contains `observation_id`, `sequence`, original operation status/replay indicator, typed measurement metadata only when completed, and a freshly derived inspection `status`. Exclude raw input, cropped image/base64, object keys/versions and capabilities. Status/action after processing is authoritative only within its original remaining lease. Disabled/unconfigured dependencies return 503; malformed input 400/415; size limit 413; receive timeout 408; origin/role/source violations 403; stale/conflicting operation 409; exhausted station allowance 429. Runtime-factory, edge/load and rendered camera evidence remain separate from local HTTP integration.

Warm workers are held in a server-owned registry with at most two live processes. Each binding includes station UUID, station epoch, runtime incarnation, calibration hash and a random worker incarnation. Prepare only through authorized inspection context; never accept a client worker-ready flag. An existing binding may be reused only while the same worker remains alive with matching context. Replacement requires a newer station fence (or runtime), and must not race an active analysis. A failed worker cannot silently restart under the old epoch. Release/shutdown closes only owned registry processes. The registry is an in-process resource bound in addition to the durable two-slot allocation; it does not replace it or establish a network/filesystem sandbox.

`POST /inspections/{id}/prepare-capture` takes an empty JSON object, expected inspection version in If-Match, Origin/CSRF and an idempotency key. It loads the server's immutable calibration JSON, verifies its profile/origin/hash, warms the actual registry worker, then rechecks authority before journaling readiness. Missing profile data or worker failure denies preparation. A ready response is informational; permit issue independently checks the live server registry. One registry belongs to one runtime and retains at most 256 station fence histories; a new runtime gets a new registry. Close/release/takeover/profile changes notify the registry after the durable fence commit; invalidation can interrupt an owned active child and release its lease when analysis exits. Late invalidation names the old epoch/runtime and cannot retire a newer worker.

Internal observation failure handling is withdrawal-only. It may reconcile this runtime's exact admitted/processing reservation after the originating user logs out; it cannot publish a finding, enable/restart capture, create work or reset budgets. An exact still-pending worker/storage/provenance failure clears pending authority, records REVIEW_REQUIRED and advances the station fence; the allocation remains reserved until reviewed closure. An expired observation may become INVALIDATED without replacing a healthy worker, preserving admitted sequence and budgets for fresh recovery. A late callback after close/takeover/runtime change records historical discard and cannot touch new work. Failure reason enums and transaction revisions are bounded; duplicate failure does not fence twice. This internal cleanup is not exposed as an arbitrary user mutation endpoint.

Successful internal processing claims an admitted observation once under the original operator/worker binding, verifies the exact incoming byte hash, then runs the warm resource-limited worker. It records a private object-write intent before sending cropped evidence to S3. Only a verified version/checksum/encryption receipt may be linked in one conditional transaction with the completed observation, evidence asset, inspection decision, optional corrective action and audit event. Purpose, actor, runtime, station, recipe, calibration, policy and data authority are rechecked after worker/storage calls. Image/base64 is excluded from DynamoDB metadata; asset hashes describe the stored crop, while observation source hashes describe the transient incoming frame. Uncertain/failed writes leave an intent for bounded orphan reconciliation, never a public asset or pass. The configured operation purpose must remain current/unwithdrawn/unexpired; it does not authorize improvement or publicity.

Pure verifier/action transition counters are stored separately from database state_version; the API version is always the conditional repository revision. Stability timestamps and deadlines are stored as integer nanoseconds; both integer bounds and the existing verifier predicates must hold. Corrective requests are charged when issued in the same decision transaction, never on acknowledgment. ACK requires the bound originating operator, current action/observation/scene and expected inspection version, preserves budgets and clears stability/current lease before new capture. A third request becomes REVIEW_REQUIRED. No client finding/action result or evidence_durable boolean is accepted as publication authority.

Current-pass reads revalidate the completed observation, permitted committed crop asset and exact object version, with a final atomic authority snapshot after storage HEAD. They floor the remaining original monotonic lease and clamp it to live dependency/session/purpose deadlines; no read or RPC extends freshness. Missing object, expired lease, revoked grant, changed binding or dead worker withdraws effective current approval. The default status path still cannot show a pass unless the full publication service and storage dependency are configured. Configured bounded multipart upload now composes internal success processing. The default unconfigured application still denies inspection workflows; hosted/runtime-factory and camera UI acceptance remain separate work.

Worker results may carry a bounded JPEG of the rectified work ROI, plus its derived SHA-256, in addition to the incoming-frame SHA-256 and calibration hash. The image is an explicit derivative, not the original input. JPEG evidence is capped at 160 KiB so its base64 representation and bounded measurement metadata fit the 256-KiB child reply. Failure to produce a valid cropped evidence image cannot yield a durable-evidence predicate. Do not persist image/base64 inside DynamoDB result JSON or expose it through ordinary status metadata; raw incoming frames remain transient. Byte provenance, derived crop, calibration and context hashes must remain distinct in observation/asset records.

Private S3 frame writes use server-generated deployment/UUID keys, expected bucket account, conditional no-overwrite, SHA-256 checksum, explicit SSE-KMS key and a non-null object VersionId. The adapter verifies version-pinned HEAD metadata/checksum/size/type/encryption before returning a storage receipt; reads pin that same version, bound bytes and verify actual SHA-256. ETag is not treated as a content hash. A missing/ambiguous acknowledgement or verification failure yields no receipt and no pass; orphan reconciliation is a separate durable-job requirement. No browser object key, bucket, KMS key, URL or presigned download is accepted by this adapter. Real AWS bucket versioning/IAM/KMS/retention and recovery tests remain required; SDK stub tests are only protocol evidence. Asset authorization, purposes, durable event linking and deletion are service-layer work beyond the raw object adapter.

Station enrollment/profile updates accept only a bounded name and calibration profile ID, and require a current approved calibration dependency receipt. Each receipt binds calibration head/hash, environment, data epoch, exact reference/purpose versions and an unexpired deadline; its private writer is separate qualification work. Enrollment assigns a UUID and consumes no inspection slot. Acquiring requires the expected station state version, epoch and currently displayed owner logical session ID (nullable). Re-acquiring with another operation advances the fence; taking over another session additionally requires `takeover_ack=true` and a bounded reason. It never silently steals an idle/expired session. Profile changes clear ownership and advance the epoch. Heartbeat is an owning-operator control operation; it records server time and does not extend camera evidence freshness or grant automatic takeover rights.

Acquire/takeover, release or profile change atomically closes any prior active inspection, clears its current lease/pending authority, removes its single allocation reservation and advances the station fence. ACTION_REQUIRED/REVIEW_REQUIRED work additionally needs an explicit disposition (`rework`, `reject`, `cancelled` or `human_accepted`); this creates historical human disposition only, never machine approval. Missing/inconsistent reservation state denies reconciliation instead of resetting counts. Idempotent retries cannot close/release twice or reclaim an obsolete fence, and return the historical operation result with a current station view. Release remains possible with inspection disabled or through a superseded API process, but only by the owning enabled operator session. New acquisition/start requires the current runtime incarnation. Inspection creation additionally requires effective runtime enablement, matching approved recipe/calibration and active deterministic policy, and atomically reserves one of at most two slots. It creates only CREATED, never a pass. Policy/dependency evidence writers remain separate implementation work. The station version/epoch and bound policy/recipe/calibration/runtime/data/actor rows must all be fenced at commit.

Station control admission uses the proposed eight operations per fixed server second per station, persisted with the operation transaction; it does not consume the separate observation-upload allowance. A conflict refetches explicitly; an exhausted counter returns 429. The station list reads at most 20 rows per page; its cursor is only a canonical station UUID position within the authenticated deployment, never an authorization capability. Every page is reauthorized. No scan is used to fill a page.

Additional control endpoints are `GET /stations/{id}` and `POST /stations/{id}/heartbeat`. Acquire/release/heartbeat bodies carry the expected epoch, and If-Match carries station state version; acquire also carries nullable expected owner session. `POST /inspections` carries station ID/version/epoch, recipe ID and opaque kit ID. `POST /inspections/{id}/close` carries expected station epoch, reason and optional disposition, with If-Match for inspection state version; it releases the slot and advances the station fence while retaining ownership. Mutating station controls share the eight-per-second counter; read/observation/action quotas still require full pipeline integration. Initial internal runtime startup creates an empty allocation head exactly once; subsequent startup/admission cannot recreate a missing head. Existing slots survive restart until explicit reconciliation. The station admission service itself publishes CREATED/history or conservative INVALIDATED views. When configured, the app delegates effective observation/pass publication to ObservationService; local publication components now exist, while full provider/operational acceptance remains incomplete.

## Pass predicate and atomicity

Freshness uses the API authority's monotonic ticks within its current runtime epoch. Persist permit issue/observation acceptance ticks and set the effective result deadline to `min(accepted_tick + current_result_lease_ms, permit_issued_tick + max_observation_age_ms)`. Recheck after transaction/read completion; late commits can never publish an effective current pass. Absolute UTC times are display/audit fields, not a browser freshness authority. Restart changes the epoch instead of reusing old monotonic values. Stability may include earlier same-scene observations, but each current result depends on fresh fully re-evaluated latest evidence.

Clamp the returned remaining lifetime further to known originating-session, required dependency/purpose and control-scope expiries. Revalidate those expiries before publication/read; if trustworthy conversion to the current authority clock is unavailable, deny current status. Unknown future revocations still require a new snapshot or client expiry; this is not zero-latency remote invalidation.

Observation-upload and control quotas are separate in [controls](controls.yaml). At two frames/second, permit plus upload already requires four HTTP requests/second before polling; a single three-request ceiling would reject the intended workflow. Apply the control ceiling to station/permit/status/action requests, and configured principal/job/global allowances to non-station work. Every quota denial is bounded and explicit; never retain rejected frames for later processing as current.

Charge the corrective-request budget atomically when a new action is created, not when its acknowledgment arrives. Duplicate selection/ACK never charges twice, and an abandoned action still consumes its request. A third corrective request is denied even if the first two were not acknowledged. ACK cannot create an action or reset the budget.

Read effective status with a `TransactGetItems` snapshot containing the inspection, station, originating session/membership, runtime/control and data-authority heads and all bound active configuration heads; independently strong reads are insufficient for a consistent multi-item view. Re-evaluate lease time after the snapshot returns and emit only the remaining conservative lease. A subsequent change still requires the next observation/read or client expiry to clear the display; no remote UI has zero-latency invalidation. [AWS transactional reads](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/APIReference/API_TransactGetItems.html).

Pass requires: approved active recipe, calibration and policy with available permitted reference dependencies; matching software deployment identity and runtime epoch; enabled inspection-control head and current data-authority/dependency receipt; current originating session/membership and operator grant; current station fence; all required constraints SATISFIED; no disallowed extras/unknown regions; adequate quality; stable observations; fresh accepted sequence; linked durable evidence. A probabilistic planner cannot satisfy any predicate.

Successful observation publication first claims the exact pending admission under its originating operator and live worker/profile bindings. Actual uploaded bytes must match admission length/SHA-256. The server records a private object intent before storage, containing the observation, server-owned object key, raw-input SHA-256, derived rectified-ROI SHA-256, context digest and operation purpose. The bounded worker supplies measurements and cropped evidence, never authorization. A versioned/checksummed private object acknowledgment plus pinned HEAD verification precede the transaction linking the asset, completed observation, inspection/verifier state, event, optional corrective action and linked intent. Preserve uploaded/unlinked intents on uncertain or rejected publication for reconciliation; they grant no reads or pass. No image/base64 belongs in DynamoDB result metadata.

After storage, recheck exact pending sequence, current actor/session, runtime/station/configuration/data/purpose bindings, worker liveness and original monotonic deadline. CAS conflicts never retry approval against newer state. Store stability and lease timestamps as integer nanoseconds. A separate machine-state version feeds the pure verifier/planner; repository revisions also reflect admission and cannot substitute for that transition counter. Charge correction requests at issue; ACK preserves consumed budget, clears stability/lease and requires fresh verification. Effective status checks current permitted asset/purpose and pinned object availability for a pass, then takes a final atomic snapshot of all collected rows and rechecks original deadlines. Missing or inconsistent evidence yields INVALIDATED with zero lease, including a stored pass missing its observation pointer. A read never renews the old lease. Storage failure or an orphan intent is not durable evidence for publication. These are implementation requirements; local SDK protocol tests cannot prove AWS durability.

Use one DynamoDB transaction: conditional inspection Update with expected version/sequence and status; station ConditionCheck; originating session/membership and runtime/control/data-authority-head checks; active recipe/calibration/policy-head checks; event Put with unique event ID; capture permit consumption when applicable. Combine conditions with updates on a given item; do not operate on the same item twice in a transaction. Reject conflicts with 409 and refetch; never blindly reapply a pass to new state. Recipe activation writes a new head version; pass transactions checking the old head fail. Live reads against changed heads derive INVALIDATED even before a sweeper persists it. See [AWS transaction reference](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/APIReference/API_TransactWriteItems.html).

An application-level idempotency record scopes keys to user + endpoint + resource and hashes the request. Same key/body returns the historical operation result but recomputes effective freshness. Same key/different body returns 409. Use explicit retention, not only DynamoDB's transaction token window. Events order by server-assigned sequence, not client timestamps.

Authorization is revalidated at sensitive commits, not only before slow work starts. Session/membership revision conditions bind the actor to the operation. Disable, logout, role loss or station release makes originating current results ineffective. Long-running exports/evaluations recheck permission before new reads/output; an already-authorized erasure job may finish under its service lease after the requestor leaves. No downloaded bytes can be retroactively revoked.

The API process acquires a durable monotone runtime epoch and starts with inspection admission disabled until current readiness/recovery checks allow owner enablement. Work and passes from an obsolete incarnation cannot commit or read as current even when the code hash is unchanged. Decoder restart discards pending work and invalidates affected station context; absent runtime/control state is a denial.

Runtime startup uses an internal, non-HTTP operation with explicit artifact/controls SHA-256 digests and a random process-incarnation UUID. It conditionally advances the durable runtime epoch and control version, disables inspection, clears the previous enable receipt and journals the transition in the same transaction. Repeating the same start identity is historical replay only and must match both digests; a superseded process cannot reclaim its old epoch by replay. The returned handle must be checked against a fresh runtime head before admitting/committing work. Existing station/inspection/allocation records are not silently reset or discarded at startup; their runtime bindings become ineffective and require explicit reconciliation.

Owner enablement accepts only a server-owned readiness receipt UUID and expected control version. The immutable receipt must bind the deployment, explicit environment, runtime incarnation/epoch, artifact/controls digests, current data-authority epoch, exact previous control version and an unexpired deadline. Issue/expiry values are strict integer milliseconds; the receipt is never future-issued and lives at most 60 seconds. Its attested checks must cover OpenCV 5, storage, configuration dependencies, authorization, worker health, recovery and the environment's release gates. A separate server-owned owner-review record binds that receipt digest and current owner identity/access revision. These records have no public write endpoint. Missing/failed/mismatched evidence denies enablement. The receipt and review writers are separate unfinished assurance work; local component tests cannot create a deployment-ready receipt. Commit checks the runtime/data/receipt/review and current actor rows atomically; control enablement is never an assertion from body booleans. Every new admission/read must still check current readiness/deadlines and the approving owner's current grant, rather than treating enablement as permanent readiness. Disable always clears the enable binding, advances control version, and journals a bounded non-sensitive reason; it remains available to an authorized owner even through a superseded process. Idempotent replay returns its historical operation result plus the current control view and never reenables an obsolete runtime.

A deployment allocation head atomically reserves/releases active-inspection slots with station and inspection changes. Close/release/takeover reconciles a slot once; retries do not double-release or allocate a third active station. Station ownership without an active inspection is not a consumed inspection slot.

## Entities

| Entity | Required attributes and relationships |
|---|---|
| DeploymentIdentity | deployment/org ID, region, code SHA, artifact hash, controls digest, environment |
| RuntimeControlHead | monotone runtime epoch/control version, active artifact/config, inspection-enabled flag and current recovery/launch receipt |
| DataAuthorityHead | deployment data-use/revision epoch, changed synchronously with purpose/deletion/reference/label mutations; dependency validation and job/candidate publication fence |
| DependencyReceipt | scoped immutable manifest/reference digests, validated data epoch and earliest required purpose/asset expiry; cannot authorize beyond its deadline |
| SessionRecord | non-secret logical ID, hashed opaque cookie lookup, principal, active/access revision, expiry and revocation; provider secrets never exported |
| Invitation | target identity binding, roles, expiry, one-time capability hash, pending/accepted/revoked/expired state and capacity reservation |
| OwnershipTransfer | source/target grant revisions, target MFA/acceptance evidence, expiry and transfer status |
| AllocationHead | deployment active-inspection count/version and operation-linked reservations |
| UserAccess | Cognito subject, invited status, roles, disabled flag, access version |
| RecipeRevision | Immutable F1 schema; approval and qualification references |
| CalibrationRevision | Camera, frame dimensions, physical scale, fitted geometry, measured errors, envelope, approval |
| Station | ID, active epoch, owner session, heartbeat, approved calibration, active inspection |
| Inspection | ID, opaque kit ID, bound versions, status/version, sequence, correction/provider counts, originating session/access version, runtime/control/data-authority bindings, scene generation, pass deadline |
| Observation | ID, permit/sequence, server times, quality, objects, constraints, admitted/verified sequence and monotonic ticks, authority/dependency versions, duration, selected evidence refs |
| Action | ID, constrained enum/ROI, source observation, alternatives, policy mode, source and validation observation IDs, decision reservation, result |
| EvidenceEvent | Unique ID, inspection version, event type, reason codes, versions and hashes; no bearer tokens |
| HumanDisposition | Immutable standalone human decision, reason/check declaration, digest/prior ID, actor and original inspection/version/observation context; latest pointer separate from machine state |
| OutcomeLabel | Tuple IDs, class/ground truth, reviewer, revision, provenance, permitted use, declared independence and reviewed conflict/independence status |
| EvaluationRun | Split manifests/hashes, code and environment, data-authority/dependency versions, metrics/raw records, exclusions and failed cases |
| PolicyRevision | Algorithm/config, eligible action statistics, evaluation manifest, approval, rollback predecessor |
| EvidenceAsset | Server-owned object key/hash, source kind, permission/deletion dates, committed/orphan/deleted status and reference/evaluation dependency lineage |
| MaintenanceJob | Export/delete/replay type, durable lease, validated manifest/build/budget, retries, owner, cancellation and progress/failure receipt |
| PurposeRevision | permitted uses, recorded notice/permission references, expiry/withdrawal version and dependent resources |
| InferenceReservation | decision ID, inspection and deployment allowance, bounded estimated usage, status and uncertain-provider completion |
| InferenceAllowanceHead | target/model/cost basis, approved request/token allowance and expiry, reserved usage/version; atomic global accounting survives restarts |

DynamoDB tables: `proofpack-state` for current/immutable configuration and session rows; `proofpack-events` for bounded event/label/run metadata. Partition by deployment and resource; never place images in DynamoDB. No unbounded scan in the station path. Auth and object-prefix checks derive from deployment configuration and session, not client-supplied org ID. Test table/index access patterns in DynamoDB Local and real AWS separately.

## Endpoint inventory

Workbench review reads are informational and must not manufacture evidence or operation authority. `GET /auth/session` allows an active member or its own restricted invitee to read session kind, server-derived CSRF and bound invitation ID; no cookie, provider token or other user's data is exposed. Restricted sessions cannot use normal member APIs. Invite acceptance requires viewing the current notice, explicit acknowledgment and the identity-bound capability; decline is separate and never coerces optional reuse. Owner-created invitations remain NOT_SENT until a separately authorized delivery mechanism actually succeeds. The browser holds a newly returned capability only in memory, hides it by default, and never puts it in URLs/storage or automatically contacts a recipient.

`GET /owner-transfers/{id}` is limited to the source owner or bound active target; unknown/unrelated transfers return 404. Show source/target names and IDs, versions, expiry/status, and eligibility for target acceptance based on current grants and recent MFA. A target need not already be owner. An explicit acceptance is revalidated by the existing transfer transaction and requires fresh sign-in afterward; a preview cannot transfer ownership.

`GET /recipes/{id}/qualifications/{qualification_id}` is an owner/reviewer read of a matching current immutable recipe qualification with live permitted dependencies. It may expose a recorded failed result, but `eligible_for_approval` is false for failure or absent matching draft. Missing/expired/withdrawn/mismatched dependencies deny the read, rather than manufacturing a successful record. Expose digest, revision, origin, reviewer reference and remaining validity; no images, object keys, raw private receipt or assumed reviewer independence. Owner approval independently rechecks successful qualification and all bindings. Editing/changing the draft, qualification ID or selected recipe clears the browser's prior review.

`GET /admin/readiness/{receipt_id}` is an owner/MFA read that validates the existing runtime/control/data/build/owner-review receipt exactly as enablement does. It returns a bounded checks summary, receipt digest, control version and remaining validity. It never starts the runtime, writes readiness, disables a gate or enables inspection. UI enablement requires an explicit reviewed selection and confirmation and is distinct from beta release. Both read previews expire locally using conservative monotonic request-start bounds; backend commits never trust that local timer.

All domain writes require session authentication, authorization, CSRF token, Origin checks, request ID and idempotency key. Login/callback are provider authentication flows. Invitation acceptance uses a restricted authenticated invitee session plus its valid one-time invitation; it cannot access ordinary app resources before activation. Logout safely clears/revokes its session even when it has expired; never refresh a grant merely to log out. Mutable updates additionally require `If-Match` state version. IDs are opaque UUIDs; strings bounded; extra fields rejected; pagination bounded to 100.

| Method/path | Role | Body/result contract |
|---|---|---|
| GET /auth/login; GET /auth/callback | Login flow | Server state/nonce/PKCE; allowlisted redirect only |
| POST /auth/logout | Any invited user | Revoke server session and clear cookie |
| GET /me | Any enabled invited user | Current server-authorized roles; no provider token |
| GET /auth/notice | Authenticated member or bound invitee | Current owner-configured operational notice text/version/hash; optional reuse remains off; no invented notice when configuration is absent |
| GET /users | Owner | Bounded membership status/roles; no credentials or session secrets |
| POST /invitations | Owner | Verified target, explicit roles/expiry; reserve pending+enabled account capacity |
| GET /invitations | Owner | Bounded signed-cursor metadata list including terminal invitations; no capability or email hash |
| GET /invitations/{id} | Owner or bound authenticated invitee | Current metadata/version only; invitee restricted to its own identity-bound invitation; no capability or email hash |
| POST /invitations/{id}/accept | Bound authenticated invitee | Current notice version/hash acknowledgment plus capability/identity/expiry checks; record acknowledgment, consume once and activate membership; revoke/clear restricted cookie and require fresh provider sign-in |
| POST /invitations/{id}/decline | Bound authenticated invitee | No notice acknowledgment required; decline own pending invitation, release its reservation once and revoke/clear restricted cookie; no membership created |
| POST /invitations/{id}/revoke | Owner | Version/reason; deny future acceptance and release pending capacity once |
| PATCH /users/{id}/access | Owner | Expected access version, roles/enabled flag; MFA/last-owner checks; deny reads before provider reconciliation |
| POST /users/{id}/sessions/revoke | Owner | Scoped session revocation; originating inspection authority invalidates |
| POST /owner-transfers; POST /owner-transfers/{id}/accept | Owner initiates / bound target accepts | Current grants, target MFA, explicit acceptance and guarded atomic transfer; no anonymous bootstrap |
| GET/POST /recipes | Any invited approved-scope read / owner write | Paginated approved summaries; owner/reviewer candidate detail only within permitted scope / validated draft |
| GET/PUT /recipes/{id}/draft | Owner writes | Expected version; new revision on approved source |
| POST /recipes/{id}/approve | Owner | Revision + qualification run; active binding update |
| POST /recipes/{id}/retire | Owner | Reason + version; affected live results invalidate |
| POST /reference-assets | Owner | Bounded verified JPEG/PNG + purpose/deadline; server-owned key/hash, no remote fetch |
| GET /assets/{id}; GET /assets/{id}/download | Authorized role/purpose | Scoped metadata or app-proxied bytes; session/membership/expiry/deletion checked per new request |
| GET /calibrations | Any invited, scoped read | Approved profile summaries; restricted qualification details/media |
| POST /calibrations | Owner | Camera metadata + server-owned references |
| POST /calibrations/{id}/qualify | Owner | Bounded diagnostic run; report or failure, never a live inspection pass |
| POST /calibrations/{id}/approve; POST /calibrations/{id}/retire | Owner | Matching qualification/version/purpose; active head changes invalidate results |
| GET/POST /stations; PATCH /stations/{id} | Any invited read / owner write | Bounded station list/enrollment/profile update; approved calibration required; profile change fences affected work |
| POST /stations/{id}/acquire | Operator | Expected epoch/session; explicit takeover reason/acknowledgment; atomically close/fence prior work |
| POST /stations/{id}/release | Owning operator session | Expected epoch; close active work, release allocation and fence once |
| POST /inspections | Operator | Station, approved recipe, opaque kit ID; bound inspection |
| POST /inspections/{id}/capture-permits | Operator | Expected version/epoch; short-lived next nonce |
| POST /inspections/{id}/observations | Operator | Multipart bounded image + permit/sequence; typed finding and effective status |
| POST /inspections/{id}/actions/{action_id}/complete | Operator | Completion acknowledgment; fresh reinspection follows |
| GET /inspections/{id}/capture-context | Originating operator | Current bound recipe/preview geometry and origin; no media or capture authority |
| GET /inspections/{id} | Same-org invited | Strong current status and lease; no-cache |
| POST /inspections/{id}/dispositions | Operator | Reason and human decision; distinct record, never machine pass |
| POST /inspections/{id}/close | Operator | Freeze historical record, invalidate current lease |
| POST /inspections/{id}/exports | Authorized operator/reviewer | Audited export job; no media beyond role/purpose |
| POST /labels; POST /labels/{id}/revisions | Reviewer | Scoped tuple/expected revision, ground truth, provenance, declared independence and method; no silent overwrite |
| POST /datasets/manifests; GET /datasets/manifests/{id} | Reviewer | Validated permitted asset/label IDs, origin, group split, versions/hashes; no arbitrary path/code |
| POST /policies | Reviewer | Bounded draft candidate using installed algorithm/parameter schema; no active update |
| GET /policies/{id} | Authorized scope | Approved summary or owner/reviewer candidate detail; no ungranted media access |
| POST /evaluations; GET /evaluations/{id} | Reviewer submit / owner or reviewer scoped result | Validated manifest + candidate/build/budget; offline leased job; owner gets approval summary without implicit reviewer/media privileges |
| POST /policies/{id}/promote; POST /policies/{id}/rollback | Owner | Successful run + reviewed revision; invalidate active bindings |
| POST /privacy/permissions | Owner | Versioned documented purpose/withdrawal/deadline; affect reads/jobs/dependencies immediately |
| POST /privacy/deletions | Owner | Exact scoped resources and confirmed request digest; deny reads before durable cleanup; no rollback of delivered bytes |
| GET /jobs/{id}; GET /jobs/{id}/download | Authorized requestor/owner | Reauthorized progress or permitted app-proxied export with expiring ticket; owner role alone does not broaden media purpose |
| POST /jobs/{id}/cancel | Authorized requestor/owner | Cancel export/evaluation/replay safely and reconcile partial output; cannot undo read denial or cancel committed erasure |
| GET /admin/runtime; POST /admin/disable-inspection | Owner | Runtime identity / audited kill-switch reason; control-head update fences racing commits |
| POST /admin/enable-inspection | Owner | Current readiness, scope and owner-reviewed recovery/launch receipt; expected runtime/control version; no client boolean can assert gate success |

Health endpoints `/health/live` and `/health/ready` are outside `/api/v1`; public liveness has no operational detail. Readiness checks required configuration, OpenCV major version and storage access; a failure rejects new inspections.

Errors: 400 malformed/unknown fields, 401 missing/expired session, 403 unauthorized, 404 absent or inaccessible object, 409 stale version/reused key, 413 limits, 422 unsupported recipe/input, 429 quotas, 503 dependencies unavailable. Envelope: `code`, `safe_message`, `request_id`, `retryable`, optional `current_version`; never stack traces or storage keys.

## Lifecycle rules and dependencies

Recipe HTTP create/save bodies contain a `content` object with name, kit SKU, bounded constraints, reference asset IDs, calibration profile ID and data origin; IDs/revision numbers are server-assigned. Each save creates a new immutable canonical-JSON revision and digest. Catalog state/version and active recipe head are separate: editing a draft does not replace the currently approved recipe. Create returns a UUID and catalog state version; save/approve/retire require that expected version. Approval accepts only `qualification_id`, verifies a private immutable qualification record matching that exact recipe revision/digest, environment, software artifact, current data epoch and current approved calibration, and condition-checks all required asset/purpose rows. Required reference manifests are bounded to 16 assets; each binds metadata/purpose versions and SHA-256, committed/readable status, operation purpose and unexpired deadlines. Qualification and byte/dependency-proof writers remain separate implementation work and have no public certification endpoint. Metadata or a client `passed` flag cannot create a genuine qualification.

Approval writes the catalog, active binding, event and idempotency result atomically with current owner/MFA authorization. Active reads revalidate qualification, calibration, data epoch, references/purposes and expiry; unknown or unavailable dependencies deny effective approval. Inspection commits must condition-check the returned active/dependency rows. Retire clears the active head and preserves revisions; a later edit needs a new qualification before activation. Idempotent approval replay returns its historical result plus current active status and cannot reactivate a retired recipe. Approved summaries are available to invited members; candidate summaries/details require owner or reviewer scope, with no implicit media privilege. The list is bounded to 20 catalog rows per page, may return an empty filtered page, and supplies a signed cursor bound to actor/view with a five-minute expiry. No unbounded scan fills a page.

OAuth initiation binds one random state to a separate host-only Secure/HttpOnly/SameSite=Lax browser cookie, a fixed configured callback, and any invitation UUID. Use authorization code with S256 PKCE and an ID-token nonce. Flow lifetime is at most five minutes. Persist only hashes of browser/state capabilities; derive verifier and nonce under the private deployment key. Atomically consume the flow before any code exchange, including provider-error callbacks. A wrong browser/state cannot consume it; a successful consumption, provider failure or uncertain exchange requires a fresh sign-in rather than callback replay. A restart must preserve outstanding/consumed state. Recheck expiry after consumption before contacting the provider. Configuration changes to client, provider domain, callback or key invalidate outstanding flows. Never accept a request-controlled redirect, return URL, issuer or token endpoint. The HTTP integration must clear the flow cookie and redact callback query codes/state from access logs. These flow components alone do not validate an ID token or establish MFA.

HTTP login accepts only optional canonical `invitation_id` and `mfa=required`. The latter requests the configured native TOTP ACR with `max_age=600` (or a stricter configured bound) and durably binds the requirement; it is never an assertion that MFA occurred. Completion of an MFA-required flow additionally checks signed MFA proof and original authentication freshness. Login responds with a provider redirect and `__Host-proofpack-oauth` cookie (Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Path=/, at most five minutes, no Domain). Callback accepts one state plus exactly one code or provider error, and bounded optional `error_description`; duplicate/unknown/malformed query fields are rejected without echo. Its query is stripped from the ASGI scope before application/access logging; deployment proxies must separately suppress sensitive callback queries. Success replaces the app session cookie, clears the flow cookie and redirects to the configured application home; restricted invitees still cannot use ordinary member endpoints. Failure clears the flow cookie without refreshing or replacing an existing app session. Successful app logout clears both app and pending-flow cookies; it is not provider logout or global session revocation. No arbitrary next/return URL is accepted. HTTPS origin and callback must match explicit application configuration; default unconfigured routes deny service. Unsupported HTTP methods return 405 without accepting identity assertions from request bodies.

Development admission defaults: at most 30 login starts and 60 callback attempts per deployment per fixed UTC minute. Durable counters use conditional transactions and survive instance restart; a concurrent conflict denies/retries explicitly rather than bypassing the counter. A denied login creates no flow. These are service safeguards, not a substitute for edge request/concurrency controls or expired-flow retention cleanup; all remain necessary for deployment. Query payloads are limited to 4 KiB before parsing. Browser-supplied IP/forwarding headers do not select a quota identity or application origin.

The Cognito adapter accepts only RS256 ID tokens from one configured commercial AWS user-pool issuer and app client. Fetch keys only from that issuer's fixed JWKS path; never follow token-supplied key URLs or provider redirects. Bound response sizes, transport time and key counts; require a unique matching RSA signing key and fail closed on unknown keys or malformed documents. Verify signature, issuer, exact single audience, token use, nonce, verified email and integer expiry/issue/authentication times. No JWT group or role claim grants application access. Native password-plus-TOTP MFA requires signed `amr` containing `pwd`, `otp`, `mfa`, the configured level-four `acr`, and no federated identity. Missing claims deny owner MFA rather than inferring it from account enrollment, a custom attribute or a client flag. Owner operations additionally enforce the existing recent-authentication bound. Actual pool ACR naming/feature-tier support must be verified before deployment. Provider refresh/access tokens are not persisted or exposed to the browser; no refresh grant is implemented. Offline ID-token signature validation alone does not prove provider revocation; live revocation/logout checks remain a separate integration requirement.

Identity development defaults are in controls: one-hour app sessions, one-day invitation upper bound, five-minute OAuth flow target and ten-minute transfer/recent-MFA bounds. Missing operational notice configuration blocks acceptance. Acknowledgment records its exact version/hash; it does not authorize optional improvement or publicity. A consumed restricted cookie is revoked and cleared; normal sign-in is required before the activated membership can use ordinary routes. Idempotent results are returned only after current authorization; a revoked/expired cookie cannot recover authority by replay. Provider token validation/delivery and live clock/network behavior are not yet proved.

A queued input is not a verified observation. Track newest admitted frame sequence separately from last verified sequence; a superseded worker cannot publish its result. Pending work cannot claim a newly verified frame or extend the last verified deadline. The UI names/timestamps the actual supporting observation and clears it on detected change, loss or expiry. This remains sampled evidence, not zero-latency physical enforcement.

Use a synchronously advanced data-authority epoch for ordinary application changes to purposes, deletion/read denial, required references or current label truth. Jobs and dependency-validation receipts bind the epoch before reading their scoped immutable manifests; sensitive publication/promotion checks the same epoch atomically. A changed epoch invalidates stale receipts/current results, even for conservative unrelated changes, until dependencies are revalidated and fresh observations arrive. This avoids pretending an unbounded dataset can be checked in one DynamoDB transaction. Bound manifest sizes and transaction actions; never silently omit a required dependency to fit a transaction. Background reconciliation alone cannot provide immediate revocation.

Dependency receipts also carry the earliest required asset/purpose expiry. Every new read, verification/publication and promotion checks that deadline even if an expiry sweeper has not advanced the epoch. Cached validation cannot outlive it. A real referenced-data change requiring new qualification is not silently repaired by refreshing only the receipt. Data rights/physical truth remain recorded human evidence rather than a client certification flag.

- Provision the first owner only through an approved deployment/recovery operation with explicit principal/roles. A first browser login never claims ownership. Owner grants require verified MFA. Guard ownership transfer/removal against a concurrent loss of the last enabled owner; privileged out-of-band provider changes require audited recovery.
- On shared-device logout/account change, clear private UI/camera buffers and object URLs, use protected-response no-store policy, and verify the intended identity explicitly before reopening an administrative session. Test provider SSO logout/account selection as well as app session revocation; an offline or partially failed logout cannot be reported as complete global sign-out. Already-delivered copies remain outside revocation guarantees.
- Count pending invitations plus enabled members against the five-account limit. Decline/revoke/expiry releases pending capacity once; disabled membership retains audit history but does not consume an enabled slot. Role changes on an existing account do not create a new slot. Provider delivery/provisioning is outside DynamoDB transactions: record uncertain/failing side effects and reconcile without enabling an unauthorized principal.
- Qualification results bind candidate/reference/calibration/software versions, origin and purpose. Only matching complete results can activate. Retiring a draft/failed profile is allowed; it never creates approval. Missing/expired/withdrawn required reference assets make dependent qualification unavailable. Reference/evaluation retention must be explicitly agreed before activation; the selected-inspection-frame default is not their lifetime.
- Offline jobs accept only validated scoped manifests and installed algorithms, enforce configured record/time/resource/budget limits, and use durable leases. Cancellation/restart cannot duplicate results or publish incomplete output. Label changes or permission withdrawal invalidate affected queued jobs/promotion inputs. Required bounds missing from configuration deny admission.
- A delayed model proposal is untrusted and never commits against its old state version blindly. At publication, re-read latest bindings and fresh eligible observation; require the same pending decision/scene and unresolved constraints, validate ROI against the current supported region, and CAS the latest version. Record both source and validation observations. Changed scene, terminal state, loss of grant or resolved constraints discards the proposal; its provider reservation remains consumed. Measurement/verifier continue independently.
- REVIEW_REQUIRED does not become OBSERVING in place. Record separate manual disposition/restart reason, close the old record and create a distinct inspection. Observation-loss INVALIDATED recovery can continue the same record with existing budgets. New inspection IDs cannot reuse old permits/pass/evidence. This protocol does not prove that an adversarial user physically changed kits; deployment quotas/allowances also bound repeated creation.
- While stopped, qualification/readiness diagnostics may run under owner authority but cannot issue live inspection passes. Resume requires current diagnostic/recovery evidence and a scope-specific owner decision; pre-beta qualification study approval is distinct from beta launch approval.

## Required schema examples

```json
{"constraint_id":"washers","status":"UNOBSERVABLE","reason_codes":["OVERLAP"],"observed_count":null,"measurement_interval_mm":null,"evidence_observation_id":"obs-example"}
```

```json
{"action":"SEPARATE_REGION","constraint_ids":["washers"],"observation_id":"obs-example","roi":[0.2,0.3,0.6,0.7],"decision_summary":"Overlap prevents a reliable count.","policy_mode":"deterministic_policy"}
```

Examples are synthetic schema illustrations. Implementation must replace illustrative IDs with validated UUIDs; normalized ROI requires 0<=x1<x2<=1 and 0<=y1<y2<=1. Do not persist or display private model reasoning.


## Browser workbench request lifecycle

The owner/reviewer/invitee metadata screens use current session-derived CSRF and explicit grants. Sensitive writes re-read the member session and preserve the selected resource version; the server remains authoritative for role/MFA/dependency/idempotency checks. A pending structured write keeps one operation key per unchanged method/path/version/body in panel memory (at most 16 unconfirmed signatures), reuses it only on an explicit unchanged retry, and clears it after a confirmed response or panel unmount. Nothing automatically retries or persists in URLs/browser storage. Losing panel context requires inspection of current server records before another create; the client does not claim cross-reload exactly-once operation.

Pending work disables section navigation. Unconfirmed logout suppresses privileged controls until sign-out retry or fresh access resolution. Existing qualification/readiness/transfer previews use monotonic request-start expiry, clear on edits and hidden/offline interruption, and fence delayed responses using a local review generation. A preview, checkbox or browser timer never supplies operation authority or replaces a backend transaction. Source-owner transfer preview rechecks current owner/MFA at the final authorization read; target eligibility recalculates MFA age after that read.


## Reference intake and operational purpose contract

Contract completion before implementation, October 14, 2026: reference request fields, creation of operational purposes, calibration-independent decoding and a shared diagnostic worker budget were unspecified. The following defines the missing reference intake boundary; calibration/reference diagnostic reports and physical qualification remain separate required work.

`POST /purposes` is an owner/recent-MFA operation with Origin/CSRF/idempotency. It accepts the exact configured notice version/hash, an opaque source-permission reference, explicit operational-use acknowledgment and an absolute integer expiry within a separately configured maximum lifetime. It records the owner attestation and allowed use `operation` only; improvement/publicity stay off. This is a record of the owner's authority assertion, not independent rights verification or participant consent. No default retention or publishing permission is inferred. Missing finalized notice/configured lifetime blocks creation. `GET /purposes/{id}` is an owner/reviewer scoped metadata read. `POST /purposes/{id}/withdraw` requires owner authority and If-Match and synchronously advances the data-authority epoch; it never promises erasure of downloaded copies. Purpose creation also advances that epoch.

Initial internal runtime startup creates the data-authority head exactly once together with the first runtime/allocation state, preserving any existing valid head. A missing head after a previous runtime is a recovery denial, not permission to recreate an empty authority history. This is not owner bootstrap, release approval or automatic inspection enablement.

`POST /reference-assets` takes multipart with exactly one `image` file and one ASCII JSON `metadata` field. Metadata contains only `purpose_id`, `source_permission_reference`, `data_origin` and integer `expires_at_ms`. Image bytes are at most 2 MiB; complete body at most 2 MiB plus 8 KiB; metadata at most 4 KiB; JPEG/PNG only, no remote URL/path/object key. Authenticate and check exact Origin/CSRF/idempotency headers, bounded owner intake allowance and shared decoder capacity before reading the body. Receive within five seconds without temporary-file spooling. Intake allowance and maximum purpose lifetime require explicit configuration; account ownership does not approve paid use.

Decode and normalize only in a resource-limited child sharing the existing at-most-two-process registry pool. Verify single-frame type, orientation and at most two million decoded pixels; return a metadata-free canonical JPEG with distinct source/stored hashes and dimensions. This probe proves format/byte provenance only; it cannot produce a measurement, calibration qualification, physical-origin certification or live pass. Diagnostic leases close their owned worker on completion/shutdown and do not steal or restart station bindings.

Before object writes, atomically reserve a body-bound upload operation and private server-owned object intent under the current owner/session/runtime/purpose/data authority. Use conditional versioned encrypted/checksummed storage and verified HEAD receipt. Final linking rechecks current grants/MFA, exact intent/operation and authority rows, permissions and deadlines; atomically publish asset metadata, linked intent, completed operation, audit event and an advanced data epoch. Store no image/base64 in DynamoDB. Failure/uncertainty leaves a private reconciliation intent and no public asset or activation. An exact retry returns its original pending/failed/completed reference under current authority without decoding/writing again; altered input returns 409. No failed pending operation silently becomes a new upload.

Asset metadata records declared origin with basis `owner_declaration` and physical-truth verification false. `physical` in request metadata cannot qualify physical recipes. Actual calibration/recipe qualification still needs genuine source/truth evidence and diagnostic outcomes; do not promote a client boolean into evidence. Reference expiry cannot exceed its current operational purpose. Lifecycle reads return no object key/version or bytes; download/deletion and orphan reconciliation remain separate required workflows.

`GET /purposes/options` is an owner/MFA metadata read of configured maximum lifetime, current server milliseconds and the current operational notice. It supplies no consent or creation authority. The UI requires an explicit duration/acknowledgment, checks unchanged notice before creation, and computes the requested absolute deadline from a fresh server timestamp. The server validates the actual deadline again. References also share the existing 2000-pixel maximum side bound as well as the two-million-pixel ceiling.

`GET /purposes` is a bounded owner/reviewer metadata list (at most 20 rows/page) with a canonical UUID position cursor and reauthorization on every page. It supports recovery after an uncertain create without scanning to fill pages. Old/private records that lack this owner-attestation contract do not become usable through listing. Purpose creation freezes its explicit absolute expiry for an unchanged in-page retry rather than recomputing a new body and creating a second purpose.

Reference objects use the server-owned `deployments/{deployment}/references/{asset_uuid}` namespace; inspection crops retain `frames/{asset_uuid}`. A frame-only adapter cannot be composed as reference storage. This separates retention/IAM lifecycle configuration; it is not proof that a live bucket policy or erasure job is correct.


## Calibration draft and diagnostic contract

Contract gap completion before implementation, October 14, 2026: calibration create/qualify body fields, printable layout, measured outcomes and physical approval authority were not defined. Preserve F1 scope and existing station dependency receipts while adding this boundary.

The fixed `desktop-v1` mat has an 800Ã—500 nominal grid at 3 pixels/mm, a 600Ã—300 work rectangle starting at (100,100), hence a 200Ã—100 mm work area, and DICT_4X4_50 IDs 0/1/2/3 with 64-pixel squares at (28,28), (708,28), (708,408), (28,408). Analytic marker corners are the square pixel centers through offset 63, translated by the work-area origin. The print artifact is nearest-neighbor scaled fourfold at 304.8 DPI with a 100 mm ruler outside the work area. Print actual size and verify the physical ruler; a digital template or screen cannot certify printing/lens accuracy. Existing development fixtures remain tagged synthetic and are not upgraded by sharing geometry.

`GET /calibrations/mat` supplies the fixed printable artifact to an invited member; it has no private data or qualification authority. `POST /calibrations` is owner/MFA/Origin/CSRF/idempotency protected. Its typed body identifies camera, fixed mat version, one current permitted reference asset, declared origin and explicit bounded visibility/measurement settings (marker displacement/reprojection, uncertainty, contour-area/blur thresholds and gray threshold). Server-assigned UUID/revision cannot be supplied. Fetch only the server-owned pinned reference receipt under current purpose; decode/detect/enroll only in the shared bounded child. The server derives enrolled corners and profile hash, records actual reference/source hashes and immutable draft JSON, and never accepts client marker corners, pass flags or physical-certification booleans.

Draft catalog and active calibration are separate. A draft does not activate a station. Owner/reviewer can inspect the draft/report; operators receive approved summaries only. Changes create a new immutable revision and clear pending report authority; existing active calibration remains separately bound until explicit new approval/retirement. Bodies/metadata stay bounded by the existing JSON/worker controls.

`POST /calibrations/{id}/qualify` runs a bounded diagnostic against an exact draft/state version, using a manifest of 2â€“8 current permitted reference cases with at least one expected-supported and one expected-rejected scene. Expected labels are owner-supplied test intentions, not trusted results. The child actually checks markers, camera displacement, planar residual/ROI and supported image quality; the service derives pass/fail from recorded outcomes, preserves failed cases and binds the report to profile/build/runtime/data/asset/purpose versions and the earliest expiry. It cannot issue a live inspection pass. Qualification is an immutable server-produced report, not a client boolean.

Owner approval names an existing current passing report plus expected draft version. Synthetic/printable activation is limited to non-beta environments and its exact declared origin. Physical activation additionally requires an unexpired server-owned reviewed physical-assurance record matching profile/report/source hashes, actual measured ruler/lens/scope evidence and current data authority. There is no browser assertion that substitutes for this record; its actual physical evidence/authorized writer remains separate required work. Missing proof holds physical approval while allowing diagnostic/failure review. Approval atomically writes the active profile plus a private dependency receipt binding references/purposes/current data epoch and deadline, advances its state, and records owner audit/idempotency. Retirement invalidates the active head without erasing immutable history; station/inspection reads must reject changed active bindings.

Calibration jobs require explicitly configured intake allowance and a diagnostic window of at most 60 seconds. A private body-bound processing job is reserved before fetching/decoding, then completes or fails once; exact retries return that original job rather than running another diagnostic. GET job/draft/report reads support recovery after an unconfirmed client response. Expired/revoked work may finish withdrawal bookkeeping but cannot publish a profile/report or approval. Stored-image fetching is internal and version/checksum/purpose guarded; it is not a public download capability.

Current calibration approval additionally requires the immutable qualification report named by its dependency receipt to remain present at revision one, pass canonical-content/hash verification, and match the approved profile, deployment environment, software artifact, data epoch and exact reference manifest. Its deadline bounds current use. Missing, corrupt, superseded, failed, mismatched or expired report evidence must withdraw effective approval, including after an incomplete restore. The report row participates in the same collected dependency snapshot and final transaction/read fences as the active head and source rows; no receipt-only fallback is permitted. A newer draft/report does not erase the separately bound active report, and cannot substitute for it.

### Operational outcome labels

Inspection creation persists its actual configured `artifact_sha256` for historical review; missing software provenance cannot be reconstructed from the current deployment. `GET /review/context` takes `before_observation_id` plus paired optional `action_id`/`after_observation_id`. Owner/reviewer metadata reads require completed observations and committed, currently permitted, unexpired evidence in this deployment. No raw media or object key is returned. A correction tuple requires an acknowledged action bound to the before observation and a later completed observation from the same inspection. A lone observation permits only `incorrect_finding` or `unresolved`; `resolved` requires a full tuple. This records a human judgment, never a machine pass.

`POST /labels` and `POST /labels/{id}/revisions` require explicit reviewer grant, Origin/CSRF/idempotency and a matching preview `source_context_sha256`. Bodies carry the exact tuple IDs, outcome (`resolved`, `unresolved`, `incorrect_finding`), distinct broad-class ground-truth counts (all configured recipe classes, at most four classes/30 parts), method (`physical_manual_check`, `reference_comparison`, `synthetic_fixture`), declared duration, independence declaration and bounded conflict/revision note. Method/origin must agree for physical and synthetic checks. No client `verified`, policy activation or optional-use flag is accepted. Source receipt/observation/recipe/hash/origin/versions and originating groups are derived server-side; no client grouping or software identity is trusted.

Each label revision is immutable, content-hashed and chained to the prior revision hash under a versioned head. Revisions require the head version and preserve the original tuple; conflicts never overwrite prior reviewers' work. Declared independence is distinct from reviewed independence, which remains false without a separate authorized review. Operational labels grant no improvement/publicity permission and are not an evaluation manifest. Label creation/correction synchronously advances data authority and journals the reviewer and source hash, fencing in-flight evaluations/approvals. Current reads and writes recheck actor/source/purpose/data/runtime dependencies and expiry; historical inspection closure alone does not prohibit permitted review. Withdrawn/deleted evidence denies label reads/writes. Exact body-bound retries return the committed revision without creating another or restoring obsolete authority.

Label services require explicit per-minute write allowance per stable issuer/subject reviewer identity (not per login), maximum declared duration and review-validity bounds (at most 60 seconds); unavailable bounds deny setup. Review pages are bounded to 20 heads with an opaque canonical cursor and may return an empty filtered page. Source preview is not a media-download grant or independent physical certification. Expired previews, source changes, interrupted review or edited tuple fields clear the browser's approval context; an uncertain write can only retry its frozen original request/key or be reconciled by reading the exact label. Durable cleanup and evaluation/replay remain separate work.

### Configured application composition

The programmatic runtime factory accepts explicit typed identity/OAuth/Cognito, application origin, deployment/environment, declared artifact/control hashes, regional object/table identities and all worker/intake/purpose/calibration/label bounds. It receives three application-owned SDK clients (DynamoDB, S3, STS) rather than discovering credentials, profiles or endpoints. Construction makes no network/database calls, spawns no workers, bootstraps no owner and never enables inspections. All services share one access/repository/runtime authority and one bounded worker registry (at most two children). There is no request-controlled configuration or test identity endpoint.

At configured lifespan startup, prove OpenCV 5, check the caller's STS account and assumed-role identity against the explicit target, and describe both distinct DynamoDB tables to verify ACTIVE status, expected account/region/table ARN and string pk/sk primary schema before runtime startup can mutate state. SDK endpoints must be fixed regional HTTPS with at most two-second connection/ three-second read bounds and one total attempt. An explicit loopback DynamoDB endpoint is permitted only in `test`, is marked local synthetic scope and cannot establish AWS identity/storage assurance. There is no loopback/custom-provider bypass for beta or synthetic-demo SDK targets. S3 operations independently retain expected-owner, scoped key/version, checksum and KMS guards.

Each app has a fresh server-generated runtime instance ID and can start only once. Startup starts the runtime disabled, preserving existing membership but creating none; startup failure serves no configured app and cleans up its owned services/workers/clients. Clean shutdown also closes these resources, including when cleanup of one client fails. Do not close unrelated process resources. The default unconfigured entrypoint remains unchanged and unavailable for protected workflows.

The capture operation purpose is explicit and may be absent during initial authorized metadata setup. In that case reference/calibration/access/label composition is available but publication stays unconfigured and inspection readiness remains denied. After an owner creates the operational purpose through the authorized setup, configure its UUID and restart; the new epoch still needs fresh reviewed readiness. Neither a configured purpose UUID nor construction of clients proves purpose, bucket, rights, provider or beta readiness. First-owner bootstrap, actual target authority/configuration, provider revocation/recovery, packaging/host invocation and the private readiness writers remain separately required work. Declared artifact/control hashes are operator-supplied bindings, not independently verified build attestations.

### Host command and installed frontend

The `proofpack` console command (also `python -m proofpack.runtime.host`) accepts `check-config` or `serve`, each with explicit `--config` and `--secrets-file` paths. Ordinary configuration is closed-schema UTF-8 JSON of at most 64 KiB, with schema version one, explicit deployment/application/auth/OAuth/AWS/worker/service bounds; unknown/duplicate fields, invalid types, nonfinite values, missing bounds and test/endpoint/profile overrides are rejected. The separate at-most-4-KiB secrets JSON contains only base64 capability and OAuth keys of 32-128 decoded bytes. Keys are never command-line values, generated defaults, configuration output or logged validation errors. Actual private paths, permissions, notice, owner bootstrap and approved target values remain operator decisions.

`check-config` validates complete constructor/service bounds and emits only validation scope and the ordinary configuration digest. It imports no SDK credential session, makes no provider request and starts no server/worker. `serve` uses one explicit-region SDK session, requires the planned EC2 `iam-role` credential provider and creates fresh bounded regional DynamoDB/S3/STS clients from that session. Static/environment/profile credentials are not accepted by this host entrypoint. Credentials retain SDK instance-role refresh; no frozen temporary secret is written or printed. No local/test target bypass is accepted by the host JSON schema. The programmatic factory retains its separately guarded isolated-test interface.

Serving is one Uvicorn worker, no reload, no access-query logging and no trusted forwarded headers; bind defaults to loopback and a wider bind requires an explicit IP option plus the approved network boundary. Host invocation is an operational action requiring the actual target/start authority; providing code or passing `check-config` grants none. Factory target checks and disabled startup remain mandatory, and initialization failure closes newly created clients.

The Python wheel includes the exact prebuilt Vite index/assets under package `web/`; building without a complete bounded regular-file frontend fails. Frontend build precedes wheel build. The application prefers packaged assets when installed and retains the checkout dist fallback for local development. A separate isolated wheel-install smoke check must serve index/assets from outside the checkout and retain unavailable readiness/protected workflows. This proves packaging/local serving only, not target deployment, real TLS/provider access or release readiness.

### Retained asset review and authenticated bytes

`GET /inspections/{id}/evidence` discovers the latest retained asset under the same metadata scope and fences the selected inspection/observation pointer. `GET /assets/{id}` provides bounded permitted metadata and a derived `can_download`; `GET /assets/{id}/download` returns application-proxied JPEG/PNG only, never an S3 URL/key/version or raw input. Frame metadata is available to owner/reviewer and to an operator for that operator's own inspection. Frame bytes require explicit reviewer role or the originating user's current operator role; owner alone has no general frame-media grant. Reference metadata requires owner/reviewer. Reference bytes require reviewer, or current owner with recent MFA who originally uploaded that reference; operators do not gain reference access by knowing an ID. Every request rechecks enabled current session/grant and exact deployment scope.

All reads require committed/readable assets, current versioned operational-purpose attestations/notice and unexpired retention. Historical inspection closure, old machine expiry or a changed active recipe alone does not forbid permitted history review. Source observations must be completed and match their asset, input/crop/context hashes and original immutable inspection bindings. Reference receipts must use the reference namespace; frame receipts use the frame namespace. Missing/withdrawn/deleted/corrupt/foreign dependencies deny new delivery. Metadata is not storage/durability proof; bytes require the existing bounded version/checksum/KMS/owner-verified object read and a final authorization/dependency snapshot after that blocking read.

The service requires explicit `evidence_reads_per_minute` (per stable issuer/subject, at most 120), `evidence_max_concurrent` (at most two per instance) and `evidence_read_window_ms` (at most 5000). The budget starts before authorization/storage and cannot be renewed by retries. After current session resolution, every authenticated attempt is charged before resource lookup, including missing/denied IDs; lookup retries cannot bypass the allowance. A durable source-bound admission audit precedes object access; a final ready audit binds current actor/source/data/runtime rows before returning bytes. Ready does not prove the person received or understood them. Body size is at most 2 MiB; concurrency remains held through the actual blocking read and released in finally, including client cancellation. This is a direct retained-asset request, not the future durable export/ticket workflow.

Metadata/response binding digest covers current asset/purpose/data and viewer session/access context. Binary headers supply exact asset ID/version, content SHA-256, binding digest and conservative remaining display validity; the browser compares them with reviewed metadata, validates bounded bytes/hash, and only then creates a local blob preview. No range request, signed URL, permanent capability, image logging or current-pass assertion is provided. Responses are no-store, same-origin-only and inline with a server UUID filename. The client clears/revokes its preview on ID change, expiry, hidden/offline state, cancellation or navigation, and explicitly labels it historical. Already delivered bytes/screenshots/copies cannot be recalled; per-request checks cannot revoke completed delivery or prove physical truth.

The new read bounds are mandatory in programmatic/host configuration; no existing private configuration is silently upgraded or given new media privilege. Actual target/privacy/retention/operational/human qualification remains required. Asset review does not grant optional improvement/publicity use, constitute an export job or authorize shipment.

## Standalone human disposition contract

Contract completion before implementation, October 14, 2026: POST `/inspections/{id}/dispositions` was listed without a request/readback/retry contract and was absent in code. An owning enabled operator may append a separate human decision to the same nonclosed current inspection using If-Match, Idempotency-Key, CSRF/Origin and `{expected_epoch, disposition, reason, human_check_confirmed}`. Disposition is one of rework/reject/cancelled/human_accepted; reason is 1â€“120 nonblank characters. Human acceptance requires the explicit boolean confirmation of a complete manual check; this remains a user declaration, not independent truth or shipment authority. Null/unknown decisions and extra fields are rejected.

Require the station owner session and originating inspection session/user, exact station epoch and inspection revision, and no pending admitted capture. Append an immutable human-disposition row with server ID, prior disposition ID, actor/user/session, recorded time, inspection/station/epoch, source inspection revision/state and latest observation ID, decision/reason/confirmation. Atomically update the inspection's latest human-disposition pointer/value, audit, existing station control allowance and idempotency result under current actor/station/work conditions. Keep machine state, correction budget, permits, evidence and verifier deadline unchanged. This operation cannot clear REVIEW_REQUIRED or restart capture. Manual decision recording and readback do not require a working camera or enabled runtime, so a stopped system can still record human handling; closed/foreign/taken-over/pending work cannot be amended here. Corrections append a new record rather than edit old history.

GET `/inspections/{id}/dispositions/latest` returns the latest immutable human record or an explicit no-record result. Reviewers and owners may read business human-decision metadata; an operator may read their own originating inspection across a fresh authorized session. Recheck current membership/session and exact inspection/pointer/record snapshots before returning; expose no image, private storage key, provider token or machine approval. Historical readback never authorizes capture. Owner-only writes remain forbidden without an operator grant and current station ownership.

A successful POST returns its immutable decision and committed inspection revision; replay returns the same historical result without changing a later decision, closed inspection or station. A reused key with different intent conflicts. The UI stops capture/clears local approval before recording, freezes body/version/key on an uncertain response, and offers an explicit unchanged retry. Keep conflicting/denied work unresolved. A reload/sign-out does not undo a committed decision; inspect latest readback before another write, and consult the supervisor if an intervening correction makes the lost response uncertain. No claim of durable client retry across page destruction is made.

Latest readback exposes the latest separately appended decision and `current_disposition` independently: a later close/takeover may record a different inline human decision without rewriting an earlier immutable standalone record. A no-record response means no separately appended record, not that no human handling ever occurred. Missing/corrupt prior standalone history blocks a correction instead of silently replacing its lineage. These records remain subject to the unfinished inspection metadata retention/deletion lifecycle; this write/read component does not close privacy acceptance.

Combined grants are a union of explicit permitted scopes: a user holding operator and owner may use their own operator-scoped history read even after owner MFA ages. Owner-only access to other business human-decision metadata still requires current owner authority/recent MFA. The extra owner title must not revoke an otherwise valid explicit operator read.

## Recipe qualification execution contract

Contract completion before implementation, October 14, 2026: the qualification writer, test-case schema, job bounds and report dependency fences were missing. POST `/recipes/{id}/qualify` is an owner/MFA mutation with exact draft If-Match, CSRF/Origin and durable Idempotency-Key. It accepts 3â€“8 distinct stored reference cases: `{asset_id, kind, expected_findings, expected_counts}`. Every run includes complete, incorrect and challenge kinds. Findings/counts follow current recipe constraint order; complete requires all SATISFIED and target counts, incorrect requires a VIOLATED finding, challenge requires UNOBSERVABLE. Expected counts are declared comparison inputs, not independent truth. No client pass/approval/independence flag is accepted. Bound the union of recipe and case references to 16, and require distinct case source hashes, matching origin, current operational purposes and exact stored receipts.

New mandatory programmatic/host bounds are `recipe_qualification_runs_per_minute` (1â€“100 per stable actor) and `recipe_qualification_max_ms` (1000â€“60000). Reserve a nonrenewable durable processing job and advance the draft head before reading bytes. Replays return that job, never charge/run again.
