- **UNRESOLVED**: unknown/unsupported family or version, legacy missing lineage, malformed/conflicting relationship, incomplete history, retained erasure marker without journal reconciliation, or an unproved collateral/ownership decision. Keep its original reference and safe reason; dependent completion remains held.

A record may participate in several typed relationships, but has one conservative accounting disposition; conflicting evidence cannot be resolved by whichever adapter runs last. SHARED dominates a sole-owner candidate; unresolved provenance holds the affected decision. Completeness requires coverage of the whole inventory, including unrecognized families. Do not turn an unsupported family into a silent exclusion so a plan can be produced. Retained markers require separate original-scope/journal reconciliation; they do not supply erased source payloads or prove historical cleanup complete.

### Derive family inputs from supported relationships

The server selects inputs from the complete corpus using validated schemas and exact typed references. Selection itself is tested independently of the family parser; a passing parser over an incomplete selection is a failure of this workflow. Reuse existing strict validators and preserve their record/payload limits, historical versions and provenance checks. The following dependency order describes data requirements, not permission propagation:

| Family | Required server-derived inputs and boundary |
|---|---|
| Capture | Select the exact closed inspection; permits/observations identify it, while actions, crop/original assets and object intents join through validated observations and original derivation. Include admitted/completed/failed capture and relevant access history. Preserve original storage target/version receipts, including failed-publication intents. Open/pending work holds rather than being force-closed by the classifier. |
| Human decisions | Use the complete capture inputs plus exact publication/disposition/history relationships and versions. Recorded human disposition remains a separate business decision and is not inferred from an automated pass. |
| Support | Include all main handoffs, successor/acceptance history and separate recipient inbox copies using validated identities and request hashes. Include expired records under an explicit reference time; expiry does not erase retained content. A recipient relationship is not an app-role grant. |
| Labels and purposes | Derive label roots from immutable revision source_context and validate head/revision history, original recipe and applicable purpose records. A label head or UUID substring alone cannot identify all affected labels. Optional reuse permission does not grant cleanup authority. |
| Improvement permission | Select complete preview/grant/permission/revocation history with exact supported source/asset bindings; preserve absent, denied and revoked states. Current permission cannot replace historical provenance or erase an unresolved record. |
| Datasets and shared groups | Inspect full validated request selections, example content, seals, catalog/job/history and group-version relationships, using the original label/permission inputs. A mixed-inspection dataset and shared split-denial group remain shared; their other source inspections are not silently added to the request. Include cancelled/failed/retired jobs, direct transitions and historical copied content. |
| Replay and audit outputs | Account for every retained idempotency result and immutable event. Decode exact deployment/operation/write/check envelopes, then validate the business family's result and output copies. Shared transaction identity identifies peers, not sole ownership. Check-only references do not establish copied content; writes do not by themselves prove that all output fields belong to the target. Unsupported operations or legacy missing bindings hold for explicit reconciliation. |
| Deployment/context and other supported records | Validate their actual family schema and reason before classifying context or unrelated. Never expand from a common runtime, station, user, recipe or active policy into blanket tenant ownership. Unknown schemas remain unresolved. |

Closure is a bounded fixed point over explicit schema-defined content/copy relationships, including reverse relationships found across the entire original corpus. It must be independent of input ordering and of which supported family first discovers a record. Detect conflicting provenance, cycles without validated resolution, missing required peers and wrong historical versions. A cycle is not permission to erase all connected nodes. Record terminal rejection/hold reasons without leaking the private payload.

### Shared records and honest completion

Classifying a record does not choose a destructive operation. For each affected or shared artifact the later plan must name a supported conditional action and disclose its actual scope. Whole-record erasure of a shared dataset/job/event cannot be substituted for target-only cleanup without separately admitted collateral scope and exact owner confirmation. A supported redaction/replacement must preserve unrelated content and provenance and invalidate dependent artifacts as required; an immutable artifact may instead need a separately authorized whole-artifact retirement/removal plan. Until that policy and its tests exist, preserve the SHARED obligation and hold completion. Do not claim a complete inspection/subject request while shared copied payload remains.

Context may remain only under an explicit validated retention rationale; it cannot retain in-scope copied payload under a new label. Classifier output cannot bypass object-version cleanup: preserve required target receipts until actual cleanup is verified. Independent journal persistence/replay, external exports/backups, restore compatibility and requester communication remain separately required obligations. A successful classification is not a removal receipt or legal completion claim.

### Result and revalidation

Return a deterministic private classification containing the original proof/inventory/scope binding, family-policy version, sorted per-record dispositions/reasons, typed edges, exact candidate/context/shared/unresolved references and explicit consumed bounds. Bind its payload-free digest to every original entry and decision. Do not expose raw family payloads as an owner preview or log. A public preview later derives only authorized counts, consequences, limitations and opaque references from a freshly validated result; it adds its own immutable owner/session/MFA/source/configuration versions, expiry and idempotent confirmation contract.

An accepted classification requires complete accounting with supported policies and no unresolved prerequisite for its declared scope. A held diagnostic may report bounded safe reasons but cannot be consumed as an executable partial plan. Every later confirmation, batch and recovery still revalidates actual authorization, drain/source versions and journal state. A digest alone does not make mutable detached objects trustworthy. No new public endpoint, worker execution, store mutation or restoration authority is introduced by this classification contract.

### Accepted private classification policy — implementation contract

Gap reported before implementation: conservative-family-accounting-v1 deliberately emits hints with unresolved obligations, and the existing contract does not name an accepted classification policy or its retention reasons. Add the private policy **whole-scope-family-ownership-v1** and a separate held-reader classification operation. This is a required component of complete W20/W21/W22, not a replacement for the remaining family policies, owner preview/confirmation, cleanup/journal/restore or requester workflow. No public endpoint, mutation, bearer capability or inspection enablement is added.

Compute classification from the same freshly server-derived family selection, complete inventory, exact scope and original drain proof. Recompute the existing accounting inside that original resource/deadline lifetime; do not accept a caller-created hint/count object as ownership. The orchestration must retain the original proof and perform the existing complete source reconciliation, configuration/authority check and final binding after classification. Existing read, accounting and collateral methods keep their contracts. The new operation needs explicit AccountingLimits and ClassificationLimits; it cannot combine a proposal and accepted classification into one ambiguous operation.

For this policy, supported content policies are exactly CAPTURE_CONTENT, HUMAN_CONTENT, SUPPORT_CONTENT, LABEL_CONTENT, OPTIONAL_PERMISSION_CONTENT, DATASET_ARTIFACT_CONTENT and REPLAY_RESULT_CONTENT, after their existing complete schema/history derivations succeed. AFFECTED and UNRELATED entries require nonempty validated inspection membership and those supported content policies, exact reasons consistent with the original requested scope, and matching typed relationships to original inspection roots. Never infer membership from a key substring, actor, station or shared transaction. AFFECTED entries must have every copied inspection inside the admitted scope; UNRELATED entries must have none inside it. A common policy name without the validated family derivation is insufficient.

CONTEXT requires explicit retained reasons: EXACT_DRAIN_CONTEXT maps to **retained_original_maintenance_fence**, and VALIDATED_LABEL_CONTEXT maps to **retained_original_recipe_or_purpose_for_interpretation**. Only the exact references established by the original drain proof or validated label derivation qualify. Context with copied content follows the content disposition; a rationale cannot hide target payload. Other context families need separately implemented, tested schema/retention policies before acceptance. This policy does not authorize blanket retention of account, user, station, calibration or audit records, or resolve a legal retention question.

Any SHARED or UNRESOLVED entry, retained marker without journal reconciliation, unsupported policy/reason, missing/extra identity, inconsistent membership/edge or digest/binding mismatch holds the entire accepted result. Preserve existing diagnostics and original obligations; never return an accepted partial classification. Shared content can become wholly in scope only after a separate ordinary owner request admits the explicitly reviewed larger scope and discovery/classification runs again. A historical proposal cannot enlarge the current request. Unresolved group/reference/replay policies remain required work, even when the proposed scope has no additions; this first policy must not relabel them to manufacture acceptance.

ClassificationLimits contains required positive exact-integer max_records, max_edges, max_work and max_summary_bytes, each bounded by the existing private signed-64-bit ceiling; no defaults or public overrides. Its work budget includes recomputed accounting and classification passes together. Effective accounting record/edge/work caps may only tighten the supplied AccountingLimits to those independent classification caps; never renew an allowance. Retain the same original absolute monotonic deadline and trusted checkpoints throughout accounting, every classification/edge/digest pass and final delivery. Check independent allocation/work bounds before materializing collections. Overflow, clock reversal, cancellation or exhausted capacity returns no accepted artifact or renewed allowance.

Return a frozen private OwnershipClassification with original inventory/scope/accounting bindings, this policy version, sorted exact per-record dispositions/reasons/memberships/policies/retention reasons, typed relationships and consumed work/summary bounds. Bind its deterministic payload-free digest to every original entry and decision. References, memberships and edges remain absent from repr and ordinary/public logs. historical/read_only are true; can_confirm_cleanup/erasure_complete/request_fulfilled remain false. The artifact is an input to later freshly authorized plan review, never an executable cleanup plan. Later confirmation/batches/recovery must independently revalidate sources, scope, authority and journal state; a forged detached Python value grants nothing.

Acceptance under T39/T40/T55: a complete supported modeled corpus may produce a deterministic frozen result; unknown/shared/marker/unsupported-context cases must hold with no accepted partial result. Exercise every independent bound, exact scope/content priority, reason/policy and reference/digest/count/edge forgery, original deadline/checkpoint/configuration changes, final source phantom and resource retention/release. Add actual-store evidence distinguishing known supported data from a genuinely unresolved history. Synthetic positive cases do not prove complete real-business coverage. Configured owner/worker/public preview, installed/full-current and human acceptance remain separate requirements.

Implementation follow-through: the separate 696-input ownership-classification candidate implements OwnershipClassification/ClassificationLimits and HeldCorpusReader.read_classification. It recomputes accounting under tightened record/edge/work caps, charges both phases to the classification work allowance, validates explicit context origins and complete content relationships/digests, then performs original final source/proof/resource checks. The reader separately validates returned policy/bindings/counts/bounds/relationships and non-authorizing flags. Selected modeled and installed protocols pass; actual local-store unknown/bound/deadline cases remain correctly held. See BUILD-STATUS and the source-bound audit for exact scope. The original duplicate fault was a no-op; the corrected actual-corruption case passes with its unchanged denial requirement, while the original failure and separate-review obligation remain retained. No actual-store accepted-positive, configured owner/worker/UI, full-current or complete requester/cleanup acceptance follows. Canonical source is unchanged.

### Operational purpose history — private provenance prerequisite

Gap reported before implementation: purpose rows and their creation/withdrawal access/replay outputs remain unsupported by the complete ownership classifier. A generic access audit or matching transaction checks do not establish ownership. Add purpose-history-v1 as a pure bounded validator over one current operational purpose Row and the explicitly supplied complete event/replay history for that purpose, using the exact deployment. It returns frozen payload-free records/dependencies and historical state only. No routes, default selection/accounting changes, rights certification, retention classification, deletion or enablement authority are added by this prerequisite.

Support the exact DataPurposes.create/withdraw schemas, including the separate original-capture attestation variant and an already-withdrawn acknowledgment. Validate immutable purpose identity/fields, exact boolean and bounded integer values, permitted operation-only flags, original notice version/hash/text when present, creation/withdrawal chronology and current revision (one before withdrawal, two after). Expiry can be historical; validation never renews use. Require exactly one intact creation and, when withdrawn, one actual withdrawal. Repeated already-withdrawn acknowledgments must leave the purpose unchanged. Reconstruct the immutable creation payload from the supported current head and compare its original write digest/revision; require the actual current withdrawn payload on the single changing withdrawal.

Each replay must reconcile with exactly one matching access event using the existing supplied-output parser and linked-writer envelope. Verify the purpose ID/result, closed operation-specific details, exact create/withdraw request digest, canonical actor/session references, allowed business writes and required checked authority/data/purpose heads. Historical dependencies are provenance only, not authenticated identities or current permissions/payloads. No unrelated peer, extra copied field, unmatched/duplicate/ambiguous output or unsupported variant may produce a partial result. The writer excludes output self-links: multiple event/replay pairs with the same envelope may be ambiguous, including legitimate repeated no-op operations. Hold that ambiguity explicitly rather than inventing a pairing; writer correlation and family ownership are later obligations.

PurposeHistoryLimits requires positive exact signed-64-bit-bounded max_records, max_payload_bytes, max_dependencies, max_work and max_summary_bytes, without defaults. Count all supplied positions/bytes, validate exact record identities/revisions/digests and bound historical dependency unions and output summaries. Preserve the caller's original absolute deadline, nondecreasing trusted clock and authority checkpoints. Test actual purpose/access/linked writers on modeled and owned-local storage, exact and one-below limits, supported original-retention/Decimal variants, replay corruption, missing/ambiguous/foreign histories, immutable/redacted output and unchanged business data. Full raw/inventory/drain/scope binding, explicit retained/affected/shared classification, held-reader consumption and final source reconciliation remain required before any complete cleanup decision.

Selected implementation follow-through: the isolated 704-input purpose-history candidate implements this supplied-family parser and five independent bounds. BUILD-STATUS and its current audit record model/original/native/installed verification and exact limitations. It changes no reader/default/writer or classification policy. Full inventory/scope/drain consumption, explicit purpose ownership/retention/shared-copy rules and final held reconciliation remain the next dependency; the parser cannot prove missing historical sibling completeness or resolve indistinguishable writer outputs.

### Held operational purpose context — explicit private v3 contract

Gap reported before implementation: the supplied purpose-history parser has no complete held-corpus consumer or retention policy. For an inspection-metadata request only, retain an operational-purpose attestation and its exact validated create/withdraw/acknowledgment outputs as interpretation context when a validated capture/media or label family actually references that purpose. This preserves the recorded notice and withdrawal history; it does not establish rights, optional reuse, current permission, user identity authenticity or ownership of unrelated business data. The explicit policy is VALIDATED_OPERATIONAL_PURPOSE_CONTEXT with reason retained_original_operational_purpose_and_history_for_interpretation. Unsupported, malformed, missing or ambiguously paired selected histories hold; unreferenced purposes and unknown records remain residual, never silently retained by prefix. Content membership still takes precedence over any interpretation tag.

Derive purpose-context-v1 from the complete original raw rows/inventory, freshly validated selection and exact drain/scope. Validate current/historical purpose references against the selected capture/label provenance; historical dependency hashes alone do not own records. Discover all relevant purpose operation outputs from the full inventory using purpose write/check identities as well as result/detail IDs, and reconcile every selected family with purpose-history-v1. Bind source-use references, frozen family records/digests, original inventory/proof/scope and requested IDs in a bounded payload-free summary. Historical checked dependency heads are not newly retained or removed by this policy. Full inventory correspondence and final source reconciliation remain mandatory; forged caller context is not a capability.

Add only explicit private read_purpose_accounting/read_purpose_classification operations with required runtime-context and purpose-context limits. V1 methods, v2 runtime-only methods, collateral proposal defaults and all public routes remain unchanged. Opt-in outputs use conservative-family-accounting-v3 and whole-scope-family-ownership-v3. Only exact verified selected purpose replay outputs may discharge their unsupported-business-codec accounting obligation; every other replay/shared/unknown obligation remains. ### Versioned invitation provenance and supplied lifecycle — contract before implementation

For acceptance of a supported versioned invitation, also retain the exact notice_version and notice_sha256 already bound to the acceptance request. Keep public projections, role/capacity rules, original retries and legacy live behavior unchanged. Do not backfill old records or claim that partial historical data satisfies the new codec. Unsupported/unversioned histories remain unresolved for classification.

Implement invitation-history-v1 over an explicitly supplied bounded tuple of versioned invitation roots and complete invite/accept-invite/revoke-invite/decline-invite event/replay pairs. Include every invitation referenced by an invite transaction's pending checks or implicit-expiry writes; validate complete per-root revision/hash chains, exactly one creation, supported pending/accepted/expired/revoked/declined variants, repeated revocations and expiry followed by revocation. Reconstruct exact versioned root payloads and original request digests, validate closed results and matching peer bindings, exact head/session/cookie/member/identity dependency shapes and prior/write revisions. Bind current selected roots; historical dependency hashes alone do not prove role, membership, identity authenticity or the full contents of another family. Preserve those separate obligations.

Require five independent explicit finite record/payload/dependency/work/summary limits, the original absolute deadline, nondecreasing trusted clock and authority checkpoints through final source reconciliation. All supplied roots and peers count against limits; duplicate, missing, foreign, ambiguous, extra, unsupported, inconsistent or stale input holds without partial output. Frozen payload-free private summaries are historical/read-only and grant neither current access, retention classification nor cleanup. Full inventory correspondence, global authentication-history ownership/retention policy, held-reader consumption, exact owner preview/confirmation, cleanup/journal/restore and requester communication remain required. Verify real unmodified public access operations on modeled and, when resource admission permits, owned-local storage; preserve legacy behavior, exact retries, all supported transitions, private/public separation, corruption, independent bounds and final callback expiry/mutation. No actual provider or human acceptance follows.

Selected implementation follow-through: newly created invitation roots now preserve versioned creation/TTL provenance; supported versioned acceptance also retains the request-bound notice version/hash. Public projections, original retries and legacy live acceptance remain compatible. invitation-history-v1 validates supplied multi-root pending/accepted/declined/expiry/revocation chains without retaining payloads in its summary or granting authority. ### Inspection-origin access context — contract before implementation

Current-store prerequisite, reported October 17 before repair: the live AccessService access-head parser compared schema_version to 1 by numeric equality and admitted undeclared fields. This is a malformed-store integrity gap, not evidence that an ordinary client can write those fields. Before historical auth classification is extended, require the current state/access-head Row, a positive exact-integer repository revision and the exact seven-field bootstrap/current-writer payload: schema_version, enabled_user_ids, owner_ids, pending_invitation_ids, bootstrap_scope, bootstrap_receipt and bootstrap_user_id. Schema marker 1 accepts an exact Python integer or finite Decimal equal to 1 from DynamoDB storage; booleans, binary floats, strings and nonfinite values are unsupported. The three ID collections are bounded ordinary lists of unique canonical UUIDs, current owners are nonempty and a subset of enabled users, and enabled-plus-pending accounts obey the existing configured five-account ceiling. Original bootstrap UUID and lowercase SHA-256 bindings remain syntactically intact; the bootstrap user need not remain enabled after a legitimate transfer. Do not repair, normalize or authorize from malformed stored state. Login/session resolution must fail with StoreUnavailable before publishing a session or actor and without writes. Normal writer-produced integer/Decimal heads must continue working. This validates current-store admission only: access-head and other auth histories still require separate complete family/schema/retention policies and remain unresolved in private ownership classification.

Gap reported October 17, PP09/W20/W21/W22, P01/P05/P06/P07: v3 leaves stored user/session/identity/cookie records unresolved even when a complete validated capture identifies its original user and session. Do not treat a key prefix, membership, display name or absence of inspection_id as evidence that a record can be retained or erased. Add an explicit private v4 operation, preserving v1–v3 and public defaults.

Derive origins only from exact original inspection roots already selected by the validated capture family. Retain that root's originating user and session, the exact identity index derived from the member issuer/subject, and every exact supported cookie that references that session. Require complete raw/inventory reconciliation and exact live revisions/digests. The session must identify that user, issuer, subject and email hash; its recorded access version must equal the inspection's originating access version and not exceed the current user's version. Session epoch must not exceed the current member epoch. Creation/authentication must precede the inspection; recorded current MFA may be later but not later than the admitted wall clock. A revoked, disabled, inactive or expired record is historical context, never renewed authority. Do not require it to be currently usable. Missing or conflicting required member/session/index, malformed selected cookie, unknown fields or unsupported schema holds the operation. Unmatched identities, other sessions/cookies, access-head, invitations, transfers, OAuth data and access/replay histories remain obligations under their separate policies.

Use exact supported current writer schemas from auth/service.py, including bounded identity/display values, UUID and SHA bindings, exact booleans, unique supported role values, positive integer versions, coherent times and nonrestricted original sessions. Stored numeric payloads may use the existing strict DynamoDB integer decoder: accept finite integral Decimal losslessly within signed-64-bit bounds, rejecting booleans, floats, fractions and nonfinite values. This does not relax exact-integer limits, clocks, result metadata or InventoryEntry revisions. Require an identity index containing only the matching user_id; cookie contains only matching session_id/user_id. Select no unrelated member through a common email, role, station, runtime or transaction. All inventory records continue to receive one accounting disposition. Content/unknown provenance dominates context; this policy cannot hide inspection content under a retention reason.

The supported member schemas are the exact bootstrap member and the exact invitation-acceptance member. The latter adds all five notice_version, notice_sha256, notice_acknowledged_at_ms, improvement_reuse and publicity_reuse fields together. Validate the bounded recorded notice version/hash, acknowledgment from member creation through original inspection time, and the writer's two explicit false reuse defaults. Partial sets, unknown additions and unsupported reuse states hold. Retaining this recorded acknowledgment neither verifies source rights nor grants optional reuse or publicity.

AccessContextLimits requires finite positive exact-integer max_records, max_payload_bytes, max_work and max_summary_bytes with no defaults. Hold on allocation/work/deadline exhaustion, clock reversal or cancellation; preserve the original absolute deadline, trusted checkpoints, source/configuration/drain revalidation and local resource ownership through final delivery. Charge origin-context work before accounting/classification, and pass only remaining classification work to each subsequent phase. Never renew an allowance. V4 requires explicit runtime and access-context limits; the existing purpose phase is included only when its explicit limits are supplied. Missing required limits cannot silently select an older operation. Select only requested inspection roots, and require the original session to have been unexpired when that inspection began; later expiry/revocation does not invalidate historical interpretation or restore authority.

Return a frozen private, payload-free, original-inventory/proof/scope-bound AccessContext with sorted exact origins and retained references, source/consumed-bound counts and a deterministic digest. Its explicit policy VALIDATED_INSPECTION_ORIGIN_ACCESS_CONTEXT has retention reason retained_original_user_session_identity_for_inspection_interpretation. References and identities stay absent from repr/public summaries. No authentication, legal retention decision, ownership, scope expansion, confirmation, erasure or requester fulfillment follows. Account/classify v4 must freshly derive and validate it; a detached artifact is not a capability. Unknown/shared/marker records still hold accepted classification.

Acceptance under T39/T40/T55 includes real access writers on modeled storage, unchanged v3 results, exact selected/unselected records, revoked/expired history, conflicting user/session/index/cookie and copied-content rejection, every independent bound, typed-return/digest forgery, combined work, final-source/configuration/drain changes and resource cleanup. Add actual local-store checks before accepting that boundary. Full owner/worker/UI preview, confirmation, cleanup/journal/restore and requester communication remain required.

[Build status](BUILD-STATUS.md#mc-17-inspection-origin-access-context--private-v4-requester-workflow-open) owns current modeled/native/installed scope, preserved failures and fixture corrections. Supported original records move to CONTEXT; other unknowns continue to hold complete classification. This does not implement public requester confirmation, cleanup, journal/restore or fulfillment, integrate the canonical source, or change beta NO_GO.

### Runtime maintenance context — opt-in private policy contract

The writer reads the clock separately for the runtime, event and created data head: their times need not be equal. Validate nondecreasing startup times rather than reconstructing missing timestamps from event time. If original written snapshots cannot be established, retain an unresolved obligation. Allowed transaction peers are only RUNTIME, that marker, ALLOCATION and DATA; allocation/data must each be an exact supported creation or historical check, never an arbitrary additional write. Historical check digests are provenance, not proof that the current head has its old value or permission to own it.

Opt-in integration detail: for the current proof's instance only, the original startup runtime candidate may use its preserved started_at_ms together with marker/event fields and the original write revision; acceptance still requires the exact original write digest. Never guess a historical start time. If a created allocation/data snapshot has since changed, leave the corresponding startup event/marker unresolved rather than substitute current bytes. Historical-instance capture origins without their original snapshots remain explicit obligations. The current disabled runtime schema supports the startup fields and the known disable extension (enable_receipt_sha256 and enable_owner_id, both null when disabled); unknown fields hold. Standalone pure context objects are diagnostic data, not capabilities: the held reader freshly derives, validates and binds them before final source reconciliation.

Selected implementation follow-through: the separate 701-input runtime-maintenance-context candidate implements read_runtime_accounting/read_runtime_classification with freshly derived RuntimeContext under the original hold, complete final reconciliation and v2-only policy identifiers. V1/collateral behavior and canonical source remain unchanged; remaining family policies, accepted actual-store positive, public cleanup/restore/requester journeys and beta acceptance remain open.

Select only startup histories matching the exact current proof runtime or a validated original capture's runtime instance/epoch/artifact and creation-time relationship. Reject inconsistent instance reuse, duplicate event/marker identity, missing required matching peers, malformed known schema or future/reversed times. Unmatched/unsupported history remains a residual obligation. Runtime context retention never absorbs capture content, arbitrary access events, identities, replay outputs or unrelated rows. Runtime markers/events receive VALIDATED_RUNTIME_PROVENANCE with retained_original_runtime_provenance.

Separately validate the current ALLOCATION and DATA heads from the same original inventory. ALLOCATION must be the exact slots-only schema with an empty map; occupied or unknown shapes hold this opt-in read and cannot authorize station release. DATA must be an exact supported epoch-only legacy counter or epoch/changed_at_ms counter, with positive exact integer epoch and any recorded time no later than the admitted wall clock. Missing time stays unrecorded; do not invent one. These receive VALIDATED_EMPTY_ALLOCATION_FENCE / retained_original_empty_allocation_fence and VALIDATED_DATA_EPOCH_FENCE / retained_original_data_epoch_fence. No copied inspection payload or arbitrary fields are permitted under these reasons. Current RUNTIME remains subject to its existing exact drain-context checks; this policy grants no new privilege over it.

RuntimeContextLimits requires finite positive exact-integer max_records, max_payload_bytes, max_work and max_summary_bytes, bounded by the existing private signed-64-bit ceiling, with no defaults or public overrides. Validate identity/revision/digest and complete original raw/inventory correspondence before accepting a frozen payload-free context result. Its summary binds original deployment/inventory/proof/scope, capture-origin references and exact consumed context references/policies; targets stay absent from repr/public logs. Keep the original absolute deadline, nondecreasing trusted clock, authority checkpoints and local resource ownership through final source reconciliation. Context derivation has its own explicit bounds; accepted classification's combined work allowance includes context, accounting and classification work, with only remaining work delegated to later phases. No retry renews a phase or operation allowance.

Acceptance under T39/T40/T55 requires genuine linked-writer positive and corruption cases, exact family/context membership, current empty-fence validation, v1 unchanged behavior and explicit v2 selection, all independent bounds/deadlines, no writes, final-source races and resource retention. Actual local-store history must show only validated context obligations moving out of unresolved; all other records and the request scope remain unchanged, and incomplete classification stays held. A prefix count reduction is not cleanup, legal retention approval, fulfilled request or beta acceptance. ### Actual held-corpus selection boundary — before implementation

The pure corpus selectors still accept supplied row tuples; they are not the W20 server read boundary. Add a private HeldCorpusReader using the exact PersistentAdmission, shared ResourceOwner, explicit HeldInventoryLimits, HeldCorpusLimits and trusted monotonic clock. HeldCorpusLimits contains the typed ReplayCorpusLimits, mandatory ReferenceCorpusLimits (version-3 selection), plus max_total_snapshot_batches, max_total_payload_bytes and max_binding_bytes, all finite exact positive integers with no operational defaults. Its totals must cover the two configured worst-case reconciliation phases before any SDK work; enforce and report actual combined consumption as well. Constructor and invalid-scope/deadline handling perform no I/O.

An exact inspection-scope descriptor binds schema/version, selector-policy version, logical deployment and ordered unique inspection UUIDs within the corpus inspection bound. The read operation recomputes this scope hash from its IDs, never accepts a raw row/family list or arbitrary key scope, and requires the existing HELD request to match it. This descriptor/hash is not an authenticated owner approval. Current owner/MFA/request admission and eventual control-plane authorization remain separate unfinished requirements.

Acquire one outer local resource ticket before the first admission/store read and retain it through seed inventory, every selection phase, final reconciliation, binding and final checks. Obtain the original drain proof first; a fresh internal HeldInventoryReader must use the same checked monotonic clock and must return exactly that proof. Every checkpoint revalidates exact configuration identities, target/client, proof and original absolute deadline. Freeze trusted wall time from the admission service for historical selection. After deriving the complete provisional selection, reconcile the original inventory again with real snapshots and a full follow-up pass; known/unknown record insertions, updates, removals or markers observed during selection hold the result. Do not obtain a replacement proof or renew the deadline. This is not a global atomic snapshot or protection against privileged/unregistered writers.

Return only an immutable private scope/proof/inventory/selection artifact, actual cumulative snapshot/payload counts, bounded projection byte count and deterministic input/metadata binding digest. Discard raw seed/final payload snapshots before returning. Stream a size-bounded typed metadata projection; omit fields explicitly private in repr and bind their original values through the inventory payload digests. Never serialize raw Row/dict payloads, decision text or object destinations into a public receipt/log. The binding is not tamper-proof authority: later preview/confirmation/commit must revalidate source versions, permissions and hidden target coordinates even if a Python artifact is forged or forcibly mutated. Unknown/residual obligations are not a completed classification or requester receipt.

Reject concurrent use of a reader incarnation without starting another store read. Cancellation/termination or unconfirmed inner completion leaves sticky pending state and retained local ownership; another read cannot clear it through time, retries or a new inner reader. Known completed failures may release ownership; failed release remains pending. Shutdown cannot close shared clients during selection/final reads. Native actual-store, adverse source/control/clock/target/lifetime cases and installed-origin evidence are required before integration; no public route, writes, hold/enrollment/resume, deletion or owner grant is introduced here.

### Exact kind-less group-declassification peer — before replay parser repair

Source `review/manifests.py` and the supported group-history validator store a `dataset-declassification#<uuid>` event with exactly group_id, from_split, to_split, at_ms, reason and resource_bindings, without a kind field. The generic replay parser previously required kind on every peer, rejecting this legitimate complete transaction. Add only this explicit peer variant: exact supported event prefix/UUID and fields, operation dataset-declassify:<matching 64-hex group ID>, known split values with DEVELOPMENT destination, bounded identifier reason, exact matching access details, typed timestamp no later than the access event and the same full decoded transaction envelope. Preserve all peers; never drop the detail or permit arbitrary kind-less rows. Full group-history and business-result validation still apply. Also freeze per-family scope/obligation iterables before assigning them to events, so a one-shot generator cannot lose queued unresolved dependencies after its first event.

### Replay corpus and business-result codecs — before implementation

The supplied replay parser proves exact shared transaction outputs; it does not validate most business result dictionaries or establish ownership. Add private ReplayCorpusLimits with explicit dataset (DatasetCorpusLimits), max_replays and max_transaction_events; exact positive finite integers, no operational defaults. Reuse the original corpus work/deadline and separate expanded-family bounds. Build a bounded reverse index over exact decoded event envelopes, compare the complete envelope after digest lookup, and include every indexed peer. A hash alone, a checked shared resource or a matching UUID cannot assign inspection scope. Malformed supported provenance/peer sets hold; missing legacy provenance and unsupported operations remain explicit residual obligations, never silent exclusions.

Derive peer scope only from independently validated family records, combining conflicting/shared evidence conservatively. First codecs cover observation admission; action acknowledgment; immutable human disposition including its exact copied decision and historical inspection write version; label create/revise IDs and logical revisions; optional-permission preview/confirm/withdraw IDs; dataset create/step/cancel/retire IDs; and group declassification IDs. Compare complete expected shapes with canonical typed values; reject extra/copied/private fields and wrong IDs/versions. The dataset-step attempt, when present, is an opaque historical UUID under the actual writer schema: validate its encoding, record the limited validation class and never claim its value is a renewed/current lease, a proven historical token preimage or authority. No lookup or ownership expansion follows an opaque token. The exact job ID remains bound to the validated transaction/family.

Source dependencies that remain unresolved (including reference assets, queued unknown sources and shared group retention/collateral scope) keep their wrappers residual even after result-shape validation. Unsupported support/lifecycle/infrastructure/other-job peer policies and business result schemas require further explicit adapters; do not accept them through a generic id/check/write fallback. Return payload-free references, codec/version/validation class, peer relationships, separately related/requested inspection subsets and safe fixed obligation reasons. Do not copy saved result bodies, token values, decision text, actor/session details or object destinations into repr, logs or a public preview. A validated result is not a completed classifier or cleanup grant.

### Dataset/shared-group selection bounds — before implementation

Dataset manifests use the shared maintenance-job namespace; only exact supported dataset kind/schema enters that family. Other job kinds remain unresolved, never unrelated or inspection-owned by their key. Select dataset catalog/manifest/examples, access/direct-transition events and shared group history through their exact dataset/group identifiers, then invoke the existing validators with server-derived raw label/permission inputs. Queued jobs retain requested intents, including unresolved source references, without inventing materialized copies. Completed/partial copied entries require their actual validated labels, grants and group versions. Report related, copied and requested inspection subsets separately; mixed jobs/groups do not expand requester authority.

Add private typed DatasetCorpusLimits with explicit corpus (InspectionCorpusLimits), family_max_records, family_max_payload_bytes, max_datasets and max_groups, all finite exact positive integers and no operational defaults. Corpus bounds cover original entries/bytes and one cumulative work/deadline across every preceding selector. The separate family bounds cover raw input expansion, including repeated shared sources passed into nested dataset/group validators; do not silently multiply the corpus limit or relax existing parser assertions. Charge this expansion against the original cumulative work budget before nested validation, cap job/group roots before processing their families, and preserve one nonrenewable monotonic deadline. Every known supported group requires complete creator/declassification/repeat history; missing history holds. Keep shared group/context rows as explicit residual obligations even after historical validation; retention/redaction and owner review remain separate policies.

### Shared asset prefix clarification — before selector repair

Source review of `evidence/references.py` confirms that committed reference images and inspection frames both use `state/asset#<id>`. A supported capture-family selection must not assume that prefix proves an observation parent. A record declaring reference kind remains an explicit unresolved reference-family obligation until its own complete supported policy validates it; it is not silently dropped, unrelated or owned by an inspection. A capture frame whose fields are changed to claim reference kind must still fail complete capture-family validation when the required frame is missing. Improvement scope kind must agree with the actual source family; reference permission history cannot grant frame ownership. This clarification is reported before the staged selector repair and preserves the full classifier/cleanup acceptance requirements.

### Required acceptance evidence

Keep existing T01–T58 and A07/child scenarios as acceptance scopes. Add their implementation cases rather than inventing a new top-level workflow or counting planned cases as execution:

1. Complete no-copy and target-only families; all required capture/human/support/label/purpose/dataset/replay inputs selected from the original full corpus, including expired/cancelled/failed records.
2. Two inspections sharing runtime/station/recipe/user/context remain separate; copied target content in a mixed dataset, inbox, event or replay wrapper is found. Unrelated content is preserved or explicitly held for collateral review.
3. Wrong parent IDs, missing reverse links/peers/history, forged hashes, stale revisions, legacy provenance, unknown family/version, conflicting adapters and prior erasure markers hold without false completion.
4. Omit each required input independently, including a copy whose only relation is inside nested retained content. Selection must fail even if the downstream parser accepts the remaining subset.
5. Verify deterministic order-independent coverage, exact once-only accounting, bounded cycles/edge work, exact-limit success and one-over rejection for each independent budget, deadline regression/expiry and payload-size limits.
6. Exercise mutation of caller objects, forged held artifacts, swapped configuration/target/proof, source change between phases, concurrent insertion, cancellation and uncertain thread exit. No accepted partial plan or premature resource release.
7. Demonstrate actual-store orchestration under one held gate and installed-package origin/source equality; retain original failed cases, all phases and cleanup evidence. Pure family/model tests cannot replace this boundary.
8. Finally execute recent-MFA owner preview/confirmation, denied roles, expired/changed intent, exact retry/uncertain outcome, actual conditional cleanup/journal/restore and rendered requester receipt/exit. Verify complete-copy obligations and honest pending/shared status. Physical/human/provider acceptance remains separate; no schema/classifier pass closes beta.

Ownership: P06 implements and verifies the server behavior; P07 assesses exact request/copy and communication obligations; P05 assesses admitted target, journal/restore and executor lifecycle; P01 authorizes exact consequential scope. These responsibility labels do not identify staffed approvers or grant access. Current disposition: CONTRACT SPECIFIED; IMPLEMENTATION AND WORKFLOW ACCEPTANCE NOT_RUN.


### Unsupported asset schemas during complete corpus selection

Reported before the held-reader integration repair, October 16, 2026. Actual local-store testing found the shared station fixture retains a legacy reference declaration under `state/asset#<id>` with no `kind` or `observation_id`. Prefix alone cannot establish frame ownership. An asset without an observation parent remains an explicit residual obligation, just as an explicitly declared reference does; this neither validates its schema nor assigns an unrelated/erasable classification. Malformed parent values on purported frames still hold. Complete capture-family validation must independently reject any referenced frame omitted by this dispatch, including a frame stripped of its parent or disguised as a reference. Existing ordinary active work is retained, including foreign/expired work; hold can stop admission but it cannot declare a drain. Current owners with recent MFA may read a bounded request/status view for this deployment, including original scope IDs/hash, source version, original owner relationship, gate phase, active count/foreign-work indication and explicit incomplete/no-cleanup flags. Reading does not hold, classify, mutate business data, grant deletion or resume. Use a bounded process-local read allowance without writing quota during a read; durable mutation allowance applies separately.

Explicit cancellation is permitted to a current owner only for the exact held request version/scope, matching gate and confirmed empty registry, with no destructive commitment. Require expected gate/runtime versions and separate acknowledgment that cancellation completes no erasure. Atomically preserve request scope/provenance in cancelled history, advance gate epoch and empty registry fencing together, advance DATA and runtime control version, clear enable/readiness authority and keep inspections disabled. Idempotent historical retry never cancels another hold or reopens newer state. Cancellation cannot abandon active/foreign work, bypass uncertain completion or reverse a future destructive status. Actual identity recovery while held and exact HTTP dispatch remain MC-01 integration requirements, not implemented by this service.

### Independent erasure journal storage — contract before implementation

PP09/W20/W22, T41/T58. Gap reported before implementation: independent erasure-journal persistence is required, but its minimized record, conditional append and confirmed-readback protocol were undefined. Add a private DynamoErasureJournal over an explicitly supplied DynamoRepository/client/deployment and an explicit journal table name/expected ARN, separate from both main tables. It creates no client/table, route, factory enrollment, cleanup capability or new role. The max_record_bytes bound counts the canonical sorted-key, compact, ASCII-escaped JSON UTF-8 encoding of the entire stored SDK Item, including its pk/sk keys, typed data and digest; it is not a provider billing or HTTP-wire-byte claim. Reserve sufficient SDK-call capacity before any effect (seven invocations for append; five for lookup), with no automatic SDK retry. Before/after each protocol, check the exact active table ARN/name, pk/sk string schema, absence of secondary indexes and disabled TTL. Bind original repository/client/deployment/table mapping and SDK configuration; require single-attempt SDK behavior and explicit finite record/API-call limits with the original deadline, nondecreasing clock and authority checkpoints. Provider-call timeouts are not a hard process deadline; a late return holds acknowledgment and the enclosing registered executor must remain owned until actual exit.

Schema1 stores an immutable intent: reference_sha256 (the existing opaque erasure-job/scope reference), resource_sha256 (the existing logical deployment/table/resource identity digest), exact source_revision within the existing Erase bound, source_payload_sha256, classification_sha256, confirmation_sha256 and original created_at_ms. Journal keys use the exact deployment and opaque resource/reference digests; a canonical digest binds every stored field. No raw resource keys, payloads, credentials, actor/session capabilities or images are copied. A digest records a supplied intent; it does not authenticate a classification/confirmation or grant removal rights.

Append uses one conditional put at most, followed by exact consistent readback. An existing byte-equivalent semantic intent returns the original record; changed intent at the same identity conflicts. Conditional failure or transport-unknown write can succeed only after matching durable readback; missing, malformed, conflicting or unavailable readback remains held. No blind retry, overwrite, delete, expiry renewal or memory fallback. Readback absence is not proof that a historical journal was complete or a resource was never erased. A journal receipt has no erase/restore/fulfillment authority. The complete service must first obtain genuinely accepted classification and exact current owner confirmation, then persist/verify journal intent before any corresponding payload scrub.

This storage boundary provides logical table separation and target checks, not IAM independence, immutable backups, complete journal coverage/anchors or provider-qualified restore authority. Actual D11 journal retention, target enrollment, independent journal coverage/replay, writer compatibility, cleanup jobs and requester closeout remain mandatory. Do not enable real collection/cleanup from test policy values. The native tests use only an owned ephemeral local database; no actual AWS or requester data is authorized by this contract.
