## Complete declared journal scope — read-only prerequisite JS01–JS05

Gap reported before implementation: per-resource journal append/read proves one immutable intent, not presence of every intent in an approved cleanup scope. Existing owner requests still have only held/cancelled states. Full owner preview/confirmation, cleanup jobs, independent history anchors, restore compatibility and requester closeout remain required; this prerequisite does not replace them.

The trusted coordinator must derive an ordered immutable tuple of JournalIntent values from its actual approved manifest. A caller-supplied digest or detached receipt is not approval. For JS01, require a nonempty tuple, exact validated intents, strictly increasing unique resource digests and one common reference, classification digest, confirmation digest and original creation time. Compute a versioned streaming SHA256 over a canonical JSON header (versioned domain, exact deployment, sorted original primary-table identities, expected journal ARN and count) followed by canonical journal bodies. Prefix the header and every body with its unsigned eight-byte big-endian byte length. This is a private deterministic binding, not a public arbitrary-key API or a stored independent history anchor.

For JS02/JS03, JournalScopeLimits requires positive exact-integer max_records, max_manifest_bytes, max_sdk_calls and max_work within the existing signed64 ceiling, without defaults or request overrides. Manifest bytes count every length prefix, header and body. Before I/O reserve five SDK calls per intent, the unchanged journal read protocol, and3N+2 bounded work units: two header/intent digest passes plus one read step per intent. Require the underlying journal to admit its original five-call read protocol. Each digest pass independently fits the same manifest-byte bound; the work allowance covers both. Do not renew a child deadline or cumulative allowance. These are protocol/configuration requirements, not accepted deployment budgets or D11 retention values.

JS04: retain the original absolute monotonic deadline, checked clock and trusted checkpoint across every digest step, native read and final delivery. Pin the exact journal/client/target/limits and scope-limit identities/typed values. Use the existing genuine consistent journal read for each selected resource; verify exact original intent, stored digest and false action-authority flags. Missing, corrupt, unavailable or mismatched durable rows hold the complete result. A changed expected manifest digest conflicts before any SDK call. Repeat the bounded manifest digest and final guards before returning; reject caller mutation, changed configuration, expiry, reversal or exhausted bounds. No accepted partial coverage and no write/erase occur.

JS05: return only a frozen minimized coverage receipt with scope digest, count, manifest bytes, consumed work and reserved SDK calls. It is historical and read-only; declared_scope_complete is true only for the explicitly supplied matching tuple. can_erase, restore_authorized, request_fulfilled, global_history_complete and independent_iam_verified remain false. A complete declared tuple does not prove that all historical scopes were supplied, that IAM is independent, or that physical copies/backups are gone. Logical append-only reads are not a multi-item atomic snapshot or protection against privileged out-of-band deletion. Real cleanup still requires current owner/MFA, actual accepted policy, source/storage closure and exact confirmation; restore requires independent complete-history anchors/replay and writer compatibility.

Acceptance: exercise missing-final and wrong-original intents, duplicates/order/cross-scope changes, exact/one-below count/byte/work/SDK limits, expiry/reversal/checkpoint/configuration changes and late mutation, correct complete multi-record scopes and all non-authorizing flags. Add actual owned-local-table coverage and prove primary rows and journal bytes are unchanged by verification. Synthetic classification/confirmation digests are not actual approval. This remains private until the complete owner/worker/HTTP/workbench workflow is integrated; no live-data erasure is authorized.

Validated-value and budget clarification (JS02/JS04): the first digest pass captures private JournalIntent copies from the same validated body dictionaries used for common-scope and strict ordering checks, after byte admission. Reads use those copies. A final caller digest catches persistent drift but cannot alone detect mutate-and-restore behavior; never reread mutable caller objects as authoritative original intents. The5N reservation covers journal protocol calls only. The trusted caller must separately bound authority, closure and checkpoint I/O within its original total budget and deadline; a private coverage receipt cannot attest those external obligations.

## Record a complete declared journal scope — JC01–JC06

Contract gap reported before implementation: the later cleanup coordinator needs one bounded operation that durably records every exact manifest intent and verifies the complete declared scope before any primary removal. Implement private append_journal_scope using the existing journal and strict manifest binding; it supplies no approval or deletion grant. The trusted coordinator still derives the actual approved manifest, performs current authority/source/closure checks, and separately budgets those checks. D11 is not inferred.

JC01: validate the same ordered nonempty manifest, immutable validated-body copies, exact expected digest and pinned target/limits. Before storage effects, reserve12N SDK calls (7N append plus5N final read),5N+2 work (two digest passes, N complete-item preflights, N appends and N reads), and require the underlying original seven-call append capacity. All items must fit their unchanged journal item-byte limit before the first append. No default, public override, new deadline or automatic scope expansion.

JC02–JC04: append exact captured intents via original conditional immutable journal semantics, then re-read every original intent. Lost replies reconcile only through durable readback. A partial failure preserves already written immutable intent records; it never rolls back journal history or reports completion. A later explicit attempt with the exact same manifest may reconcile existing records and fill missing ones within its own bounded execution; it does not renew original approval, creation time or retention. Changed original intent conflicts without overwriting.

JC05: preserve the single original operation deadline, checked clock/configuration and trusted checkpoint through preflight, each append/read and final delivery. Repeat the original caller digest, deny persistent mutation, and use captured values to prevent mutate-and-restore substitution. No accepted partial receipt.

JC06: return a frozen minimized JournalScopeCommit with the same digest/count/byte/work/call fields, historical=true, read_only=false, declared_scope_complete=true. can_erase, restore_authorized, request_fulfilled, global_history_complete and independent_iam_verified all remain false. This records intent, not primary erasure, a persisted manifest/global anchor, an atomic multi-item transaction or protection against privileged deletion. Complete cleanup jobs/owner confirmation/independent history/restore/requester integration remains required.

Acceptance: exact creation/retry, partial outage then exact reconciliation, lost reply, wrong manifest/changed intent, pre-effect item/count/SDK/work limits, cancellation/expiry/configuration drift, final missing/corrupt readback, caller mutation including restoration, all authority flags and unchanged primary stores. Local synthetic tests do not approve actual business data operations.

## Independent sealed journal history — JA01–JA08

Gap reported before implementation: full W19/W20/W22 requires independently retained scope/history anchors, but root enrollment, atomic sequencing and exact restore checkpoints were undefined. Add a private JournalAnchorStore over the original DynamoErasureJournal, no client/table creation or public/factory/worker enrollment. Its root, mutable conditional head and immutable scope anchors live in the separate journal table. JournalAnchorLimits requires explicit positive exact integers for max_scopes, max_total_intents, max_item_bytes, max_sdk_calls and max_work; item bytes may only tighten the journal item-byte ceiling. No defaults or approved D11 values are inferred.

JA01 initialization: an externally selected opaque genesis SHA256 identifies one journal enrollment. Only a genuinely empty deployment partition may be initialized. Require trusted exclusive provisioning/authority checks before/through effects; a query for emptiness does not itself provide a writer lock. Atomically create immutable root and initial head with absence conditions, then exact consistent readback. Existing exact root/head may reconcile an original retry; an already advanced head is historical enrollment, not a reset. Different enrollment or unrecognized records hold. This does not import pre-enrollment erasure history or certify IAM independence.

JA02 seal: accept the exact original expected head checkpoint (sequence and digest), original ordered JournalIntent tuple and expected scope digest. Require every original durable intent through read_journal_scope; no detached scope receipt grants authority. Atomically put one immutable content-hashed anchor and conditionally replace the head from the exact original sequence/digest. Anchor binds exact deployment/table/genesis, previous digest, next sequence, scope digest and every minimized original intent. Whole-item bytes and all calls/work must fit before mutation. No primary data writes, blind transaction retry, branch overwrite, scope expansion or approval renewal.

JA03 original acknowledgment: a lost transaction reply succeeds only after exact consistent anchor/head readback. An exact current-head retry returns original identity without writing; a different or advanced head conflicts. A sealed historical scope remains retrievable through history verification but is not a reusable current confirmation. Partial intent prefixes remain unsealed and cannot authorize cleanup.

JA04–JA06 verification: caller supplies a trusted independently retained genesis and exact head sequence/digest checkpoint, not just a value read from the restored primary store. Read current root/head, traverse every immutable predecessor to genesis with descending exact sequences, rederive anchor and manifest digests and read every original intent. Repeat original head/root binding and target/deadline checks before delivery. Missing/tampered/cyclic/out-of-order/cross-target/over-limit/late results hold with no accepted partial receipt. This proves sealed-history completeness relative to the explicit checkpoint since genesis, not completeness of unsealed intents, pre-enrollment history, privileged writes or all external copies. Live seal/restore coordinators must protect checkpoint enrollment and revalidate current owner/policy/closure themselves.

JA07 bounded execution: use the original absolute deadline/nondecreasing clock, exact pinned journal/client/ARN/configuration and checkpoints throughout all steps. Reserve method-specific worst-case calls before storage effects; history work includes every root/head/anchor and supplied intent. Every nested scope read uses remaining original total call/work allowances; no renewed child timeout. Enforce max_scopes and max_total_intents before allocation/iteration. Keep input copies privately validated; output contains only minimized checkpoint/count/digest/budget fields.

JA08 receipts: historical=true, sealed_history_complete only after complete checkpoint traversal; can_erase, restore_authorized, request_fulfilled, global_history_complete and independent_iam_verified=false. Initialization/sealing receipts do not claim full history verification. Full primary tombstone replay, compatible writers, configured cleanup, owner preview/confirmation and requester communication remain required. Actual D11/target/IAM/backup enrollment and permission are unresolved; synthetic local use is not approval.

Acceptance: actual SDK/local-store initialization, exact enrollment retry and changed genesis, atomic seal and unchanged retry, lost reply, stale competing heads, wrong/partial manifest, missing/corrupt root/head/anchor/intent, exact/one-below independent limits, late/cancelled/configuration changes and immutable outputs; verify primary rows unchanged and local cleanup. Keep full workflow/provider/human acceptance separate.

JA implementation/budget follow-through: journal_anchors.py now implements private initialize/seal/read_history with closed canonical SDK items and target-bound digests. Initialization reserves10calls/32work; seal reserves11+5N calls/64+8N work; history reserves8+S+5I calls/64+16S+8I work, where S is the exact requested sequence and I is configured max_total_intents (zero for sequence0). These are conservative protocol reservations, not measured cost, elapsed-time guarantees or accepted deployment defaults. Whole-item byte limits bound initial SDK/JSON decoding; independent count bounds are checked before typed-intent allocation/traversal. Native unknown reads are held; transaction-unknown replies reconcile only through exact durable state. Preserve existing original owner/drain/primary-source authority separately. Local161/same161installed/31store proof covers the component, not full W19/W20/W22 or actual checkpoint/IAM enrollment. Root provisioning requires a protected exclusive procedure; an empty query alone is not a lock or proof of pre-enrollment absence.

## Restore identity and release fence — RS01–RS06

Gap reported before implementation while the750-input regression is frozen: W19 requires tombstone replay before exposure, but the isolated-target mapping, trusted history checkpoint and startup/data-admission release contract were unspecified. The current journal primitives do not implement this workflow. P05/P06 own technical delivery; P01/P07 supply actual authorized purposes/retention and target decisions. No deployment, restore, real erasure, new account role or release authority follows from this contract.

RS01 — preserve both identities. Existing resource_sha256 is the hash of metadata-payload-erasure/v1, logical deployment, logical table role and resource key; it excludes physical table names. The declared scope digest additionally binds the original physical state/events mapping and journal ARN. Restore verification must retain the original source identity projection for journal history and use a separate explicitly verified target repository for the isolated restored tables. Do not recompute the old scope against new physical names, change deployment prefixes, rewrite old digests or silently backfill lineage. A cross-region/account migration requires separately supported and verified clients/target mapping; it is not inferred from matching names.

RS02 — protected enrollment. Require an independently retained exact genesis/head checkpoint, original source table/deployment identity, exact isolated target identity, backup/snapshot identifier and complete inventory provenance, current installed artifact/writer protocol and explicit host-operation authority. Do not obtain the trusted checkpoint solely from the older primary backup. Missing or unsupported enrollment stays held; no invented owner, retention value or legacy no-erasure attestation. Root enrollment alone does not prove pre-enrollment erasure history absent.

RS03 — reconcile through bounded inventory. Journal intent records retain opaque resource digests, not raw keys. Derive each original logical identity from an exact complete restored-target inventory, match declared journal intents without exposing raw keys/payloads in ordinary logs, and preserve original source versus target relationships. Bound pages, records, bytes, dependencies, calls, work and cumulative job time before each typed allocation/step. Unknown/missing scope, changed inventory, unsupported payload/marker versions, mismatched source revisions/digests or ambiguous mapping holds release. Absence in one page, an ordinary snapshot returning None, or a missing row does not establish fulfilled deletion. Full backup/inventory provenance must explain an absent target; retain that limited conclusion separately from original request fulfillment.

RS04 — deny exposure before reconciliation. An isolated restore must not serve app data, authenticate from restored sessions, admit ordinary workers or perform ordinary writes until reconciliation is accepted. Inspection-disabled alone is insufficient because historical reads and auth/configuration paths also exist. Fixed no-data liveness may remain available. Recovery uses a separately authorized host procedure; the ordinary app is not a privileged bypass. Preserve the existing distinction between Row, ErasureMarker and absent values: snapshot() intentionally hides markers, inventory_snapshot() is required for reconciliation, and marker absence is not erasure proof. No unconditional Write/Delete, old-revision resurrection or reset of the journal/maintenance fence.

RS05 — reconcile effects and compatible writers. A sealed intent is not proof that primary erasure occurred or that its supplied approval digests were valid. The complete cleanup coordinator must supply genuine accepted job/classification/current confirmation and intent-to-effect receipts; uncertain/interrupted intents remain denied/pending. Validate exact existing markers and source/provenance before any authorized conditional scrub, then reconcile unknown commit outcomes by durable readback. Preserve immutable journal history and backup/downloaded-copy obligations. A changed source cannot be silently overwritten. Replayed markers must remain non-resurrectable by create/update/idempotency/recovery/configuration paths; restored sessions and previous runtime/enablement authority must remain invalid. Actual IAM/host restrictions on older binaries require separate evidence.

RS06 — release is a separate guarded decision. Bind the completed reconciliation to original source identity, exact target, backup provenance, trusted checkpoint, complete reconciled inventory, current artifact/writer protocol and new runtime/control/data authority. Recheck the journal head, target, sources, executor drain and budgets before atomically recording the release fence; changed bindings invalidate it. Configured startup, all data-reading routes and ordinary workers must consume that fence before exposure. Owner recovery/inspection enablement remains separately required; the receipt cannot enable inspections, approve shipment or mark a requester fulfilled. Complete media/versioned-object/derived-copy duties and actual target/identity/IAM rehearsal remain explicit dependencies.

Acceptance under T23/T28/T47/T54/T58: same logical source identity on distinct physical restore tables; changed/cross-target identities held; preserved original scope digests; trusted-checkpoint rollback/missing/tampered history; complete and incomplete inventory; Row/marker/absent distinctions; uncertain intent/effect reconciliation; incompatible writers/old sessions; interrupted/resumed bounded job with no renewed authority; denied historical reads/workers before release and after a changed head; exact source/target/artifact-bound release with separate owner enablement. All RS01–RS06 execution starts NOT_RUN. Test only isolated synthetic local data until exact target and operation are authorized.

### Sealed-history manifest delivery for restore reconciliation

The private JournalAnchorStore.read_manifest returns the same fully verified genesis-to-head history as read_history, plus immutable scopes ordered newest to oldest. Each scope retains its exact sequence, anchor digest, original scope digest and copied original intents. The result binds the original logical deployment, physical state/events mapping and journal ARN; a restore target is separate and cannot rewrite these identities. Return no partial manifest on missing/corrupt source, changed configuration/head, cancellation or original budget/deadline exhaustion. Existing complete-history reservations bound all scope/intent allocations; zero-head history has an empty tuple. No raw primary keys or payloads are returned.

This private component supports RS01/RS03 only. Counts, copied intents and sealed history do not establish trusted enrollment, complete restored inventory, accepted classification/confirmation, applied erasure, compatible writers or release. All consequential receipt flags remain false. Full RS01–RS06 consumer/startup/worker/release integration remains required; the manifest component has scoped source/installed/local-store execution in BUILD-STATUS, while complete RS01–RS06 execution remains NOT_RUN.

### Restore backup-to-target inventory binding — RI01–RI06

RI01: a private read-only reconciliation operation takes the original JournalAnchorStore and independently retained checkpoint, an exact isolated DynamoRepository target with the same logical deployment but distinct physical tables, exact target table ARNs in the journal's supported account/region, an explicit backup/provenance digest, original-source InventoryPass and current installed-artifact digest. Validate the original inventory's canonical summary against the original source mapping; preserve its contents and original identities. These supplied provenance values require actual host verification and are not authenticated by a hash. Unsupported cross-account/region or physical overlap stays held.

RI02: describe and verify both target table identities, active key schema and disabled TTL; preserve exact SDK/client/configuration identity. The host-supplied checkpoint must enforce isolated target admission and executor drain throughout; this reader cannot create that barrier. Collect every page of both target partitions, compare the complete ordered entries with the supplied backup inventory and retain the target's own canonical summary. Different physical mapping must not change original logical resource hashes or journal scope digests.

RI03: derive original opaque resource hashes from each target entry. Deduplicate only exactly identical intents repeated across sealed scopes; conflicting intents for one digest remain held. A live row matches an intent only at its exact original revision/payload digest and is pending effect, not approved cleanup. A marker matches only its exact resource/reference and original revision plus one, and is marker-observed, not proof of approved cleanup or requester fulfillment. An absent intent target is unresolved-absent, never deletion proof. Mismatched rows/markers or ambiguous identities stay held.

RI04: reserve cumulative journal reads, target describe/TTL calls, both complete inventory scans and bounded mapping work before any SDK effect. Preserve the original absolute deadline, monotonic clock, cancellation, count/page/summary-byte limits and configuration. Return no partial result on exhausted bounds or changed inputs.

RI05: repeat the complete target scan and trusted history read and recheck exact backup/provenance/artifact/source/target/limit bindings before delivery. A mismatch withholds the result. This finite read relies on the actual host barrier; it is not an atomic whole-database snapshot or durable release fence.

RI06: result retains exact source/target/checkpoint/backup/artifact/inventory digests and private match statuses; no raw payloads are delivered or logged. All can_erase, restore_authorized, can_release and request_fulfilled flags are false. Global enrollment, genuine classification/current confirmation/effect receipts, primary scrub, media/copy duties, writer compatibility, startup/worker fencing and guarded release remain separate required RS01–RS06 work. The private RI01–RI06 component has scoped model/installed/local-store results in BUILD-STATUS. Full RS01–RS06 integration and human acceptance remain NOT_RUN under existing T23/T28/T47/T54/T58; no new public API, role or real restore authority.

### Protected restore data fence — RF01–RF07

Gap reported before implementation: configured startup currently starts runtime before metadata enrollment; the restore release contract has no protected-record schema, same-name table replacement identity, or atomic repository condition. The fence below is required before RS04/RS06 can be accepted. Current diagnostics do not create a valid release.

RF01: consume an exact protected release in the independent journal table, separate from primary backups. Bind logical deployment, both physical target names and TableId creation identities, exact journal ARN, externally retained genesis/checkpoint, current installed artifact/writer protocol, backup/source identity/inventory/effect/classification/confirmation/media-coverage digests and a new runtime-generation digest. Keep the expected release digest in the trusted host configuration. Missing, held, malformed, mismatched or changed evidence denies access; never infer release from no row, an empty table, expiry or read-only RI results. Same-name tables with different TableId are different targets. Actual ordinary-role denial of control-plane table replacement and protected writes remains mandatory IAM evidence.

RF02: the protected rows use the existing canonical anchor root/head and a closed canonical restored-data-release record keyed by the target identity digest. Check exact original root/head and body/hash in one consistent read. Release digests are domain separated. A stored hash alone is insufficient: validate and hash the actual canonical closed body. Release proof digests are references for the genuine coordinator, not evidence authenticity or ownership. No ordinary app endpoint or this reader writes release/enrollment records.

RF03: an attached DynamoRepository fence protects every snapshot/marker/inventory read, query/partition page and transaction. Check exact target metadata and protected state under one original finite monotonic deadline, fixed single-attempt SDK configuration and bounded calls. Consistent snapshots include the three protected rows in the same TransactGet; writes include exact-body/hash ConditionChecks for all three rows in the same TransactWrite. Protected change between preparation and commit rejects the write atomically. Three protected items leave at most97business items per transaction. No unconditional scrub, public role or independent primary bypass is added.

RF04: queries have pre/post protected checks before output, without claiming an atomic whole-partition snapshot. Preserve no partial output on changed identity/configuration/head/release, timeout, malformed reply or cancellation. Unknown write outcomes remain uncertain and need durable exact readback; do not retry blindly. A gate check does not defeat a privileged administrator who bypasses the required quarantine/restore/IAM procedure.

RF05: configured non-test startup must eventually require this exact fence before the first runtime/auth/data write, and all HTTP/auth/static/data paths and ordinary workers must consume it; only fixed no-data liveness may bypass. Isolated explicit loopback test configurations may retain separately labelled unfenced fixtures. Attaching a private repository fence alone does not satisfy configured startup/HTTP/worker acceptance. Host configuration and genuine protected release writer integration remain required until verified.

RF06: restore startup must invalidate restored sessions and prior runtime/control/data/enablement authority; owner enablement stays separate. The release record cannot approve shipment or mark a privacy request fulfilled. Actual enrollment, backup provenance, accepted classification/current confirmation/applied effects, media/copy duties and old-binary/IAM restrictions remain prerequisites, not caller checkboxes.

RF07: source/model/local-store tests under T23/T28/T47/T54/T58 must challenge missing/corrupt/changed release/root/head, target name/TableId/config/client substitution, expired/backwards clocks, exact transaction97/98 boundaries, query pre/post change, commit-time gate change, unknown commit reply, all repository entrypoints and current package. Configured full-journey/actual-target acceptance starts NOT_RUN.

RF implementation applicability: RC01–RC07 configured consumers, RO01–RO05 pending OAuth generation, RD01–RD08 startup/DATA/revisions and HS01–HS07 private reference/readback now have selected local source/installed proof in BUILD-STATUS. Strict full runtime/auth-history readers remain structural, not present authority. Original8creation-ID positive calls remain unaccepted because the emulator omits TableId; neither synthetic metadata nor the new actual missing-metadata denial replaces them. Genuine effect/release/enrollment writers, full private restore coordinator, classification/current confirmation, cleanup/requester/media duties and actual target/IAM/backup/provider/log/human evidence remain unfinished RF06/RS05/RS06 work; no full restore admission claim.

### Fenced inventory snapshot capacity — FC01–FC04

FC01: DynamoRepository.snapshot_capacity is100 for the existing unfenced adapter and97 when the exact original protected data fence is attached. Reading capacity performs no SDK I/O, but rejects a removed/replaced fence or changed fenced repository identity. Other inventory adapters retain their declared supported capacity or the existing100-item default; all capacities must be exact integers in1–100.

FC02: inventory.reconcile captures the effective capacity once, calculates complete snapshot batches from that value and checks the original max_snapshot_batches before snapshot I/O. Use that same captured size for every batch. Page size, record/payload/summary bounds and original absolute job deadline remain unchanged. A smaller capacity never increases the caller's batch allowance automatically.

FC03: recheck original capacity, target, client, clock and authority at existing checkpoints and after clock callbacks. Missing/invalid/changing capacity or a changed/removed attached fence withholds all output; never silently switch97to100, restart, extend validity or treat a partial read set as complete. Preserve live/marker/absent distinctions and complete follow-up inventory comparison.

FC04: tests under existing T41/T54/T58 cover97/98,194/195, zero rows, independent snapshot/payload bounds, changed/invalid capacity, actual attached-fence model reads and the existing100-item unfenced behavior. This compatibility change does not complete mandatory configured startup/HTTP/worker wiring or provide a real restore release. Original positive TableId-dependent local-store calls remain blocked; no adapter fallback or relaxed creation-ID check is authorized.

FC execution applicability:209source/same209installed and16unfenced local-store cases pass at the current source boundary. The separate5-case fenced-store diagnostic is memory-held before database startup, with no executed phases or consumed original60-second execution allowance. Original TableId-dependent positive calls and full restore/host/human acceptance remain unaccepted.

### Configured restore-fence consumers — RC01–RC07

RC01: non-test ApplicationConfig requires an exact RestoreFenceConfig, matching application artifact, supported journal account/region distinct from both primary tables, and explicit metadata admission. The original check window must fit both HTTP and worker leases. The only unfenced factory exception is environment=test with the already validated explicit loopback database endpoint. HostDocument exposes mandatory restore_fence plus metadata_admission; the restore object contains journal_table, journal_arn, genesis_sha256, checkpoint(sequence,digest), release_sha256, target_table_ids(events,state) and max_check_ms. Derive its artifact binding from application.artifact_sha256; do not accept a conflicting second artifact. Syntax validity does not verify protected records, rights, target identity or readiness.

RC02: construct one exact RestoreDataFence and attach it to the application's shared DynamoRepository before constructing access/runtime/services. Startup verifies caller/table identity and the protected target/release before the first runtime write. A failed check does not start ordinary workers or create runtime/auth authority; cleanup still follows actual executor exit. A source-bound local fixture can inject metadata/proofs only as a disclosed diagnostic, never actual release acceptance.

RC03: the shared ASGI restore boundary runs outside data/auth/static handlers and metadata admission, after query redaction. It checks before downstream work and every response start/body delivery, denies unsupported file-send extensions and closes unsupported WebSockets. A held response is a sanitized503 with no provider text, cookies, redirects or data. Only fixed no-data GET/HEAD liveness bypasses. If headers have already started, withhold subsequent bytes and do not fabricate a replacement successful response.

RC04: restore checks and request/response lifetimes use the same ResourceOwner as metadata HTTP/ordinary-worker admission. No asynchronous cancellation is proof of executor exit: retain uncertain ownership when completion is unconfirmed, and track actual sync checks through their finally callbacks. Ordinary worker repositories share the attached fence; protected hold must stop discovery/reservation/effects. This does not grant privileged restore-coordinator bypass.

RC05: legacy session bodies keep their existing closed shape. A fenced login emits exactly that shape plus session_schema_version=2 and restore_generation_sha256 from the validated protected release. Fenced resolution requires that exact version/current generation; legacy, missing/malformed/mismatched generation, or a version2 session on an unfenced resolver is denied. _fresh repeats this check, so old Actors cannot regain authority. Neither old sessions nor generation fields are automatically backfilled; reauthentication requires the existing real provider/MFA/notice checks.

RC06: shared session-shape validation accepts only the original closed legacy fields or the closed version2 extension with an exact integer2 (including lossless finite DynamoDB Decimal2, excluding floats, booleans, strings and nonfinite values) and lowercase64hex generation. Auth-history and inspection-origin readers retain all existing identity/time/linkage checks and treat version2 as structural history, not a present grant or retention/cleanup right. Existing report policy IDs remain structural-review format IDs; changed code/artifact applicability and explicit input session version are still required. No old-session validity or erasure authority follows from history parsing.

RC07: verify mandatory/missing/malformed host configuration without SDK I/O; shared constructor identities; startup denial before runtime writes; HTTP/auth/static/HEAD/live/streaming/cancellation behavior; worker effect denial; old/new/mismatched session generations; strict versioned history parsing; real package identity and cleanup. Complete restore DATA-authority regeneration, genuine effect/release/enrollment writer, backup/media/copy/IAM proof, actual creation-ID-capable target and human acceptance remain required RF06/RS05/RS06 work. No current consumer test can substitute for those obligations.

## Pending sign-in after restore — RO01–RO05

This refines W04/W19, T23/T28/T54/T58 and P02/P05/P06 recovery. Before this change OAuth flows used the private-keyed provider configuration without the protected runtime generation. A restored pending flow must not authorize a later code exchange. These requirements do not complete runtime/DATA invalidation or the genuine release workflow.

RO01: constructors remain no-I/O. For a real fenced DynamoRepository, derive the flow configuration binding from the existing private-keyed provider binding and the validated protected runtime-generation digest when beginning or consuming a flow. Unfenced isolated adapters retain their existing binding. No generation backfill, restored-flow migration, new role or host option.

RO02: capture the effective binding before quota/flow writes. Preserve the closed existing pending/consumed row schemas, browser binding, exact invitation, MFA, PKCE/nonce, private-key rotation, original quota and absolute expiry. The opaque configuration remains a private-keyed digest; no raw generation, token or provider material becomes public.

RO03: protected consumers reject legacy or different-generation pending flows before changing their status or returning exchange material. Unfenced consumers also reject protected-generation bindings. Changed or missing protected release holds the original resolver; starting a new flow requires the current configured release and ordinary provider/MFA/notice checks.

RO04: repository protected conditions remain on the commit. Recheck the captured effective binding after the actual write and before returning an authorization redirect or ExchangeMaterial. Changed generation/release holds output even if a write committed. Retain unknown/pending/consumed state and spent quotas; never automatically retry, refund, reopen, rebind or exchange after an ambiguous or held outcome.

RO05: test current-generation success, no-I/O construction, old/current/unfenced generation mismatch, invalid/missing release before work, protected change at commit and revocation after commit before output. Preserve original legacy OAuth and strict auth-history tests. Model proof is not an actual Cognito exchange, successful restore, current DATA authority, physical outcome or human understanding; beta NO_GO.

## Protected runtime startup and DATA advancement — RD01–RD08

This specifies the previously incomplete startup transition under RF06/RS06, W19/W20/W22, T23/T28/T41/T54/T58 and P01/P05/P06/P07. It does not authorize a restore, release/enrollment writer, cleanup, requester completion or inspection enablement.

RD01: constructors remain no-I/O. A protected DynamoRepository startup captures the validated release runtime generation and requires its runtime artifact to match that release. A new process incarnation atomically writes a disabled runtime with advanced runtime/control counters, an immutable startup marker/event and a conditionally advanced DATA epoch. Preserve occupied allocations; never silently release kits or recreate missing heads from a previously started runtime.

RD02: the runtime/event/DATA shapes remain unchanged. A protected marker has the existing four fields plus exactly startup_schema_version=2, restore_generation_sha256, previous_data_epoch and data_epoch. Normalize only lossless finite DynamoDB integers; booleans/floats/strings/nonfinite values and unknown/missing fields are invalid. New DATA epoch equals previous+1, with previous0 only for a genuine absent initial DATA head. New runtime/control/DATA epoch counters remain in1..2^63−2; physical row revisions retain the RD08 resource-binding range. Unfenced isolated startup retains its existing legacy marker and DATA behavior.

RD03: each new protected process incarnation advances DATA once, including an ordinary restart under the same release. This intentionally invalidates earlier DATA-bound manifests/approvals; fresh permitted preparation is required. Existing initial DATA is conditionally updated by its exact revision; existing allocation is checked, not cleared. Runtime, marker, event and DATA must commit together under the existing protected transaction conditions. Missing, corrupt, future-dated or exhausted heads hold startup with no partial write.

RD04: an exact marker retry, including readback after an unknown commit reply, advances nothing. It requires the same artifact/controls/environment, protected generation, current runtime instance/epoch and a current DATA epoch at least the marker's recorded epoch. A later runtime incarnation, legacy marker on a protected repository, protected marker on an unfenced repository, malformed marker or different generation denies reuse. No backfill or automatic new identity/attempt after uncertainty.

RD05: preserve ordered startup/event/DATA timestamps and reject backwards time before commit; recheck the captured protected generation and clock before returning a handle. Revocation after a completed write holds output; retain committed state for exact authorized readback. Owner inspection enablement and readiness remain separate. Current provider/MFA/notice checks, original quotas/leases/deadlines and protected transaction key limits remain unchanged.

RD06: both strict runtime-lineage and held runtime-context readers accept the closed legacy marker or closed version2 marker as structural history. A v2 event must include the exact DATA write: creation when previous epoch0 or a one-revision conditional advancement otherwise, with the marker's new epoch and original time constraints. Never accept a conditional DATA advancement under a legacy marker. Preserve missing historical preimage obligations and existing report policy IDs; parsing does not authenticate a current process, prove backup provenance or permit cleanup.

RD07: verify first/repeated/new-incarnation starts, lost replies, cross-generation/legacy/unfenced/superseded markers, malformed/counter/clock/head cases, concurrent DATA/release changes, post-commit revocation and both full history readers. Retain legacy runtime/control/lineage/context and configured factory regressions. Genuine protected enrollment/release/effects, actual creation-ID/IAM/backup/media/provider and physical/human acceptance remain separate unfinished requirements; beta NO_GO.

RD08: shared repository decoding must preserve exact physical revision identity before any ordinary Row can participate in startup, immutable-marker retry or a conditional write. Accept only integer1..2^63−1, including lossless finite DynamoDB Decimal integers. Reject booleans, strings, fractions, nonfinite and out-of-range values; never coerce/truncate them into another revision. Existing resource bindings permit a last conditional previous revision2^63−2 producing row revision2^63−1; runtime/control/DATA epoch counters remain bounded as specified above. Erasure-marker and ordinary-row readers retain their separate content/authority semantics.

RD implementation applicability: closed v2 markers, atomic DATA advancement, exact retry and strict physical revisions have local evidence. HS01–HS07 adds the no-handle, typed read-only host observation/reference step; genuine restore/release/effect/cleanup coordination and actual private-log/target/operator acceptance remain unfinished. Existing structural report IDs and observations do not certify current authority, provenance, retention, erasure or serving readiness.

## Private interrupted-start observation — HS01–HS07

This is the read-only P05/P06 step within W19/W20/W22, T23/T28/T41/T54/T58 and A06-RC1. It does not complete genuine restore/release/effect coordination or requester closeout.

HS01: serve emits one private stderr JSON STARTUP_REFERENCE before entering the server, containing only attempt_identity_only scope, the actual constructed instance_id, configuration_sha256 and configured_artifact_sha256. The configuration digest identifies exact host-document bytes only, not the secrets file, credential validity, current installed binary, committed startup or readiness. The operator must retain the original reference through the approved private service log; do not infer it from a later runtime or invent a replacement. A retained log is not authenticated independent provenance.

HS02: read-startup requires the original canonical --instance-id and lowercase --configuration-sha256 together with the existing private config/secrets files. Reject a document mismatch or invalid original identifiers before credential discovery. No alternate endpoint, credentials/profile override, repair switch, bind/port, owner/worker start, write, re-enrollment, release or scope-expansion option.

HS03: use one refreshable instance-role session and the existing fixed regional bounded clients. Validate STS/account/role, primary metadata and the exact protected target/release/journal/creation identities before observing application state. Use the existing configured HTTP lease as the total private-operation clock allowance and preserve per-fence/per-SDK budgets; clock rollback or deadline exhaustion holds output without retry or renewal. This is not an actual IAM/provider attestation.

HS04: RuntimeControl.observe_startup requires protection and the supplied original instance; it performs bounded snapshots of only runtime, original marker, allocation and DATA. It never calls start, sets its own handle, writes, queries/scans, clears allocations, backfills state or creates a new identity. Reuse RD validation, require an existing exact current protected marker, schema-validate both snapshots, reject visible changes between them, and recheck generation/time before output. The result is a version-bound point-in-time observation, never a guarantee that the store will remain unchanged.

HS05: expose only a closed frozen StartupObservation containing instance/runtime/control/DATA/startup-DATA epochs, row versions, observed time, protected generation and fixed read_only/authority-none fields. Missing, corrupt, cross-generation, superseded or changed state holds. Successful host output adds STARTUP_OBSERVED, the original document digest and fixed no-readiness/no-enablement/no-fulfillment declarations. No raw rows, allocations, users, credentials, provider material or diagnostic exception text.

HS06: read-startup constructs no application, lifespan, worker or server. Close every created client exactly once on success, partial initialization, target/observation failure and cleanup failure. No success output if cleanup fails. Return the existing sanitized host failure/exit2 on held/invalid/unavailable observation; parse errors must not echo arbitrary argument values. A missing marker is a hold, never permission to perform ordinary startup.

HS07: verify the retained-reference ordering, original-instance/config mismatch, successful observation/readback after unknown commit, missing/cross-generation/superseded/changed heads, protection/clock/deadline denial, no handle/write/server/worker, fixed-client credential rules and exact cleanup. Preserve legacy host/configuration/startup regressions. Local synthetic reference/identity tests do not accept actual private-log retention, installed-artifact identity, TableId/IAM/backup/provider, full restore or human understanding; beta NO_GO.

HS implementation applicability: serve reference and read-startup are source-implemented with local model/installed proof. Positive role/release/creation identity is synthetic; the actual local negative observation holds after DescribeTable before reading primary records when TableId is unavailable. Actual original-log retention, caller authority, installed-host identity, protected positive target, full genuine coordinator and uncoached P05/P06 interpretation remain unaccepted. A first random-parameter baseline remains rejected; stable product probes and frame-corruption failures retain their exact immutable oracles and subjects.
## Configured installation identity prerequisite — IV01–IV08

Contract reported before implementation, October 18, 2026. P05/P06, W04/W17/W19, T04/T24/T28/T54. Existing configured artifact/release equality does not establish what files are installed. This prerequisite does not complete release enrollment, restore, cleanup or beta readiness.

- **IV01:** Non-test configured hosting requires a typed `installation` with only an absolute local `wheel_path`. The sole expected digest is the existing `application.artifact_sha256`; no alternate digest, bypass flag, URL, extraction or caller-selected package root. Omission is permitted only in the existing explicit isolated test profile. Host JSON schema remains closed; old documents lacking the field fail syntactic admission.
- **IV02:** `check-config` validates syntax/bounds without opening the wheel or discovering SDK credentials. `serve` and private startup readback verify installation before credential discovery; configured lifespan verifies again before OpenCV/target/runtime/enrollment startup. Invalid content yields sanitized failure and no startup writes.
- **IV03:** Read an ordinary local wheel with at most 32 MiB compressed bytes, 1,024 members and 32 MiB declared expanded bytes; each member is at most 4 MiB. Accept only canonical distinct package `.py`, flat built web and the closed supported wheel metadata inventory. Deny traversal, absolute/backslash/colon/NUL names, duplicate/case aliases, directories, encrypted/symlink/special members and unexpected executable payloads. Never extract.
- **IV04:** Derive the actual package and web paths from application modules. Verify the exact backend and served web inventory and bytes, including index, against that approved wheel. Reject missing, extra, changed, linked or special files; ignore only ordinary `__pycache__` directories. Bound directory visits, depth, path length and bytes. Recheck inventory/file identity after comparison; do not treat a caller-supplied tree as installation evidence.
- **IV05:** Bind configured startup to the immutable web bytes captured by that app instance, so replacing disk assets between construction and startup cannot attest different served bytes. A private readback has no app and checks the current disk tree only.
- **IV06:** Use a monotonic 5-second validation allowance; check before/after reads and reject rollback/expiry. This bounds acceptance, not the physical completion of a stalled OS read. Configured lifecycle verification retains the existing ResourceOwner ticket until actual synchronous exit. Private readback also retains its original total deadline and closes owned clients before output.
- **IV07:** Positive, digest/content/inventory/unsafe-input, precredential/prewrite, changed-snapshot, timeout and actual built/installed-wheel evidence are required. Synthetic archives and SDK identities must be disclosed. No file verification can attest already loaded Python/native code, host/kernel integrity, permissions, genuine IAM or subsequent immutable deployment; those remain separate obligations.
- **IV08:** Output is local installation consistency only. It cannot enable inspections, approve readiness, authenticate business intent or fulfill a privacy request. A changed application/test subject invalidates earlier broad regression applicability; preserve earlier failures and current full-suite NOT_RUN until executed.

## Fresh first-install enrollment — FE01–FE10

Gap reported before implementation, October18,2026: configured hosting cannot start without pre-existing protected release and metadata enrollment. A fresh empty installation has no backup, erasure classification or deletion effects; never manufacture those restore proofs. P01/P05/P06, W02/W04/W17/W19, T04/T23/T24/T28/T47/T54. This is a first-install variant, not restore or cleanup permission.

FE01: `restore_fence.release_kind` defaults to the existing restored-data-release protocol; initial-data-release must be explicitly selected and bind an explicit journal_table_id and sequence-zero genesis checkpoint. Restored releases retain their exact old body/digest domain. Initial releases use their own domain and closed fields, including approved configuration/enrollment identities, controls/runtime generation, initial operation/instance, actual target/journal creation IDs, empty-inventory identity, enrollment role and finite review interval. No backup/classification/confirmation/effects/media digests are invented for the initial kind. Configured consumers validate the exact expected kind/hash and physical journal identity; a fresh receipt never satisfies a restore request.

FE02: add private prepare-enrollment and enroll commands with config/secrets files and a bounded private enrollment file. The enrollment file contains only operation_id, instance_id, role_arn, created_at_ms and expires_at_ms. Require canonical UUIDs, a direct named enrollment IAM role in the configured account, exact positive integer times and a review interval of at most300,000ms. Documents remain closed and bounded; no endpoint/profile/credentials/arbitrary-key/repair/bypass/server options. These commands construct no app, worker or server and do not bootstrap an owner.

FE03: validate local installation before credential discovery, then the existing instance-role/fixed regional client contract. Check actual STS account and the explicitly named assumed enrollment role, all three ACTIVE physical table names/ARNs/TableIds, exact pk/sk string schemas and DISABLED TTL. Require distinct state/events/journal IDs and no operation purpose for this metadata-only bootstrap. Real IAM must separately quarantine all three tables, deny concurrent/old ordinary writers and deny ordinary writes to protected state. Configuration is not evidence that those IAM controls exist.

FE04: preparation is read-only. Under the quarantine prerequisite, perform complete consistent empty scans of all three tables twice, without filters, limits greater than1, pagination omissions or accepting an empty page with a cursor/nonzero scanned count. Any foreign deployment, data or partial prior enrollment holds. Return a minimized candidate release digest and exact original operation/configuration selectors, not readiness or authority. P01/P05 review it through their existing approved process and place that exact digest in the final private host configuration before commit. Preparation never edits that file or approves its own proposal.

FE05: bind the candidate to the normalized ordinary application configuration, excluding its expected release digest and internally derived initial-configuration digest to avoid circular hashes. Separately managed capability/OAuth key material is not included and this receipt does not approve those keys. Material changes to ordinary host fields or the original packet invalidate the candidate; omission versus an equivalent typed default is the same semantic value. The loader derives initial_configuration_sha256 internally rather than accepting another host-file digest, and configured initial-release consumers check that exact configuration binding. The raw document fingerprint remains separate for private startup references. Commit requires the approved expected release hash to equal the recomputed candidate. Recheck role/target/TTL, original installation and empty scans immediately before one all-or-nothing transaction. An application-side scan is not a global empty-table lock; actual IAM quarantine remains mandatory external acceptance.

FE06: atomically insert the three protected records plus the canonical initial disabled runtime, v2 generation-bound original startup marker, DATA epoch1, empty allocation, provenance-bound runtime event, OPEN admission gate and empty registry. Every inserted key has attribute-not-exists protection. Preserve the existing resource-binding format and admission configuration identity. No inspection enablement, readiness receipt, owner/session/user, purpose, privacy job, quota reset, shipping approval or requester-fulfilled flag. Initial OPEN admits ordinary bounded metadata work only; inspection remains disabled and other gates remain held.

FE07: keep one original operation/instance/event/release identity and fixed payload through retry. Before any new write, reconcile an exact existing protected trio and original seed marker; no second transaction when it is already committed. Lost reply alone never authorizes repeating the write within the same invocation. If post-write readback is unavailable, report outcome unknown/held. An explicit later exact retry may read original committed enrollment after review expiry, but cannot create/recreate/repair any record, reopen a HELD admission gate or restore old runtime authority. Changed/missing/partial protected state holds.

FE08: retain the original monotonic total allowance from metadata HTTP lease, nondecreasing wall time,32 cumulative SDK calls and the packet's original review expiry for any fresh write. Check immediately before transaction and before delivery; do not renew bounds or silently retry. Close all owned clients on every result and suppress success if cleanup fails. No partial candidate/commit output or private exception/provider contents on failure. A committed receipt states only the observed bootstrap fact; readiness/enablement/request fulfillment remain false.

FE09: require exact source and offline installed tests covering preparation-no-write, approved commit, independent current fence/runtime/admission consumption, unknown commit readback, unchanged-intent retry after expiry, concurrent missing-key conflict, partial/foreign data, changed configuration/packet/target/role/TTL/clock/installation, explicit-kind separation and cleanup. Actual local missing-TableId denial must not be replaced by a synthetic metadata adapter; positive real-target/IAM acceptance remains separate when unavailable.

FE10: actual authorized invocation, retained external approval/checkpoint, quarantine/old-binary/IAM proof, original log/host trust, genuine restored release/effect/enrollment coordination, complete privacy/requester/media duties, D11 and human acceptance remain required at their respective boundaries. Fresh enrollment cannot approve beta, operate on nonempty restored tables or authorize a production change from this local build task.


FE implementation applicability: private prepare/review/enroll, initial-kind/normalized-configuration/physical-journal binding and atomic conditional metadata seed have current source/installed proof in BUILD-STATUS. A final prewrite expiry failure and actual configured serve lookup failure are preserved; their original oracles now pass. Old mocked serve-reference evidence did not establish actual app startup; current code uses runtime_control. Actual native denial uses real missing TableId; the separate raw storage projection is not private-command or IAM acceptance. No owner, readiness or inspection enablement is created. Fresh first-install support cannot satisfy restored release/effect/classification/privacy/requester duties. Full current suite, actual target/quarantine/old-binary/role retirement/provider/physical/human proof and D11 remain unresolved; beta NO_GO.

## First-owner browser handoff — FO01–FO06

Source gap reported before implementation: ordinary verified sign-in cannot create the initial owner, and the existing atomic AccessService.bootstrap has no production sign-in caller. Metadata enrollment deliberately does not create a member. The [FO01–FO06 contract](../.loop/repairs/first-owner/contract.md) governs the missing handoff: separate explicit MFA flow bound to configured exact identity/approval, fixed callback and distinct secure cookie; existing closed row schema/generation fence/shared quotas; owner-only atomic bootstrap and exact uncertain-result reconciliation; original flow/MFA expiry enforced at final identity check; no ordinary-login promotion or inspection enablement. No actual owner approval is supplied by this documentation or agent. Implementation and verification states are reported in BUILD-STATUS; full workflow and beta completion remain unproven.

## ALB HTTPS target-hop ingress — AI01–AI06

Reported before coding: the planned HTTPS ALB/private HTTP hop conflicted with unconditional proxy_headers=False and OAuth's fixed HTTPS base origin. The [AI01–AI06 contract](../.loop/repairs/alb-ingress/contract.md) defines the repair. Frozen optional ingress accepts only ALB mode and two canonical nonoverlapping RFC1918 IPv4 CIDRs (/16–/27); absent/null remains untrusted. Root parsing and programmatic configuration reject unsupported shapes before credential discovery. Trust participates in normalized release/raw host binding, and server options are captured before app/client construction.

Only explicit trust enables installed Uvicorn forwarding for those socket peers. Environment wildcard, untrusted peer, wrong Host and absent/plain-HTTP scheme cannot start OAuth. X-Forwarded-Host cannot replace the configured Host; secure cookies/origin/CSRF/PKCE/MFA/admission remain required. Actual SG exclusivity, TLS listener/certificate/host preservation and ALB metadata handling are independent target prerequisites. Local source/installed Uvicorn/ASGI evidence is not actual network/provider/owner approval or inspection readiness. BUILD-STATUS owns applicability. Nothing is provisioned, and PP12 CloudFormation/cost/install work remains open.
